## Preface to the Whole Book.

<image-desc>An ornamental historiated woodcut initial 'D' within a hexagonal frame, decorated with flowers and foliage.</image-desc>

Greed is a root of all evil; says Saint Paul in his first epistle written to Timothy, chapter 6, verse 10. As many examples of greedy and unjust people and their punishments demonstrate, and as are recounted in both ecclesiastical and secular histories; however, the world cares nothing for this. Greed nevertheless reigns through usury and exploitation <note>original: "Schinderey," literally "skinning," referring to the cruel over-taxation or financial bleeding of the poor</note>, cunning and deceit throughout the entire world. He who cannot now lie and cheat is considered no wise man by the world. The Devil incites people to greed to such an extent that clergy and laity, nobleman and peasant, householder <note>original: "Haußmann," referring to a head of a household or a tenant farmer</note> and merchant, young and old, small and great, are all ensnared by it; and through greed, people fall from one sin and injustice into another, and

<summary>This preface introduces the book by citing Saint Paul's warning against greed, lamenting how usury and deceit have corrupted all levels of society from the nobility to the peasantry.</summary>
<keywords>greed, Saint Paul, Timothy, usury, exploitation, morality, social classes, sin</keywords>

Much is said of the Jews—that they are beyond all measure money-hungry, usurious, and exploitative <note>original: "schinderisch," referring to financial "skinning" or ruthless extortion</note>, lying and deceitful—let no one wonder at this, for they are a heathen people <note>The author characterizes them as "heathen" in the historical sense of being outside the established Christian church of his time</note> who now no longer know what they believe, and the deceit that occurs through them is not their fault, but the fault of the authorities who tolerate them; Among us Christians, however, there is a race or kind of people called *smoke-selling chemists* <note>original Latin: "fumivendulos Chymicos"; "selling smoke" was a common Early Modern idiom for selling empty promises or fraudulent schemes</note>, or chemical sophists, alchemical gold-beetles <note>original: "Gold-Käfer," a derogatory term for those obsessively pursuing gold through false means</note>, and deceitful smoke-sellers; these act even worse, and are by no means equal to the Jews, but are to be esteemed as something even higher <note>The author uses "higher" ironically here to mean "more advanced in their villainy"</note>; these same people can handle matters much more artfully and, in the eyes of many, more sincerely than all others. # Preface

Making gold and silver—or even the **Blessed Philosopher’s Stone** <note>original Latin: *Lapis Philosophorum Benedictus*; the legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold and granting long life</note>—is nothing to them. They do not value it highly because it requires too much effort. Instead, they claim to possess **Particulars** <note>original: *Particularia*; in alchemy, these were "short-cut" processes that allegedly transformed specific metals in small amounts, rather than the "Universal" transformation of the Stone</note>, which they say accomplish the same thing with much less labor. They claim to be able to prepare the most precious **Medicines** <note>original: *Medicinalia*</note>, yet when it comes down to it, it is all for nothing; one always hears that it vanishes like a phantom <note>original: "auf eine Lami aufgehet"; likely referring to a *Lamia*, a deceptive spirit or a delusive vision that disappears upon closer inspection</note>.

Every honest man should consider how to guard himself against such people and be all the more cautious. For someone who has truly **wrought** <note>original: *elaboriret*; to have painstakingly completed a complex chemical or "alchymical" process</note> such a great work, and by God’s grace-filled help and blessing has brought it to its conclusion, would not run about like a **peddler** <note>original: *Kramer*; a traveling merchant selling trinkets at a fair</note> at a fair, selling it for money (which he would not value, given the flowing and rich abundance provided by this **Art** <note>The "Art" refers to Alchemy</note>), nor would he beg others for his livelihood for God’s sake. To this end, the reading of this old rhyme can serve very well and be quite useful:

->He who truly possesses the Art,<-
->Does not offer it for money in the mart,<-
->For he would indeed be a foolish man,<-
->Who understands and can perform such a high work,<-
->And yet would wander about the world,<-
->To hawk and sell the Art like common wares.<-

# Preface

...greedy people are never satisfied until the earth covers them, according to the proverb: *They shall be content with a sarcophagus* <note>original Latin: *Sarcophago contenti erunt*; a grim reminder that only the grave satisfies the person who craves worldly possessions</note>. They do not stop until someone finally presses their eyes shut in death.

->The miser does not cease,<-
->Until he is scraped into the grave.<-

Much is said about the Jews—that they are beyond measure greedy for money, usurious, exploitative, lying, and deceitful. Let no one be surprised by this, for they are a heathenish people <note>The author reflects the common prejudices of his time, viewing non-Christians as "heathen" or lacking a moral compass</note> who no longer know what they believe; and the fraud that occurs through them is not their own fault, but the fault of the authorities who tolerate them.

Among us Christians, however, there is a certain race or kind of people called <term>smoke-selling chemists</term> <note>original Latin: *fumivendulos Chymicos*; a term for charlatans who sell "smoke" or empty promises rather than real chemical results</note>, or chemical sophists, alchemical **gold-beetles**, and deceptive **smoke-sellers**. These people behave even worse, and are not to be compared to the Jews at all, but are to be regarded as something much "higher" <note>Here, "higher" is used sarcastically to mean more advanced in their ability to deceive</note> in their craft. They can handle matters much more artfully and appear more sincere in the eyes of many than all others. They have learned their "art" so exceedingly well that one does not realize they have been cheated and led astray until the swindlers have become invisible and no one knows where they have gone. These people promise the public that they will teach them...

<summary>The author warns against the insatiable nature of greed and identifies "smoke-selling" chemists (fraudulent alchemists) as the most dangerous of deceivers, more sophisticated and elusive than common swindlers.</summary>

...to make gold and silver. Also, the <term>Blessed Philosophers' Stone</term> <note>original Latin: *Lapis Philosophorum Benedictus*; the legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold and acting as a universal medicine</note> is nothing to them; they do not hold it in high regard because it requires much labor. Instead, they claim to possess <term>particular processes</term> <note>original Latin: *Particularia*; in alchemy, these were specific, shortened methods or "recipes" that claimed to produce gold or medicine without the long, difficult process of creating the universal Stone</note> that do the very same thing, and with much less effort. They claim they can prepare the most precious <term>medicinal remedies</term> <note>original Latin: *Medicinalia*</note>, but when it comes down to it, it is absolutely nothing; one will always hear that it all vanishes into a deceptive phantom <note>original German: *daß es auf eine Lami aufgehet*; likely referring to a "Lamia," a mythical creature used here as a metaphor for a vanishing illusion or a trick that leaves the victim empty-handed</note>.

Every honest man should consider how to guard himself against such people and be more cautious. Just think: anyone who has actually labored on such a work and, through **God’s grace-filled help and blessing**, has brought it to completion, would not run about like a **peddler at a fair** to sell it for money (which he would not even value, given the flowing and rich abundance this art provides). Nor would he beg others for a livelihood for God’s sake. To this end, the reading of this little old rhyme can be very helpful and useful:

->He who possesses this Art,<-
->Does not offer it for money;<-
->For he would indeed be a foolish man,<-
->Who understands and can perform such a Great Work,<-
->To then wander about the world,<-
->To peddle and sell his Art.<-

# Preface

...the greedy are never satisfied until the earth covers them, according to the proverb: *They shall be content with a sarcophagus* <note>original Latin: *Sarcophago contenti erunt*</note>; they do not stop until someone closes their eyes for them;

->The miser does not leave off,<-
->Until he is buried in his grave.<-

Much is said about the Jews—that they are immeasurably greedy for money, usurious and exploitative, lying and deceitful. Let no one wonder at this, for they are a heathen people <note>The author uses "heathen" here in a derogatory sense common to the 17th century to denote those outside the Christian faith</note> who no longer know what they believe; and the fraud carried out by them is not their fault, but that of the authorities who tolerate them. Among us Christians, however, there is a race or kind of people called **smoke-selling chemists** <note>original Latin: *fumivendulos Chymicos*; "selling smoke" was a common idiom for selling worthless illusions or non-existent secrets</note>, or <term>Chemical Sophists</term> <note>practitioners who use clever but fallacious arguments to deceive people into believing they possess the secret of gold-making</note>, <term>Alchemical Gold-Beetles</term> <note>a derogatory term for those who scurry after gold but produce nothing of value</note>, and deceitful <term>Smoke-Sellers</term>. These act even worse, and are not at all like the Jews, but must be regarded as something else—indeed, as much "higher" in their craft. These can handle their business much more artfully and, in the eyes of many, more sincerely than all others. They have learned their craft so exceedingly well that one does not realize they have been cheated and led astray until the deceivers have become invisible, and no one knows where they have gone. These promise people that they will teach them...

# Preface

...artfully. They claim to be able to **make gold and silver**; likewise, the <term>Blessed Philosophers’ Stone</term> <note>original: *Lapis Philosophorum Benedictus*; the legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold and curing all diseases</note> is nothing to them—they do not value it highly because it requires too much labor. Instead, they claim to possess <term>Particulars</term> <note>original: *Particularia*; in alchemy, these were minor or partial processes that supposedly performed a specific transmutation or "particular" chemical feat without the universal power of the Stone</note> that accomplish the very same thing, and indeed with much less effort. They claim they can prepare the most precious <term>Medicines</term> <note>original: *Medicinalia*</note>, yet when it comes down to it, it is all for nothing. One will always hear that it all <note>The author uses the phrase "auf eine Lami aufgehet," a regional idiom meaning to vanish into thin air or end in a puff of smoke</note> vanishes into thin air.

Every honest man should consider how to guard himself against such people and be all the more cautious. Reflect upon this: he who has <term>perfected</term> <note>original: *elaboriret*; to have successfully completed the long and laborious chemical refinement process</note> such a work and brought it to a conclusion through God’s grace-filled help and blessing would not run about like a merchant at a fair to sell it for money—which he would not value, given the flowing and rich abundance provided by this Art—nor would he beg others, for the sake of God, to provide for his sustenance. To this end, the reading of this old rhyme can serve very well:

> He who does this Art possess,
> Does not for money it profess;
> For he would be a foolish man,
> Who understands this lofty plan,
> To wander through the world and stray,
> To hawk and sell the Art away.

# Preface

> They accept quite poor money for it /
> Because through it he can soon have /
> Enough gold, money, wealth, and property, /
> Above all else, a God-blessed spirit /
> Which pleases God much better /
> Than everything that is in the world.

There are others who think themselves even wiser; they first wander through all the **Philosophical books**, through and through, and write out <term>commonplaces</term> <note>original: *locos communes*; these were thematic notebooks where scholars collected key quotes and ideas for later use</note> from them. Afterward, they already know how to bring these into an order that is acceptable to the world. In their published books, they maintain a fine <term>connection</term> <note>original: *connexion*</note> or an agreement that sounds identical to other <term>Philosophers</term> <note>original: *Philosophis*; in this context, "philosopher" almost always refers to an alchemist or "philosopher of nature"</note>, so that even learned people are often deceived by them. These people would have <note>This is a colorful German idiom ("dem Teufel ein Bein auß dem Hindern geschworen") meaning they would swear a solemn oath on something utterly impossible or false because they are so convinced</note> sworn a bone out of the Devil's backside that such a person had described the Art <term>from head to toe</term> <note>original: *à capite ad calcem*; a Latin phrase meaning "thoroughly" or "from start to finish"</note>. Then people say, "If only one knew where the <term>Adept Philosopher</term> <note>original: *Philosophus adeptus*; a master who has actually succeeded in the Great Work of alchemy</note> lived?" (Indeed, I say: such a fool!) "I would spare no expense or cost; I would go to him, even if it cost me 1,000 <term>Ducats</term> <note>A gold coin used for trade throughout Europe</note>." 

If I were then to advise such "good people"—as I have indeed often done—I would tell them that unless they have a good <term>naturally fundamental root</term> <note>original: *radicem naturaliter fundamentalem*; a foundational understanding of the laws of nature</note>, they should, for this reason, al-

<summary>The author warns against "book-alchemists" who compile excerpts from existing texts to appear as masters, noting that they deceive even the learned. He argues that without a fundamental understanding of nature's "root," these efforts and the money spent seeking these "adepts" are wasted.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the "Preface" (Vor-Bericht) from the previous section. The text picks up mid-sentence, concluding a thought about the futility of human striving without divine favor in the pursuit of alchemy.</meta>

# Preface

abandon themselves to great care and worry; for they will learn nothing of the <term>Philosophical-Natural Universal Work</term> <note>original: *Philosophisch natuͤrlichen universal-Werck*; this refers to the "Great Work" of alchemy, the ultimate goal of creating the Philosopher's Stone which could transform metals and heal the body</note> for the rest of their lives. I set the reason for this here: it does not depend on anyone's running and racing, but on God's grace. When God wants to make us partakers of such a <term>Secret</term> <note>original: *Geheimnuß*; a common term for alchemical knowledge, which was believed to be a gift from God revealed only to the worthy</note>, He can awaken a man from the East and a bird from the West to carry out His command. Even a poorly started work must have a good outcome when God grants something to a person. It follows the proverb of the ancient wise men:

->What is not hoped for in a year happens in a moment.<-
<note>original Latin: *Accidit in puncto, quod non speratur in año.*</note>

God can strike a rock so that water flows out of it before one even realizes it; Manna must also lie beneath the dew by the all-wise command of God. His <term>Almighty Hand</term> <note>original: *Allmachts-Hand*</note> can still give even in the evening, and only then let quails come up to cover the camp <note>These are references to the Biblical Book of Exodus, where God miraculously provided for the Israelites in the desert</note>. Thus, it depends <term>absolutely</term> <note>original: *absolutè*</note> on the blessing, the <term>benediction</term> <note>original: *benedeyen*</note> of God, and His mercy. Therefore I say again:

->It is in vain that you rise early,<-
->And then go to sleep hungry,<-
->And eat your bread with hardship;<-
->For to whom God grants it, He gives it in sleep.<-
<note>This is a paraphrase of Psalm 127:2, emphasizing that success comes from divine favor rather than exhausting labor.</note>

<summary>This page concludes the author's preface by emphasizing that alchemical success is a divine gift (the "Universal Work") rather than a result of human effort. # Preface

> Therefore: See what rich blessing clings to him
> Who lives as a man in the <term>fear of God</term> <note>original: *Gottes forcht*; a traditional religious concept of deep respect and awe toward the divine, considered the beginning of wisdom</note>,
> From him, the ancient curse and wrath depart
> Which are born into the children of men.

But where God is to bless us, we must pray for it; alongside prayer, there must be work, and the ancients have masterfully and well rhymed together ***ora & labora*** <note>Latin: "pray and work"</note>, pray and work; but how shall he work who does not know at what? How shall he follow Nature in her **operations** <note>original: *Operationibus*; refers to the chemical or spiritual processes performed in an alchemical experiment</note> who does not know in what way, how, or through what she performs them? Pray to God for this with your whole heart and devout desire; if He grants it to you, and it is not a hindrance to your soul’s salvation and welfare but rather beneficial, then He will surely give it to you. But if, on the contrary, it would be harmful and damning to you, God (if you persist in zealous prayer) will surely know how to lead you away from it and toward other things beneficial to you. For that, however, you must be of a strong faith, and say with David in his 130th Psalm, verses 5, 6, and 7: I wait for the Lord, my soul hopes for the Lord, my soul waits, and I also hope for the Lord from one morning watch to the next; let Israel hope in the Lord, for with the Lord there is grace and **redemption** <note>original: *Erlösung*</note>;

<summary>This page emphasizes the spiritual discipline required for alchemy, arguing that success comes through the balance of prayer and labor (ora et labora). It highlights that divine guidance is necessary to understand the operations of Nature and warns that God only grants such secrets if they benefit the seeker's soul.</summary>

<keywords>ora et labora, divine blessing, Nature's operations, Psalm 130, prayer and work, spiritual alchemy, fear of God, redemption</keywords>

# Preface

I will not speak further of such things here / but rather direct the kind reader to my <term>Chemist well-pleasing to God</term> <note>original: *Chymicum DEO benè placentem*; likely a reference to another work by the author emphasizing the religious duties of the alchemist</note>, or the so-called Alchemical Rules of Instruction, in order to better see its proportions and nature from it; but that we come again to the <term>Sophistical</term> <note>In an alchemical context, "sophistical" refers to deceptive, fraudulent, or purely theoretical books written by those who have never performed the work successfully</note> books / I can indeed testify in truth / that nothing more ridiculous has appeared to me, nor appears to this day / than when it says in those writings / <term>our Stone</term> <note>original: *noster Lapis*</note>, <term>our Tincture</term> <note>original: *nostra Tinctura*</note>, <term>our Philosophical Gold</term> <note>original: *nostrum Aurum Philosophicum*</note>, <term>our Mercury and that of the Philosophers</term> <note>original: *Mercurius noster &amp; Philosophorum*</note>, our stone / our gold-powder / our philosophical gold / our mercury and that of the wise / etc. and the fools have not seen them in their entire lives.

Yet one must bear with them; meanwhile, however, they should know / that the <term>fable</term> <note>original: *Fabula*</note> of apples and horse-droppings, recorded and described for our instruction by wise people of old / becomes true in them / and one must especially attribute to them / that where they intend to mix their books, as mentioned, (which are clearly enough before our eyes and, alas, common today) among the true philosophical ones / just as one cannot prevent mouse droppings from being among the sugar / and dregs from being among the wine /

# Preface

## Vor-Bericht

And so I say one cannot stop them / from saying along with the <term>horse-figs</term> <note>original: *Roßfeigen*; a common euphemism for horse droppings</note>: <note>original: *Nos poma natamus*</note> "We apples are swimming."
For so the horse-figs spoke / as they swam on the water among the apples; yet they should be quite sure of this / that a wise and sensible man / will know how to pull the <term>rogue’s caps</term> <note>original: *Schalckkappen*; the pointed hats worn by jesters or tricksters</note> well and truly from their eyes / they will not be <note>original: *judiciren*</note> judged by outward appearances / but one will know how to <note>original: *anatomiren*</note> dissect them <note>original: *nervosè*</note> vigorously and say: see here, fellow, you have this from one source / that from another / and see how your work appears? Like a beggar’s cloak, patched and assembled from a thousand scraps; like the costume of a fool or a **Pickelhäring** <note>A stock comedic character or "clown" in 17th-century theater, known for his multicolored patchwork clothing</note>; like an **Apple of Sodom** <note>A legendary fruit that looks beautiful on the outside but is full of ash and soot inside</note> and many such things; fools and children eat <term>goat-beans</term> <note>original: *Geißbonen*; another euphemism for animal droppings</note> as if they were raisins / but not sensible, wise, and clever people.

But so that you may have spoken quite bitterly of all **Sophistical** books / listen to this: that you namely speak of the **Philosophical Work** like a blind man speaks of color / whatever one tells him, namely that something is black / white / green / yellow / red or blue / he cannot argue against it / for he knows and understands no better / so I say to you also, it goes for you and all **Sophists** / what one prescribes for you, that you must believe / know then—

# Preface

## Vor-Bericht

...it is not yet known whether the wise intended it to be understood in this way or not. But if a blind man is addressed with untruths, or if one trifles with him in such a manner—if he insists, for example, that what is black is white, or what is white is red, yellow, green, gray, and so on—even if a sighted person offers a serious <term>counterpart</term> <note>original: *contrapart*; an opposing argument or testimony</note> to him, yet he still believes it is otherwise because he has been so persuaded, that would indeed be an exceedingly foolish matter.

Therefore, I say, when a **sophist**, or even an otherwise sensible, clever man, wants to defend and assert a matter in public print that he does not understand, it would be a foolish act. (I wish to make an absolute distinction <note>original: *absolutè*</note> here: namely between those who write about these things without understanding them, and those who devote themselves to the work through diligent study and <term>laboring</term> <note>original: *laboriren*; working in the laboratory</note> according to the laws of nature.)

Yet one must give such people credit: although they have not made the <term>Philosopher’s Stone</term> <note>original: *Lapidem Philosophorum*; the legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold</note>, nor are they able to make or prepare it, their books and <term>writings</term> <note>original: *Scripta*</note> certainly can. In their own minds, they have already <term>elaborated</term> <note>original: *elaborirt*; a term for the chemical refinement process</note> it long ago. They have also perfectly learned the <term>power of transmutation</term> <note>original: *Virtutem transmutativam*; the ability to change one substance into another</note> and they <term>tinge</term> <note>original: *tingiren*; to transform a metal’s essence or color using a tincture</note> every man...

## Vor-Bericht

...continually their minds with new passions; the best thing is that they keep the operations of the first matter or the philosophical work <note>original: *Operationes primæ materiæ seu operis Philosophici*; refers to the initial stages of the alchemical process to create the Philosopher's Stone</note> within themselves and in their own minds. But dearest **God**, we would gladly favor and serve them, if only they thought to humble themselves, and did not aim so high above us with their peacock’s tail and their fool's head, tinted by the universal sophisticated balsam <note>original: *per Balsamum Sophisticum universalem*; "sophistic" here means fraudulent or counterfeit, implying their "wisdom" is a fake chemical coating</note>. They imagine that they have already trampled the fool's antics underfoot in their childhood shoes; we gladly leave it to them. Perhaps that is where the great over-cleverness and foolish wisdom come from, because the balsam has risen so strongly to their noses, brains, and even through their minds; yet they must know,

# Preface

## Vor-Bericht

...that we also still understand something and indeed have the power to face them with the truth.

To such people, one must rub the truth right into their mouths and deal with them as one does with small, senseless children; one must give them milk and soft food to eat, so that they may digest it all the more easily. Yet one must also deal with them cautiously and not trust them too much, lest they bite one's finger. If they bite, they know (just like children) that they are punishable, and they should know that we do not care much for their reactions, but will only deal with them all the more harshly. They should know that we count them among the babblers and deceivers—especially the insolent and useless frauds and contradictors—of whom **St. Paul** says in the Epistle to Titus, chapter 1, verses 10 and 11, that their mouths must be stopped <note>original: *daß man ihnen müsse das Maul stopfen*; a literal and forceful biblical idiom for silencing false teachers</note>. If they then come attacking us with <term>calumnies <note>original: *calumnien*; false and malicious accusations designed to slander someone's reputation</note></term>, they should know that we do not care much about it; innocence shall surely avenge it, and our truth shall stand firm when their lies suddenly pass away. We will then tell them how their chattering suits them, namely according to the words of **Solomon** <note>original: *Salomonis*</note> in his Proverbs, chapter 26, verses 4, 5, and 6: Like dancing to a cripple...

# Preface

## Foreword

...so it suits an unwise man and a fool to speak of wisdom; a proverb in a fool’s mouth is like a thorn-branch that stings in a drunkard’s hand. Do not answer a fool according to his folly, lest you also become like him; but answer a fool according to his folly, so that he does not think himself wise. If you see one who thinks himself wise, there is more hope for a fool than for him, etc. <note>These verses are from Proverbs 26:7, 9, 4, 5, and 12. The author uses these biblical warnings to justify his harsh tone against his intellectual opponents.</note>

One cannot hold it against us if we set the follies of such despisers of truth before their eyes and place the spectacles right on their noses <note>The German idiom "die Brillen recht auf die Nase setzen" means to force someone to see the truth clearly or to show them the reality of their own errors.</note>, so that we do not sin against them by overlooking their faults. We have good reason for this, for **God**, who dwells in heaven, has (thanks be to Him) incorporated us into the truth for the very purpose that we might have cause to tell the truth to others. We are bound by our good conscience to oppose such abominable, cruel, and innumerable lies—which have been publicly published and brought to print by ancient as well as modern **authors** <note>original: *Authoribus*</note>—with the unadulterated truth in such a way that everyone may clearly and obviously see the distinction between the path of the **philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophen*; in this context, the author refers to "true" alchemists who seek the genuine secrets of nature</note> and the **sophists** <note>original: *Sophisten*; a derogatory term for "pseudo-alchemists" or those who use deceptive arguments for profit</note>, between the truth and the lies. # Preface

...had it been permitted / it would also have been free for us / to title and name this little book and simple work **The Mirror of Unheard-of Truth** <note>original: *Speculum veritatis inauditæ*</note>; but the gossip / that we might be boastful of our art / does not please us / therefore we have left it under the old title / as its creator titled it from the beginning; Yet I say / that this little book will certainly endure forever because of the truth contained within it, according to the words of Solomon, Proverbs chapter 12, verse 19: "The truthful lip shall be established for ever: but a lying tongue is but for a moment" / and according to the old German proverb:

->Truth Conquers!<-
<note>original: *Vincit Veritas!*</note>

> Truth stands for ever and ever /
> Lies pass away very suddenly and in a short time.

But so that one may know the origin of our little book regarding the <term>Hermetic River</term> <note>original: *Hermetiſcher Fluß*; a metaphor for the transmission of secret alchemical knowledge or the "flow" of the universal solvent</note> / let everyone know / that its beginning stems from Poland / the author is certain to have been an <term>Adept</term> <note>original: *Adeptus*; in alchemy, one who has reached the highest level of knowledge and successfully created the Philosopher's Stone</note> and a Knight of the Golden Cross; he lived previously in Koÿs Kavola, a small town 5 miles from Lublin; he is related to me / and was the dear brother of my wife’s grandmother (on her father’s side); his name he [has placed] under a **cabalistic knot** <note>original: *nodo cabaliſtico*; a coded or encrypted name, often using numerology or letter transposition to hide an author's identity from the uninitiated</note> in the treatise **The Golden Age** <note>original: *Aureum Seculum*</note>

If I tell you the truth now, then follow me,

*If I tell you the truth, why do you not believe me?* <note>original: "So ich euch die Warheit sage / warum glaubet ihr mir denn nicht"; citing John 8:46.</note>

also do theirs.
But we have wished to bring this (our little book)
to light, and indeed improved or elucidated,
eased, explained, and expanded it; for it
has never before come to light. And even if
it had previously come out, it would have been
a shame, for it was very obscured, intricate,
and written in a truly philosophical <note>The term "philosophical" in this context refers to the dense, symbolic language of alchemy intended only for the "sons of art."</note> manner; 
thus we say it would have been a shame
had it come to light before now.

and explained or interpreted by us,
it would be a pity if it—like a little light
shining anew for the world—were to
remain under a bushel <note>A reference to Matthew 5:15: "Neither do men light a candle, and put it under a bushel, but on a candlestick; and it giveth light unto all that are in the house."</note> or lie hidden under a bench,

# Preface

Table (for it is well worthy of this honor above others); 
we will spare no effort to do this and even more in honor of our dear late Mr. Cousin; 
we wish to write about all his works which are in print, using plain words yet with good understanding; 
also, for the benefit of all the inexperienced, we wish to publish several more books under the title <term>The Chemist Well-Pleasing to God</term> <note>original Latin: "Chymicus Deo benè placens"</note>, 
since serving the world with books is now especially our great pleasure and joy, as it is also to instruct them further.

yet we say explicitly that a <term>Gordian</term> <note>original: "Nodus Gordius"; an ancient metaphor for an intricate or unsolvable problem.</note> or <term>Cabalistic Knot</term> <note>original: "Cabalisticus"; referring to hidden, mystical, or coded knowledge common in alchemical traditions.</note> still lies hidden in our little book;

If it does not succeed immediately the first time, do

> *your own strength, is the mastery of the Creator drawn forth;*

# Preface

...received; with these, the little rhyme agrees: *Not from my own knowledge, but from the grace of the Holy Spirit* <note>original Latin: "Non ex mea scientia, sed ex Spiritus sacrosancti gratia"</note>, not out of my own science <note>In this context, "science" (Wissenschafft) refers to acquired technical knowledge or intellectual study.</note>, but out of the power and strength of the Holy Spirit; and also this which follows: *Whatever pleases GOD, all nature gratefully accepts!* <note>original Latin: "Quod D E O placet id omne natura gratenter accipit!"</note>

->Whatever God most graciously commands,<-
->Nature performs with a good spirit.<-

All the above is explained and clarified by the following: *Pray therefore and work, considering all things vain, except to love God, and to serve Him alone; for this is to walk in masteries and divine ways, with our Lord Jesus Christ as a companion* <note>original Latin: "Ora propterea & labora, reputans omnia vana, præter amare Deum, & illi soli inservire nam hoc est in magisteriis ac divinis viis ambulare, comite Domino nostro Jesu Christo"</note>, which in German follows thus:

->Pray to God from the bottom of your heart,<-
->And serve Him alone;<-
->Work then at every hour,<-
->Diligent and pure;<-
->Pray and work at all times:<-
->In this lies the Art<- <note>The "Art" is a traditional term for Alchemy, often called the "Sacred Art" or the "Hermetic Art."</note>
->And the soul’s salvation;<-
->Without this, it is in vain.<-
->JESUS grant His grace thereto,<-
->That one may serve Him<-
->In constant, prayerful rest,<-
->And devise nothing else<-
->Outside the power of His will<-
->And holy guidance;<-
->May He bless us day and night,<-
->Through His protection for all time.<-

# Preface

I say therefore once again / that one should not become discouraged / if it does not wish to succeed immediately in the first attempt / but rather the saying goes / hold fast in prayer and work / so God will enlighten and bless you:

> Although it might seem as if He would not [help],
> Do not let this frighten you,
> For when He is most truly with you,
> He chooses not to reveal it;
> Let His Word be more certain to you,
> And even if your heart speaks a loud "no,"
> Yet do not let yourself be terrified. <note>This verse is a common Lutheran hymn stanza, emphasizing faith in God's promises even when His presence is not felt.</note>

I have accomplished a work of Christian love / and placed my sun-clear revelations before everyone’s eyes / well is it for him who has the grace to recognize and understand them; But that I should set down [instructions like] / take ☉ (Gold), ☽ (Silver), ☿ (Mercury), ♂ (Iron), ♀ (Copper), ♃ (Tin), ♄ (Lead), ♁ (Antimony) <note>These are standard planetary symbols used in alchemy to represent the seven metals and antimony.</note> / or the like / that would be inconvenient for me / and a great sin. <note>The author suggests that using traditional cryptic symbols would be a "sin" because it would obscure the "sun-clear" truth he wishes to share as an act of charity.</note>

- - - Something hidden, which <term>nature</term> <note>original: "uatura", likely a printer's error for "natura"</note> beneath the deepest
Mountains has concealed, for which the human race through every [land]
Seeks, enduring and exhausting themselves in labors:
By which alone they might grasp the measure and the sum of value,
And through which, O mortals, the exchange of goods would be
More just, and trade between various peoples
More suitable; if anyone should bring this forth by certain art
Into the light, so it might pass into common usage,
He would have done it, and thus, that which is precious might be thought of as cheap; <note>This Latin verse describes the discovery of the "hidden treasure" (likely the Philosopher's Stone or the secret of metallic transformation). It suggests that if the secret were made common knowledge, the world's economy would be more equitable, even if the "precious" substance lost its rarity.</note>

> Immediately, the other arts would fall away so sluggishly, 
> as much as this untiring one has raised its head: nor perhaps 
> would the Father [God/Jupiter] himself sharpen mortal hearts further

> of dread Jove and his worthy thunderbolt fashion Ossa placed

> or the fire stolen from the ethereal seats,

...word more; and even if the World, the Executioner, the Devil, and Hell, along with all their followers, were to stand there, they should know that for their sake I will not be or become a perjurer or a willful violator of the vow sworn to the **Golden Cross** <term>Golden Cross</term> <note>Likely a reference to the "Order of the Golden and Rosy Cross," a secret society of alchemists and mystics who claimed to possess the secrets of the Philosopher's Stone and the transmutation of metals.</note>. No one was willing to reveal anything to me; but the God of Heaven, whom I intended to serve with my whole heart, He sent me an **ancient paper angel** <note>original: "uhralten Papierenen Engel." I saw the witness which is set in the philosophical heaven for all the philosophical children of Noah, namely the **Bow of God** <note>The rainbow, or "cauda pavonis" (peacock's tail), in alchemy signifies the appearance of many colors, a sign that the transformation is nearly complete.</note>; I also saw...

# Preface.

...the industry of the work, and the labor of the hands: Therefore, blessed is he who has once perfected this **Art** <term>Art</term> <note>original: "artem." Here referring to the Great Work of alchemy.</note>; if he were to live for a thousand thousand years, and every day had to nourish 4,000 poor men, he would never be in want; for even a rich man possessing the **Stone** <term>Stone</term> <note>original: "lapidem." The Philosopher's Stone, the catalyst for transmutation and the "Universal Medicine."</note> from which the **Elixir** <term>Elixir</term> <note>original: "Elixir." A liquid preparation derived from the Stone.</note> is made—much like one who possesses **Fire** <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: "▵." In this context, fire is a metaphor: just as one candle can light many others without losing its own flame, the Stone can transform metals without losing its power.</note>—can give **Fire** [▵] to whom he wishes, when he wishes, and as much as he wishes, without any danger or loss to himself. Because all **Gold** <term>Gold</term> <note>original symbol: "☉," the sign for Sol or gold.</note> is like a tiny grain of sand, and **Silver** <term>Silver</term> <note>original symbol: "☽," the sign for Luna or silver.</note> shall be esteemed as common mud in its presence; because its light is inextinguishable; length of days and health are in its right hand, while in its left hand are glory and infinite riches. For the acquisition of it is better than trading for the purest **Silver** [☽] and **Gold** [☉]; its fruit is more precious than all the wealth of this world, and all things desired in the world cannot be compared to it <note>The author is quoting or paraphrasing the biblical Book of Proverbs (3:14-16) and Wisdom of Solomon (7:8-9), equating the Alchemical Stone with Divine Wisdom.</note>. (See page 172 in the manuscript).

Therefore **Gold** [☉] is tested by **Fire** [▵], by which everything that is not **Gold** is rejected; O most excellent **Gold of the Philosophers** <term>Philosophic Gold</term> <note>original: "Philos. ☉." A distinction is made between "vulgar gold" (money) and the "philosophic gold" which is the perfected substance of the Art.</note>, by which the **Sons of Wisdom** <term>Sons of Wisdom</term> <note>original: "Sapientiæ filii." A traditional title for true alchemists and seekers of divine knowledge.</note> are enriched—and not by that common gold which is so greedily desired! Come hither, you who seek the **Treasure of the Philosophers** <term>Philosophers' Treasure</term> <note>original: "thesaurum Philosophorum."</note> through such varied efforts: first recognize what that stone is which was rejected by you, before you go seeking it.

# Preface.

So let every person know, whoever fears the Lord, that it does not depend on understanding the matter well; instead, it depends on diligent and fervent prayer, true and firm trust in God, and above all, on a true and strong faith. For whoever has faith as a grain of mustard seed (which is indeed the smallest of seeds) shall be able to move mountains—which, however, no one is required to do. Thus, I ask: if the flickering wick of faith that dwells within us—when it is ignited through the **Fire** [△] <term>Fire</term> <note>original symbol: "△," the alchemical sign for fire. In this context, it represents the spiritual "fire" of the Holy Spirit.</note> of the Holy Spirit in fervent prayer and constant hope—receives a flame and becomes once again a clear-shining light and torch of faith, would it not be able to achieve even greater things?

However, because **by praying and working the treasure is dewed from the cloud** <note>original Latin: "orando & laborando thesaurus è nube roratur." This reflects the famous Benedictine motto "Ora et Labora," suggesting that success in the laboratory (the "treasure") is a gift from heaven granted to those who both work and pray.</note>, one must conduct oneself as mentioned above. Yet, holy prayer is always to be preferred over work; one should also trust in prayer more than in work (since the taking and giving of all things remains in God's hands). To this end, God has provided His grace-filled help and assistance, such as with the Prayer of Solomon for Wisdom (Wisdom of Solomon, chapter 10) or with the Prayer of Jesus Sirach <note>Also known as the Book of Ecclesiasticus.</note>, or otherwise with certain Psalms of David on var-

<meta>catchword: var- (likely "various" or "various occasions")</meta>

<summary>This page emphasizes that the success of the alchemical work depends less on intellectual understanding and more on fervent prayer, faith, and the grace of the Holy Spirit. It introduces the maxim of "praying and working" as the necessary path to obtaining divine "treasures."</summary>

# Preface

Not that I intend to boast / as if all wisdom were hidden away with me / but rather, remembering my **Christian duty** / not to bury the small **pound** <term>pound</term> <note>German: "Pfund." I have therefore wished to venture / to **share** <note>German: "communiciren." **Translator's Vocabulary Note:**
**A Cabalistic Enigma:** A riddle based on hidden traditions.
**Wine:** Often represents the transformative spirit or "solvent."
**Food 666:** The crude, heavy matter of the earth.
**Lords of the Clouds:** The volatile gases produced during heating.
**Egyptians:** In this context, it refers to the "uninitiated" or those who remain in spiritual darkness.
**The mystery/face (facinegem):** The hidden essence of the Philosopher's Stone.
**Pallas:** Divine wisdom required to complete the Work.
**Sacrificed/Maccaberis:** The destruction of the old form to allow for the new.
**Pipes:** The specialized equipment used for distillation.
</note>

I found a virgin holding a torch in her hand, and I followed her in the manner of Cupid <note>referring to the blind or devoted following of a guide/desire</note>; and we came into a great Palace, and there was a magnificent and powerful King named Black-foot, or in German **Blackfoot-Lamefoot.** <note>original: "Nigropes, sive Germanicè Schwartzfuß-Lamenfuß." # Missive Letter of Brother R. C. <note>original: "Epist. Missiv. Fr. R. C." This refers to a "Frater Roseae Crucis" or Brother of the Rose Cross, a member of the legendary Rosicrucian Order.</note>
->*page 77.*<-

Our Matter is Spirit, not Body:
Our Matter is not a mineral, but mineral Sulfur and Mercury, an oiliness and a vapor; an immature mineral Electrum <note>In alchemy, "Electrum" often refers to a natural alloy of gold and silver, but here it represents a specific intermediate stage of the "Great Work."</note>: Our Matter is not gold, but the Seed of Gold, or virgin Gold: Our Matter is not a Metal, but the Root of all Metals.

->*In the same place*<-

## For my remembrance, keep this:

The ugly rogue Saturn <note>Saturn is the alchemical name for the metal lead. It is called a "rogue" because it is seen as heavy, dull, and "malefic," yet it contains the secret beginning of the work.</note> is nearest to our King—the immature Electrum, the Star of the Sun <note>original: "Astro Solis"</note>, the Red Lion <term>Red Lion: A common alchemical symbol representing either a powerful acidic solvent or the final, perfected state of the matter (the Philosopher's Stone) capable of "devouring" or transforming other metals.</term>, the Dunech <note>Dunech is an obscure alchemical term, often associated in Paracelsian texts with the "spiritual" essence of a substance or sometimes antimony.</note>, and the Air of Hermes <note>original: "Aëri Hermetis"</note>. Our lead is our First Matter <note>original: "materia prima"</note>, the man in the hermaphroditic body <note>original: "corpore Hermaphroditico" – representing the union of male (sulfur) and female (mercury) principles within a single substance</note>, the earth of our field, etc.

<summary>This page defines the "First Matter" of the alchemical process not as common metal, but as a spiritual "root" or "seed." It specifically identifies the "rogue" metal Saturn (lead) as being closely related to the final, solar state of the work.</summary>

# COMMUNICATION OF A 
# CABALISTIC TALE. <note>original: "COMMUNICATIO FABULÆ CABALISTICÆ." While "Cabalistic" refers to the Jewish mystical tradition, in this 17th-century alchemical context, it generally implies a secret, symbolic, or divinely revealed oral tradition.</note>

At a time when the hours felt long and I shortened them by going for a walk, I returned home again, very weary from the said recreation. Laying myself down on my bed to rest a bit from that weariness, I fell asleep in deep thoughts concerning the **Universal Work** <note>original: "Opere universo." In alchemy, the "Great Work" (Magnum Opus) refers to the process of creating the Philosopher's Stone.</note>: there comes toward me in a vision upon a green heath a purely naked female form, carrying a torch in her hand, draped about her loins only with a silken veil; and after I asked her who she might be, she answered me: she was **naked Nature.** (a)

<note>(a) Naked Nature: Nature is described by others much differently than by the author here; but because she revealed herself to the author openly and with illumination, or as a companion <note>original: "apertè & illuminativè seu comitativè"</note>, he has depicted her here at the Palace of the Adept Sages <note>original: "Sophorum adeptorum Pallatium"</note>, naked, carrying a torch in her hands (to light his way). For whoever knocks at the door of this Heavenly Goddess, to them it shall be opened by God through her.</note>

<summary>The author describes falling into a weary sleep and experiencing a vision of "Naked Nature," who appears as a torch-bearing guide to the Palace of the Adepts.</summary>
<keywords>Cabalistic Tale, Universal Work, Naked Nature, Palace of the Adepts, vision, alchemy, allegory</keywords>

...than; whoever seeks with **God**, he shall find through **Nature**; whoever prays to **God**, he shall be heard by **God**, and through this holy medium <note>original: "heilige Mittel." The text suggests that Nature acts as the physical vehicle for God's spiritual gifts.</note>, that for which he prayed and was heard by God shall be given to him; whoever asks **God** for light, he shall be enlightened through her [Nature], and steadily kept upon the right **Path of Wisdom.**

And all those who had a desire and love for **Wisdom**, she enlightened (b)

<note>(b) According to the testimony of **Solomon** in his Proverbs, Chapter 3, verses 13 to 15 and following: "Blessed is the man who finds wisdom," etc., up to verse 26. But although great promises should act as an enticement and a spur, it usually happens as **Solomon** says earlier in the opening of that book, Chapter 1, verses 20 to 33: that **Wisdom** cries out in the streets, etc. This is exactly what happened to this author, for he stated just before that **Wisdom** met him, and after his questioning, she gave him a report and commanded him to follow her.</note>

She welcomed them heartily and helped them mightily, asking me meanwhile: What then might my desire be, that I followed her so eagerly on foot? I answered that I desired nothing else but that—since she was **unveiled Nature** <note>original: "entblößte Natur." In alchemical literature, "Naked Nature" refers to the underlying reality of the physical world, stripped of false theories or appearances.</note> and a mistress of all sciences—she might do me the favor of **revealing** <note>original: "reveliren"</note> to me how the **great King** (c)

### Of the Triangle of the Center

(c) By the **King of the Wise** is understood here (the one so named by the <term>adept philosophers</term> <note>original: "Phil. adeptis." Those who have successfully achieved the "Great Work" of alchemy.</note>) the <term>legitimate son and heir of the Philosophical-Metallic Kingdom, begotten of Hermes Trismegistus and Aura</term>, or the supreme ruler of all metals.

But **Hermes**, of whom we now speak, was the **King of Egypt**, called **Trismegistus** or **Thrice-Greatest**, because he possessed a perfect and exquisite knowledge of all things contained in this macrocosm, which are distributed into three **Kingdoms**: the **Mineral**, **Vegetable**, and **Animal**. He not only prepared the <term>quintessence</term> <note>The "fifth essence," the purest and most concentrated form of a substance, thought to be the life-force of the universe.</note> of all powers—both of the superior heavens and the inferior earth—as a vessel for this entire <term>Theater</term> <note>likely referring to the "Theatrum Mundi" or the world as a stage for God's creation</note> of nature, but he also transmitted it to posterity in an enigmatic and obscure style. It is said that he found the description of such a **Treasure** in the **Emerald Tablet** <note>original: "tabula Smaragdina." A legendary foundational text of Hermeticism and alchemy.</note> in the valley of **Hebron** after the Great Flood, and that through God's instinct and inspiration, he grasped the true meaning of that **Tablet**.

**Aura**, however, is a <term>Hermaphroditic creature</term> among the minerals; that is, it contains both the male and female genders under one bond. From this Aura, **Hermes** begat a son; but because this son was lame in his feet (meaning he was not yet perfect in his nature and properties, which were meant to be his primary strength), he required special supervision and an upbringing that springs from nature through **Art** <note>In alchemical texts, "Art" refers to the skilled work of the alchemist in assisting or perfecting natural processes.</note>. This upbringing then proceeded in the following manner: **Hermes** placed his son **Phoebus** <note>A poetic name for the Sun, here representing the solar/gold principle.</note> into a <term>sweat-bath</term> <note>original: "Schwißbad." A method of gentle heating, such as a water or steam bath, used to transform or purify substances.</note>. There, through the gently penetrating heat, he began to sweat violently and powerfully, and then drank all the water back into himself; and after...

It describes Hermes as the master of the three kingdoms of nature and introduces the alchemical process of perfecting an imperfect "son" through a heated "sweat-bath."</summary>

was prepared for him/ and he commanded me/ that I should close the mentioned **Crystalline Room according to the manner of nature**, with the **lock** (q)

<margin>(q) **Lock signifies the Seal** <note>original: "Sigillum"</note>; but what kind of **lock** this may be/ is clearly shown below/ that it is not perhaps a **lute of Wisdom** <note>A specialized clay or paste used to hermetically seal glass vessels; original: "lutum Sapientiæ"</note> or anything else of the sort/ but rather the **Seal of Jupiter** to which the mentioned **Jupiter** has the **key**. **See the Title below: On the Seal.**</margin>

that had been given to me/ note however/ that to this **lock Jupiter** had the **key**/ and it was thus impossible for me to dissolve it again without the **consent** of the great **Jupiter**; After he had now been locked in the said manner/ and had laid himself down upon his **bed**/ I began, through the promptings of **Nature** (to whom I was especially recommended)/ a truly **Artificial** <note>In alchemy, 'Artificial' refers to the 'Art'—the skilled intervention of the alchemist to assist and accelerate natural processes.</note>

(r) Artificial fire <term>$\triangle$ <note>The triangle is the standard alchemical symbol for fire.</note></term> is precisely what was already discussed above; yet it is seen here that everything Art does, it does by mimicking Nature, for everything flows, grows, and matures out of Nature through the application of Art.

<margin>(t) Through this sweating, the **radical dissolution of the matter** <note>original: "solutio radicalis materiæ"</note> is prefigured here, without which nothing can be exalted <note>Refined to a higher spiritual or chemical state.</note>, though it can still well exist **in its own being** <note>original: "in suo esse annoch"</note>.</margin>

# Explanation of the Center in
Di

...and become Lord of all Lords. Understand through this the transformation of his natures: the shedding of corporeality and the adoption of spirituality. Many **philosophers** have expressed this with the following short proverb: **make the fixed volatile, and the volatile fixed, and you have the entire mastery** <note>original Latin: "fac fixum volatile, & volatile fixum, & habes totum magisterium." This refers to the alchemical process of dissolving a solid into a gas or liquid (volatile) and then solidifying it again (fixed) to purify it.</note>, to which I then give my assent based on my own experience. Others follow with this:

> **If you dissolve the fixed, and make the dissolved fly,** <note>original Latin: "Si fixum solvas, faciasque volare solutum"</note>
> **And capture the winged bird, it will make you live in safety.** <note>original Latin: "Et volucrem capias, faciet te vivere tutum."</note>

> Dissolve the fixed Miraculous Stone,
> Into a good black water; <note>The "black water" refers to the *nigredo* or putrefaction stage, where the matter dies and decays before being reborn.</note>
> Imprint Mercury's wings upon him,
> Equip him with his hat, <note>Mercury’s winged hat and sandals symbolize volatility—the ability of the substance to rise as vapor in the flask.</note>
> Send him toward heaven as a messenger,
> Before the throne of Jupiter's lightning;
> Then Phoebus <note>Phoebus is another name for Apollo or the Sun, representing the Gold or the perfected Spirit.</note> will, upon the journey,
> Go out strongly to meet him.
> "O dear God, tell me from where?
> I wish to be your companion,
> And fulfill your desire;
> You shall be my son.
> Lay aside your wings and the hat,
> And give them over to Vulcan; <note>Vulcan is the god of fire and the forge. Giving the wings to Vulcan means using fire to "fix" the vapors back into a solid state.</note>
> Vulcan, bring the good armor,
> Prepared for Achilles. <note>In alchemy, Achilles represents the "invulnerable" or perfected matter that can withstand any fire.</note>
> And arm this son of mine,
> With all of my power;
> Set him at the right hand upon my throne,
> Give him greater dominion,
> That he may rule with glory,"

### The Triangle of the Center <note>original Latin: "Trigono Centri." In alchemy, the "center" often refers to the hidden, inner essence of a substance or the central point of the Great Work.</note>

->The world and all the time of times<-
->And let the Sages’ King be good,<-
->Born from the blood of Venus.<- <note>In alchemy, Venus represents the metal Copper, but "Venus's blood" often refers to a specific red tincture or the spiritual energy associated with the planet/metal.</note>

Now I can assure all readers and practitioners: if they do not direct the work they have begun so that it first reaches the shedding of **corporeal crudity** <note>The raw, impure state of the physical matter.</note> and the adoption of **spiritual subtlety**—the sign of which is a **velvet-black deathly color**—for its approaching fixation and exaltation to an eternal, glorious state, then the beginning and the end will be the same (resulting in nothing). For within the deepest **blackness** <note>Known as the *nigredo* stage, where the material "dies" or decomposes in the flask.</note> lies hidden the highest **whiteness**; the blackness is a sign of **dissolution**, dissolution is a sign of **putrefaction**, putrefaction is a sign of **coagulation** and **fixation**. In short, **putrefaction** is the end and the beginning of the entire Work, because within it, as in death, lies hidden the highest life.

I was lying in such water, and it drove a cold sweat out of me; for this reason, I asked **the Expert Rupert** <note>Likely a pseudonym for a teacher or a personified alchemical principle.</note> what was to be done now. He told me that I should maintain the <term>⟂</term> <note>The symbol ⟂ often refers to the bottom of a vessel or a specific orientation of the furnace/apparatus.</note> exactly as it was now, and not stop until a change could be felt in the room and the water gradually disappeared. However, when I asked what kind of water this was, the aforementioned **Expert Rupert** answered me that it was the water in which the King takes pleasure (vv)

(vv) For if this water were not present, I can certainly assure you that everything would be lost and perish. <note>original Latin: *Ignis nam & aqua nostra sufficiunt ad totum opus.*</note> For our fire and water are sufficient for the whole work; our **water** <term>$\nabla$ [the alchemical symbol for water]</term> is stronger than **fire** <term>$\Delta$ [the alchemical symbol for fire]</term>, because it makes a pure Spirit out of the physical body—something fire cannot do. For he who knows how to destroy our **Sun** <note>Gold</note> with our water so that it is no longer a body but spirit, that person has reached the greatest secret <note>original: *arcanum*</note>. They have called the subject of this art a "putrefied dunghill" <note>original Latin: *putrefactum sterquilinium*. This is a common alchemical trope suggesting that the material for the Philosopher's Stone is something common and overlooked, though many took it too literally.</note>; therefore, many have worked with actual dung and found nothing, but this water of ours is the Water of the Sages. ->Let the Nymph be pure, and the water most pure.<-
->and furthermore<-

> Seize this **Arcadian boy** <note>A poetic reference to Mercury (Hermes), who was born in Arcadia. In alchemy, "capturing" him refers to stabilizing the volatile liquid mercury.</note>, unfaithful and far too fleeting,

> Turn **Bronze** <note>original: *Æs*; often used to mean copper or a base metallic body.</note> into pure water, and care for nothing more to be done.

For through the aforementioned water, these things are brought about: **Solution**, **mortification**, **putrefaction**, **regeneration**, **conjunction**, **coagulation**, and **fixation**. In short, the **water** <term>$\nabla$</term> is the principal part of the entire Work, as can be seen in the Allegory itself, and shall be treated more extensively below under the title "On Solution." This water is commonly called by the name **viscous water**, because with the help of this water, the Sages’ **Wondrous Stone** <note>The Philosopher's Stone</note> turns its inner part outward and dissolves itself so completely that it becomes workable and fit for every operation, and finally reaches **fixity** itself.

<page-num>27</page-num>

# Regarding the Triangle of the Center <note>original Latin: *Trigono Centri*</note>

The winter has passed and the summer has arrived; therefore, let my friend come into his garden and eat of his noble fruits. My friend is white and red, chosen from among many thousands; my friend is mine, and I am his, he who grazes among the roses. <note>This entire passage heavily borrows from the biblical Song of Solomon, a common source for alchemical allegory representing the union of the "Red King" (Gold) and "White Queen" (Silver).</note> I, who saw and heard these things, trembled and shook from sheer anxiety and terror; I fell down upon my face out of pure fear, distress, and need. But the old Rupertus <note>Possibly a reference to Robertus Vallensis, an early alchemical historian, or a personified guide</note>, who was with me, raised me up again from the earth and said: "Do not fear, for behold, I announce to you a great joy which shall happen to you; salvation has come to you, and that which all the world desires has been born." (pp)

# CHAPTER I.

## Explanation of the Cabalistic
## Enigma.

It is known to the whole world and well-known to everyone what that philosopher says:

> Nothing exists outside of Nature. <note>original: *Extra Naturam nihil.*</note>

In the German tongue <note>original: *Teutsch*. The author is signaling a transition from the formal Latin maxim to a vernacular poetic explanation.</note>, this means:

> Everything that lives, moves, and weaves upon the earth,
> Must be born from Nature,
> Humans, cattle, birds, and fish in the sea:
> Everything comes from God and Nature,
> Without them, nothing can exist,
> Everything, yes everything, must suddenly pass away.

<term>Cabalistic Enigma</term> <note>original: *Cabalistici Ænigmatis*. This refers to a "Kabbalistic riddle," a term used by early modern scholars to describe hidden or mystical truths about the universe that require special knowledge to decode.</note>

<summary>This page marks the beginning of the first chapter, which introduces the fundamental principle that all life and matter are born from and sustained by Nature and God. It uses a Latin maxim and a German poem to emphasize that nothing can exist outside of the natural order.</summary>

<keywords>Nature, Cabala, philosophy, creation, God, natural law, Kabbalah</keywords>

--- Page 99 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "Cabalistic Enigma" from the previous page, specifically focusing on the "Triangle of the Center" and the dual relationship between God and Nature.</meta>

# The Triangle of the Center <note>original: *Trigono Centri*. In the context of 17th-century alchemy and mysticism, this refers to a geometric representation of the fundamental forces—often God, Nature, and the individual—meeting at a central point of creation.</note>

This intends to teach us that everything dwelling here on Earth consists of two things—namely, of God and Nature. God gives the command "Let it be" <note>original: *Fiat*</note>, and Nature creates <note>original: *Creat*</note>. God commands, and Nature performs. Since it is certain that without Nature not the slightest thing can exist, it remains certain that everyone must devote themselves to her, holding her as their guide and leader, following her if they wish to reach and attain their desired end, purpose, and goal. Tell me if there is anything in the world (a)

<margin>(a) yet not in such a way as if man—created by God and born of Nature—should follow Nature throughout his entire life like cattle, and thus regulate himself according to whatever she suggests to him (or as the learned call it, the stars <note>original: *Astris*</note>), performing everything she dictates. Rather, it should be said of him according to the Proverb:</margin>

->The wise man rules the stars,<- <note>original: *Sapiens dominatur Astris*. This was a popular Latin maxim of the period, suggesting that while the stars might influence a person's physical nature, a wise person uses their intellect and soul to overcome astrological fate.</note>

->Destiny and Fortune<-
->Lead the unlearned fools,<-
->But he who is wise breaks the tether;<-
->And all the stars' power and nature<-

# Explanation of the Center in...

But let me state this in beloved brevity: whoever wishes to perform anything through Art <note>original: *Kunst*. In this context, it refers to human skill, craftsmanship, or the alchemical "Great Art."</note> so that it is called both skillful and yet natural, must imitate Nature or the so-called Stars <note>original: *Astra ad imum imitiren* — literally "imitate the stars to the core."</note> to the very core. However, that one should want to bind one thing to the other in such a way as if neither could exist without the other—this is not feasible. Rather, if something can be accomplished to a certain degree without such things, one does not need to look for special heavenly influences and constellations <note>original: *Constellationes*</note>.

For if a brewer always had to wait until the Sun <note>represented by the symbol ☉</note> had a constellation and influence with Mercury <note>represented by the symbol ☿</note> and Venus <note>represented by the symbol ♀</note>, and only then would brew beer so that it would not turn sour but remain fresh and pleasant, then the majority of people would unfortunately—through no fault of **God**—be forced to drink nothing but water due to a great shortage of beer.

Likewise, if a shoemaker wanted to wait until the Sun, Mercury, and Jupiter <note>represented by the symbol ♃</note> had a concentration <note>This likely refers to a conjunction or a specific astrological alignment.</note> and only then began to make good shoes comfortable for messengers to run in, how else could it turn out but that most messengers would have to run barefoot?

I do not say that the former would not result in durable beer, or the latter in skillfully comfortable shoes—and no one who has tried it alongside other artistic feats can deny it—yet it would be a very silly and foolish thing if everyone were to bind themselves to it.

Nevertheless, certain things have (according to the teaching of **Solomon** the Wise) their appointed time; and it is sometimes also good when one knows how to begin their business with the macrocosm and microcosm in harmony <note>original: *macro & microcosmo consonante*. This refers to the Hermetic principle that the "greater world" of the universe and the "lesser world" of human affairs should be synchronized for the best results.</note>;

that does not have Nature as a leader, I

**Allegory** <note>original: *Allegoriam*; a symbolic story or "cover-name" used to hide the true nature of the substance.</note> or cover-names / openly written and brought to light / so we will not exceed their **limits** <note>original: *Limites*</note> either / yet what is spoken / remains spoken / and what is written remains written / we have set it down clearly enough / and for further clarification we write in the following refuted baseless and Sophistical opinions / still more for the benefit of the **Sons of Wisdom** <note>original: *Sophiæ Filiis*; a traditional term for those initiated into the secrets of alchemy.</note> / whoever does not believe / that we mean them well / let him hold these writings of ours to a test against the truth / and come then / and say what deficiency he has found therein / it is enough / that we prove the possibility and impossibility / so that everyone may know how to finally guard and protect themselves from false **opinions** <note>original: *Opinionibus*</note> of vain labor and **toils** <note>original: *laboribus*</note> (through which the **Son of Doctrine** <note>original: *Filius Doctrinæ*; the student or practitioner of the alchemical teachings.</note> loses and must leave behind not only precious time / but body and life finally to the great detriment of the Art through his own art of willful ignorance) / and finally to take care / also to distinguish the **true from the false** <note>original: *verum à falso*</note> / and thus through this distinction may see a bright-burning Light of Truth before his eyes and spirit.

# Of the Triangle of the Center

<note>original Latin: *Trigono Centri.*</note>

That anyone should desire what is unknown to him certainly seems foolish; it is surely a waste for men to seek something whose true nature they do not understand, for in such a search no hope of success remains. Therefore, I advise all who seek, that they first learn the true existence of that which they are looking for before they begin their search. In this way, they will not happen to be frustrated in their labors. A wise man seeks what he loves, yet he cannot love what he does not know, otherwise he would be a fool. From true knowledge, love is born, as is the truth of all things—which alone flourishes among all true Philosophers.

<note>The author uses this Latin opening to establish a philosophical foundation: alchemy is a pursuit of the mind and spirit as much as the laboratory.</note>

Therefore we say once more: Great laboratories are great labyrinths <note>original: *Laboratoria, Labirynthoria*; a play on words suggesting that massive laboratory setups often lead to confusion rather than clarity.</note>. Let everyone know that the **universal work** <term>Universal Work: the creation of the "Philosopher's Stone" or "Universal Medicine" that was the ultimate goal of alchemy</term> may be prepared better and more suitably behind the furnace rather than inside it. The method of how this is possible is treated in more detail under the title of the **Athanor** <term>Athanor: a self-feeding furnace designed to maintain a constant, steady heat for weeks or months</term>, or the sixth chapter concerning the **philosophical furnace** <note>original: *philosophischen Ofen*</note> and its preparation. 

It is therefore not necessary (as I have several times observed silently with wonder and a laughing heart) for one to enclose a perimeter of 100 or even 150 paces to build a stone laboratory, setting 50 to 60 furnaces within it, thinking that by such means they will finally "catch the hare by the foot and the fox by the tail" <note>An idiomatic way of saying they hope to achieve their goal through sheer brute force and complexity.</note>. Indeed, I do not despise the preparation of **medicinal laboratories** <note>original: *Laboratoria Medicinalia*</note> for the testing and separating of ores, nor the performance of other operations belonging to those two tasks. 

However, it is a pity that so many blind guides and those blind who follow them <note>original: *blinde Leiter und leitende Blinde*</note> surround themselves with:
*   **reverberating furnaces** <term>Reverberating furnace: a furnace that reflects heat from the roof onto the material being heated</term>
*   **wind furnaces** <term>Wind furnace: a furnace that uses a bellows or natural draft to reach high temperatures</term>
*   **digesting furnaces** <term>Digestion: the process of gently heating a substance in a sealed vessel for a long time</term>
*   **calcining furnaces** <term>Calcination: heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a powder or ash</term>
*   **circulating fires** <term>Circulation: the process of evaporating and condensing a liquid repeatedly in a closed vessel</term>
*   **dissolving fire** <note>original: *dissolvir- ∆*; the symbol ∆ represents fire</note>
*   **distilling furnaces**
*   **fixing furnaces** <term>Fixation: the process of making a volatile substance stable under heat</term>
*   **fumigating furnaces**

# In the Triangle of the Center,

...to storm and climb <note>The text begins mid-word, likely completing a verb like *erstohlen* (to steal) or a reference to the "heights" mentioned on the previous page.</note>—I tell them, by God’s will, this shall not happen for all eternity. They will all have to seek their "farewell to Heaven above Earth" <note>original: *valete Cœli super terram*; a dismissive Latin phrase suggesting these people are forever barred from higher spiritual or chemical truths.</note> in the attempt, and thus remain Sophists as they are. For they do not know what the <term>Solar</term> <note>Symbolized by the ☉ symbol in the text.</note> twofold-doubled <term>Hermetic Red Lion</term> <note>A symbol for the fixed sulfur or the "short-cut" to the Philosopher's Stone; the "red" state of matter.</note>—which is gifted with double courage for vengeance against such fools, and whose sign is the white cross—truly is. 

"But wait," they think, "you shall not have spoken that word to us in vain; we are not as blind as people think! Our own seeing eyes shall see it for us, and not yours! Our reason shall surely grasp it, and we will then ensnare it with the **Net of Vulcan** <note>In mythology, Vulcan used a fine metal net to trap Mars and Venus; in alchemy, this refers to using fire and specialized lab equipment to "capture" volatile spirits.</note>. There, we have caught it! Now look, it is in our power! Did we not say it was Gold <note>original symbol: ☉</note>? Can you deny it?" 

There you have caught the donkey by the ear! Hold him tight, for one donkey has surely caught and trapped another. Now the fox must lose his pelt; strike him now with the **Club of Hercules** <note>A metaphor for the forceful destruction or "killing" of a base metal to release its essence.</note>; strike hard so that he dies and you may catch the **Lion’s Blood** <note>A poetic term for the deep red liquid or "tincture" produced during the alchemical process.</note> in your melting pots and **alembics** <note>original: *Alembicen*; a classic pear-shaped distilling apparatus.</note>. Now go quickly and place it in the Chapel of the Dead; have a few dozen fire-zealous Requiem Masses read over it, so that he does not remain in Hell but might **return to life** <note>original: *revificire*; to revive or resituate a spirit back into a body.</note> once more. 

Holla, holla! There he comes already! **Little Jack** <note>original: *Hänslgen*; a mocking, diminutive name for the alchemical matter being "resurrected."</note> is alive again! Do you not see how he lies there and stretches his legs in the air? He stretches and reaches; his soul is already passing in and out of him. But he must first sleep his fill before he rises, so that he may stay awake all the longer afterward. Why does he sleep so long? Oh, just give his little foot a tug; the lazy lout has surely [slept] long enough...

# Regarding the Triangle of the Center #

...to say further: Now we must take the fertile rainwater, the crystal-clear May-dew, and the drink of the gods, Nectar, and give them to drink of it so that they may impregnate one another and thus the <term>Philosophical King</term> <note>A common alchemical term for the perfected Philosopher's Stone or the final, "ennobled" state of matter.</note> may be born; in his presence shall be joy in abundance, splendor, pride, gluttony and carousing, whoring and debauchery for eternity <note>The author is being deeply sarcastic here, suggesting that the "sophists" seek the Stone only to fund a life of sin and excess, rather than for spiritual or scientific enlightenment.</note>.

Haha, that will be our mockingbird, <term>Mercury</term> <note>symbol: ☿</note>, who has shown us so many tricks; if we had <term>precipitated</term> <note>The process of causing a substance to settle out of a solution; the author is mocking the technical jargon used to justify failures.</note> him, he would certainly be the White Eagle with the yellow glows. If we could <term>fix</term> <note>To "fix" a substance in alchemy means to make a volatile or "fleeing" element (like Mercury) stable under the heat of fire.</note> him, then we would be rich people; but it is not quite possible.

Yet something occurs to me: could it be Mercury mixed with Sulfur? If we tried this, it would not be a bad plan; for when we consider it a little, we learn that Mercury and <term>Antimony</term> <note>symbol: ♁</note> along with <term>Salt</term> <note>symbol: ⊖</note> are the two highest and most noble <term>Principles of natural things</term> <note>original: *Principia naturalium rerum*; referring to the Paracelsian Tria Prima (Mercury, Sulfur, and Salt), though the author here substitutes Antimony for Sulfur in his list of "noble" principles.</note>; and not only that, but much more besides.
