### Triangle of the Center ###

...continue on; Now we must take the fruitful rain-water, the crystal-clear May-dew, and the nectar, the drink of the gods, and give them some of it to drink so that they might impregnate one another and thus the **philosophical king** <note>The final, perfected state of the alchemical matter, often the Philosopher's Stone itself</note> be born; with him there will be joy in abundance, splendor, pride, gluttony and carousing, whoring and debauchery eternally; but why are they dying? The **water** <note>symbol: $\nabla$</note> has broken their necks; yes, now our business is nothing again: I thought as much, that nothing would or could come of it.

But tell me, what is the high-soaring **White Eagle** <note>An alchemical term for the volatile substance (usually sal ammoniac or a purified mercury) that "flies" up during sublimation</note>, and what are his yellowish-white feathers <note>original: "Gluten," meaning plumage or the "wings" of the substance in the flask</note>? Ha ha, that will be our mockingbird **Mercury** <note>symbol: ☿</note>, who has played so many pranks on us; if we **precipitated** <note>A process where a solid is made to settle out of a liquid solution</note> it, it would surely be the White Eagle with the yellow feathers; but we have already tried it very often, and it has never wanted to do us any good; because of the many rogue's tricks it has shown us, we trust it no more; if we could **fix** <note>original: "figiren." Fixation is the process of making a volatile substance stable under heat</note> it, then we would be wealthy people; but it is not quite possible.

Yet something occurs to me: could it be **Mercury** <note>symbol: ☿</note> mixed with **Sulfur** <note>represented here by the symbol for Fire: △</note>? If we tried this, it would not otherwise be a bad plan; For if we consider it a little, we learn that **Mercury** and **Fire** alongside **Salt** <note>symbol: ⊖</note> are the two <note>The author likely refers to Mercury and Sulfur/Fire as the active pair, or the Tria Prima (Mercury, Sulfur, Salt) as the primary group</note> highest and most noble **principles of natural things** <note>original: "Principia naturalium rerum"</note>; and not only that, but much more besides; do you not know what all the Sages have said and written:

> In Mercury exists whatever the Sages seek; <note>original Latin: "Est in Mercurio, quidquid quaerunt Sapientes"</note>
> ->Whatever all the Sages ever desire<-

Now, because Mercury <note>symbol: ☿</note> cannot be **fixed** <note>To "fix" a substance is to make a volatile material stable and solid under high heat</note> in and of itself, **Sulfur** and **Salt** <note>symbol: ⊖</note> belong to it; let us take note of this. Perhaps if we look at it in the light, Mercury and Sulfur <note>symbol: △</note> already have a salty nature within them; therefore, it is described to us only with yellowish-white rays and glows. The **Eagle** is Mercury, and the yellowish-white rays or glows will certainly be the Sulfur alone.

Now let us try it; now you fools will cook the "hare in pepper" <note>A German idiom for being in a difficult situation, here used mockingly to describe the chemical digestion process in the flask</note>; I already pity the poor devil. But how will the good **Eagle** fare when his wings are burnt and he is chased into the chimney? Yes indeed, I pity him. They have sworn together that they want to burn him into a red **Antimony** <note>symbol: ♁; here referring to a "fixed" or solid state, often associated with the color red in the final stages of the work</note>; they intend to keep their word honestly. There the **White Eagle** <note>A common alchemical term for the purified, volatile spirit that rises during sublimation</note> will have to lose his hair; they will trim his beard thoroughly so that he might look like a newborn four-week-old child, and they could finally set the **philosophical crown** upon him. 

He is plucked and flayed, boiled and roasted—this is a tragedy for all mankind to hear! They still do not want to devour him; he must be entirely red, like blood, so that he might strengthen them all the more and truly become the **King of the Sophists**; O you gallows-thieving scoundrels, stop cooking the good hare! 

Yet we must give him a somewhat stronger fire, for the Philosophers say: *in the redness, increase the fire so that it may flow* <note>original Latin: "in rubedine auge △ ut fluat"</note>. We will follow this advice, for the more often it flows and **coagulates** <note>Changes from a fluid to a solid state</note> again, the higher it **tinctures** <note>The power of the alchemical medicine to transform or "dye" base metals into gold</note>. 

But now our **Philosophical Egg** <note>The sealed glass vessel used to contain the alchemical matter during heating</note> has shattered, and the young chicks <note>The volatile vapors or spirits escaping the broken vessel</note> have flown away into the air! There our business is nothing once again, even though we had already brought it so far...

->The mine yields a red clay,<-

Now there you have it, that it is a mineral; but that it must be the mineral of Mercury, and no other than this, you have [heard] above—

a little philosophical proverb, which reads thus:

> **In Mercury is** <note>original: *Est in Mercurio*</note>
> **Whatever the Wise seek;** <note>original: *Quidquid quærunt Sapientes*</note>

**Mercury** <term>☿; the volatile, fluid principle in alchemy</term> shares with the Wise what they demand of him; admit it, we have guessed it! Now we want to labor according to your following process. What is it worth? We want to hit the mark; if we do not hit it, then you are to blame, for we have followed your words in everything. We shall surely attain it in the end. The dog would be beaten <note>A German idiom meaning the hardest part of the task is over</note> if only the hide were quickly prepared, so that one could have gloves or pants made from it. That was a defiant courage—not another word or I shall run away! Yet, my lords, you just try it; if it succeeds, it succeeds for you; if it ruins you, I do not know you, and I never commanded you to grope about so madly and foolishly with blindfolded eyes. 

Whoever buys this little book of mine, let him read and reread it, not once but several times; let him properly resolve the secretly creeping philosophical code-names; let him take the **Operations of Nature** <note>original: *Operationes Naturæ*; the observable laws of the physical world</note> in hand and ask them for advice on what is possible or not. I have told you above clearly enough, and it is proven, that it is an **immature mineral electrum of Gold** <term>Electrum ☉ minerale immaturum; ☉ is the symbol for Gold. Electrum refers to a natural alloy</term>. Believe Nature, and believe me in nothing. But why did the **Star** <note>The image shows two overlapping squares forming an eight-pointed star, often representing the *Regulus* of antimony, a star-like crystalline structure seen as a sign of success</note> fail the **sophists** <term>Pseudo-alchemists who seek riches through false recipes</term> once again? Eh, is that nothing? It is because they are fools, and in the bright sunshine, they want to grope about in darkness.

Now, countless people are still found who, with **crude or running Mercury** <term>☿ crudo oder currenti; liquid quicksilver as found in nature</term>, **Gold** <note>☉</note>, and **Silver** <note>☽</note>, a mix-

Then, one must tear the heart out of his inner body and draw the blood from it, and then set that same blood with the glow of the White Eagle <note>Often refers to purified mercury or sal ammoniac</note> upon the Philosophical Triumphal Chariot. One must hitch the white Doves of Venus <term>Venus; associated here with copper or the "fixed" feminine principle</term> before it and choose Mercury <term>☿; the messenger god and the principle of volatility</term> as the driver, who, instead of a whip, should use his double-winged serpent-staff <note>The Caduceus, a symbol of commerce and alchemy</note> to drive it onward, so that it might proceed more swiftly.

I once presented the riddle above to a distinguished gentleman <note>original: *Cavalier*</note> who loved alchemy <note>original: *Chymiam*</note> with all his heart (for the sake of gold-making). He studied and researched it diligently and believed himself to be standing on no bad foundation. When I asked him what might be well understood by the metaphors above, he answered me: that according to the opinion of all the Philosophers, gold—or the highest and most perfect metal—is understood through the Red Lion's blood, and specifically the Sulfur of the Sun <term>🜍 Solis; the "soul" or coloring principle of gold</term>, which is red. This is to be understood through the Eagle's glow, which is the Sulfur of the Moon <term>🜍 ☽; the coloring principle of silver</term>, which is white. 

As for the Philosophical Triumphal Chariot, it could hardly be anything else but the Philosophical Magnet, the powerfully attracting Magnesia, or the Philosophical Steel. The white Doves of Venus are nothing other than the fixed Vitriol of Venus <note>Copper sulfate</note>, and the Mercury of Venus <term>☿ ♀</term> extracted from it. For this reason, Mercury was enigmatically designated and named the driver or the one who completes the Work.

For even though the **Philosophers** call it by its right name, it is nonetheless always clothed in **Aesop’s Cloak** <note>original: "Æsopi Mantel"; a metaphor for the use of fables, allegories, and riddles to hide a plain truth</note>, so that it might not become known to all the world—no, not even to one person in a hundred thousand. 

Therefore, the second cause [of failure] may well be the multitude and abundance of the Philosophers' "cover-names." Because they change the names of one single thing so often, and describe it with so many colors—hardly any of which agree with the faithful truth—those **Sons of the Art** <note>original: "Filii Artis"; a traditional term for students and practitioners of alchemy</note> subsequently go astray. In reading through many of their different books, they find such a multitude of these philosophical names that they fall into error, miss the right road, and wander miserably around in the great labyrinthine building of the exceedingly skillful master **Daedalus** <note>The mythical architect of the Labyrinth; here representing the complex and confusing nature of alchemical literature</note>. ### The Triangle of the Center

They attempted to obtain the <term>Fleece</term> <note>referring to the "Golden Fleece" mentioned on the previous page</note> from animals or vegetables, but they all withdrew empty-handed, because the things from which they wanted to obtain it were not capable of imparting it to them. <note>original Latin: "Fatum enim est & videtur consilium eorum, qui suadent, in aliquo quærendum esse loco, id, quod in eo non continetur, naturaliter."</note> For it is fate, and it seems to be the counsel of those who suggest it, that one searches in a place for that which is not naturally contained within it.

This happens, however, because:
Firstly, they did not know the <term>suitable matter</term> <note>original Latin: "Materiam convenientem"</note>, or the right fundamental <term>matter</term>. 

Secondly, they did not know the <term>required operation</term> <note>original Latin: "Operationem debitam"</note>, the right preparation for the Work. For they did not know how to judge what the <term>first principles of the thing</term> <note>original Latin: "prima Principia rei"</note> or the nearest origin of the <term>matter</term> <note>original Latin: "Materiæ"</note> might be. These are three, namely: <term>mercury</term> <note>represented by the symbol ☿</note>, <term>sulphur</term> <note>represented by the symbol 🜍</note>, and <term>salt</term> <note>represented by the symbol 🜔</note>; and then the <term>subject</term> in which these both <note>mercury and sulphur</note> are mixed by nature in a proper measure. This <term>subject</term> is called <term>Lechezech</term> <note>A technical term likely derived from Hebrew "lekhach" (substance/instruction) or "lach" (moist), used by some alchemists to refer to the prime matter or "virgin earth" containing the three principles.</note> by the philosophers. (a)

Thirdly, because it is difficult and almost impossible to <term>prepare</term> <note>original German: "præpariren"</note> one alone, how much more difficult—indeed, entirely impossible—it is to <term>fix</term> <note>original German: "figiren"; to make a volatile substance solid or stable under heat</note> and <term>perfect</term> <note>original German: "perfectioniren"</note> both at once.

(a) <note>original Latin: "Qui capit hoc verbulum felix esse potest."</note> He who understands this little word can be happy.

->Explication of the Center<-

## Whether this most worthy King might perhaps be understood as the **Sun** <note>represented by the symbol ☉, denoting Gold</note>

<margin>(b) Nor do those who work only with gold act according to the intention of the Philosophers.</margin>
There are others who exert themselves to hunt this most precious prize from (b) **Gold**, yet they also do not act <term>according to the intention of the Philosophers</term> <note>original Latin: "secundùm intentionem Philosophorum"</note>. For although its **Sulphur** <note>represented by the symbol △, referring here to the internal fire or "soul" of the metal</note>, which proceeds from the force and power of its own fire, may first <term>tinge</term> <note>to "tinge" is to alchemically transform the color and nature of a base metal into a noble one</note>, it is nonetheless not capable of generating other **Gold** <note>symbol: ☉</note> alongside itself, and much less capable of drawing the same to higher states or multiplying it. 

From this, it also follows that everyone—as many as think that silver and the remaining common, diminished metals can be transmuted into gold by means of gold—will happen to be deceived, as will those who are listened to with pleasure. For gold possesses no more color than it requires for itself to color its own **quicksilver** <note>original Latin: "argentum vivum," or mercury; the theory held that gold was a perfect balance of mercury and sulphur</note>. Experience has taught learned men that a <term>medicine</term> <note>an alchemical preparation or elixir</note> can indeed be made from gold that is able to <term>tinge</term> pure silver, or another properly purified metal, into a perfect gold color; however, no more **Gold** <note>symbol: ☉</note> is created by this art than the weight of gold from which such a medicine was originally drawn. When, however, the Philosophers spoke of gold and its preparation, they meant **Virgin Gold**...

<summary>The author argues that using common metallic gold to create the Philosopher's Stone is a mistake, as gold contains only enough "color" (transmutative power) for itself and cannot multiply or transform other metals without losing its own mass. He introduces the concept of "Virgin Gold" as the true starting point for the Work.</summary>

But is this a **costly work** <term>Opus sumptuosum</term> or an extremely expensive undertaking / and is it truly said that both the rich and the poor can attain it? If it were so [expensive], only the rich would be able to acquire it / whereas the poor would have to stand back. ## ->Whether it perhaps be mercury alone?<-

Others are those who wish to prove their **Art** <term>Alchemical mastery</term> (c) from **mercury** <note>original symbol: ☿; referring to quicksilver</note> alone / but they err greatly / for as that Philosopher reports: First they **sublimate** <term>heating a substance to vapor and condensing it back to solid to purify it</term> it / then after 80 days / they **fix** <term>to make a volatile substance solid and resistant to heat</term> it in a gentle fire / until it attains a proper metallic cast. Now it is well known to the true **Philosophers** <note>alchemical sages or natural philosophers</note> / that the one true **Medicine** <note>the Philosopher's Stone</note> has a single origin (which is perfected from the suffering matter alone without mercury as the active agent) <note>original Latin: "quae ex sola materia patiente sine ☿ agente perficit"</note>. Secondly, it is also well known to them / that mercury has such impurity about it / that it is impossible for it, in and of itself—without the addition of sophisticated tricks and labors—to be either **dissolved** <term>solviren</term>, **digested** <term>digeriren</term>, or **coagulated** <term>coaguliren</term>, much less perfected. And I would hold as my opinion / that even if he [the alchemist] had already overcome both of the aforementioned initial labors and came to the third / it [the mercury] would, according to its habit, put on its messenger-shoes <note>a reference to the winged sandals of the god Mercury; an alchemical metaphor for the substance evaporating or volatilizing</note> and ask the air to be its godfather / indeed, to speak truly / the whole mess would break loose / and everything would go to the devil in the air <note>meaning the experiment would fail spectacularly, likely through an explosion or the substance vanishing as gas</note>.

# Explanation of the Center in...

...[a] special consent, betrothal, and alliance of the celestial stars with that which is to be born; among which stars, each one knows how to separately impart its own nature and properties (many of which are very contrary in themselves, and found to be even more contrary when all taken together).

From this follows a distinction of the **elements** from which the metals are produced, and furthermore a special distinction in the **perfection** or completeness of the metals. For just as an unequal generation results in an unequal perfection—which is either **pure or impure**, that is, either **perfect or imperfect** <note>original Latin/German: "quæ vel pura vel impura, das ist entweder perfect oder imperfect ist"</note>—so too one matter precedes another; and after these are mixed and united with one another, they are transformed into minerals, transposed, and **assigned their specific properties** <term>proprietirt: the process by which a substance acquires its distinct alchemical "signature" or specific physical characteristics</term>.

->Whether it might perhaps be crude mercury, gold, and silver.<-

Many people are found who believe themselves to be closer to the truth than others, especially those who wish to obtain the **fleece** <term>vellus: a reference to the Golden Fleece of Greek mythology, symbolizing the successful completion of the Great Work</term> from **mercury, gold, and silver** <note>represented by the symbols ☿, ☉, and ☽</note>. They believe that by mixing these, one can extract the **souls** <note>original: *Animas*</note>; and since these souls would then originate from the most perfect bodies and were introduced through the most noble spirit, they think it inevitable that they must **tinge** <term>tinge: the alchemical power to "stain" or transform a base metal into the nature of gold or silver</term>. 

But these people also err greatly; they are deceived and led astray by the names used by the **Philosophers** <note>A term used for alchemical authorities like Hermes Trismegistus or Geber</note>, because they apply those names to physical, common bodies exactly as they are set down by the Philosophers. For example, the Philosophers write that **mercury, salt, and sulfur** <note>represented by the symbols ☿, ⊖, and 🜍</note> are the **principles of the Philosophers** <note>original: *principia Philosophorum*</note>, and they say this on account of their colors.

Others [say] that they are **copper, iron, and lead** <note>represented by the symbols ♀, ♂, and ♄</note>, also because of their colors.

### ->Whether it then might perhaps be a (a) Mineral, such as Arsenic or Orpiment or other similar things<-

( a ) Since the question arises here whether it might be a **Mineral**—that is, such a **matter** <note>original: *materia*</note> which exists indeed in a body, whether metallic or non-metallic, yet exists outside of perfection or the **circle of fixity** <term>circulo fixitatis: a state where a substance is "fixed" and can no longer be changed or evaporated by fire</term>. Thus, we cannot say otherwise with a good conscience than that it is a mineral matter. the like—that would not suit us well, and would agree poorly with the truth (which is known to all true possessors of the art). Yet there are others who merely labor to conquer and **fix** <term>figiren: the process of making a volatile substance stable in fire</term> the minerals—and among them, **Arsenic** <note>represented by ⧖</note>, **Orpiment** <term>Auripigmentum: a yellow arsenic sulfide used as a pigment and in alchemy</term>, and similar **volatile substances** <note>original: *Volatilia*</note>. ...would do so. This one separates the three **Principles** <term>Principia: the "Tria Prima" of Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury which Paracelsians believed composed all matter</term> <note>original symbols: 🜔 🜍 & ☿</note> from **Antimony** <note>original symbol: ♁</note>. He puts them together for **digestion** <term>digestionem: the process of maturing a substance through gentle, continuous heat</term>, and obtains a red **precipitate** <note>original: *præcipitat*</note>; this must then be **fermented** with **Sulfur** and **Gold** <note>original symbols: 🜍 ☉</note>. If this does not produce a **Universal Medicine** <term>universale: a "cure-all" substance capable of transmuting any metal or curing any disease</term>, it at least produces a good **particular** <term>particularchen: an alchemical result that only works on specific metals rather than all of them</term>. One part **transmutes** <note>original: *tingiret*</note> 40, 50, up to 60 parts. (Keep this in mind so you do not err; we shall note it down). It will provide and remain a **Royal Medicine** <term>Medicinam Basilicam: a high-grade medicinal preparation, often derived from antimony</term>.

Another man tortures himself with **Arsenic** to **fix** it, then to **dissolve** it, to **coagulate** it, and to **reiterate** <term>reiteriren: to repeat a process, such as distillation, many times to increase potency</term> this up to the thirtieth time, which occurs over nine months—and thus exactly matches the **Philosophical time** <note>The "Philosophical Year" or nine-month period mirroring human gestation was a common symbolic timeframe for the Great Work</note>. They handle this as carefully as peasants handle cow dung, namely as follows: they **calcine** **Silver** <note>original symbol: ☽</note> with **Mercury** <note>original symbol: ☿</note> into a lime; this they place in a strong **furnace fire** <note>original: *Fluß 🜂*</note>, and to one drachm of lime they pour half a drachm of the <note>original: arsenic symbol ⚻</note> **arsenical** [substance]. This is then supposed to draw into the silver lime (or perhaps into the glowing crucible) and **destroy** it, making it **potable** <term>potabel: drinkable; alchemists sought "potable" metals for use as powerful medicines</term> and **penetrating** <term>intrant: the quality of a substance that allows it to "enter" and transform the interior of a base metal</term>. Afterward, **Copper** <note>original symbol: ♀</note> is **transmuted** along with the **Silver** <note>original symbol: ☽</note>. 

However, when it comes to the **cupel** <term>Capelle: a shallow, porous vessel used to test the purity of gold and silver by exposing them to high heat</term>, it sucks the fire in so artfully that the silver dust remains **fixed**, yet can only be **reduced** back to metal with great difficulty. One part **transmutes** one part of copper into nothing; and such **Adepts** quietly count their "profits" behind their ears <note>A German idiom implying the profits are non-existent or purely imaginary</note>.

The rest of them torture and plague themselves with **Orpiment**, **Vitrils**, **Nitre**, **Sal ammoniac**, **minerals**, and earths with **Sulfur** <note>original: *Sulob Suebur*, likely a corruption or anagram for *Sulphur*</note>, and other such foolish things, child’s play, and the like, which are despised for the **transmutation** of metals. But when it comes to the **projection** <term>ad projiciendum: the final stage where the alchemical medicine is cast upon a base metal to turn it into gold or silver</term>, they are either...

<summary>This page critiques alchemists who waste years on complex "Sophist" recipes involving arsenic, antimony, and common minerals. The author mocks their failures at the cupel and their imaginary profits, contrasting these with true philosophical practice.</summary>

# The Trigon of the Center

...will be stable, and even less so in minerals or animals which are distant from **Gold** <note>original: *Auro*</note>, and are less suited to the generation of gold than gold itself. Nature also **delights in a nature similar to itself** <note>original: *Natura quoque sibi simili natura gaudet*</note>, and transmutates into such things more easily than into foreign ones. But since in the **transmutation** <term>transmutatione: the alchemical process of changing one substance or metal into another, specifically into gold or silver</term> of an **imperfect body** <note>original: *corporis imperfecti*</note>, the highest perfection or a **perfect medicine** <term>medicina: in alchemy, this refers to the "Elixir" or "Philosopher's Stone," which was thought to "heal" base metals of their impurities to make them gold</term> is required—which cannot be drawn from any source more closely than from that which is most perfect and of its own nature—for more powerful and closer things must always be chosen in all matters for the sake of an easier transition. Since all perfection of bodies also depends on the **tempering** <note>original: *temperamento* — the balanced mixing of elements or qualities</note> of their parts in composition, nothing will be more tempered than gold, or closer to this work. Therefore, this perfection of transmutation must be sought from the **Gold (of the philosophers)** <note>original: *Auro (philosophorum)* — a subtle, spiritual essence of gold rather than common metal</note> or from nothing at all.

I say, therefore, that these people err and fail terribly; for the nature of **Arsenic** <note>original: *Arsenici*</note> is **volatile** <term>volatilis: a substance that easily evaporates or turns to gas when heated</term> and **combustive** <note>original: *adustiva* — corrosive or burning</note>, and it is very difficult to **fix** <term>figiren: to make a substance "fixed" so that it remains stable and solid under the heat of fire rather than evaporating</term> the same. And in my opinion, at the very least, before it is brought to any perfection in the true **philosophical manner**, it would happen to it just as it does to **Mercury** <note>original symbol: ☿</note>, and everything would burn away into a red powder. Since it is none of these, I say once again that it is the **matter in which the power of the Spirit of the soul** <note>original: *materia in qua sit virtus Spiritus animæ*</note> and

# Prologue.

Since we have previously discussed more extensively in the preparatory discourse delivered to the <term>Sons of the Art</term> <note>original: *Filios Artis* — a traditional title for students or practitioners of alchemy</note> and promised to show them the foundations of the science—or (so to speak) upon what the City of God is built and now stands eternally firm—as well as what it is and how one is to be led through the right path; we wish here to apply ourselves to a beloved philosophical brevity and bypass all long-winded discourses and conversations. In the meantime, however, we shall <unclear>set forth</unclear> the matter in such a way that even the unlearned, provided they can read, may understand it—indeed, even little children can learn it, because it is truly <term>child's play</term> <note>original: *Ludus Puerilis* — an alchemical term suggesting that once the "secret" is known, the physical operation is simple and requires little effort</note>:

<summary>In this prologue, the author promises a concise and simple explanation of the alchemical "science," contrasting it with a previous longer discourse. He claims the process is so straightforward that even children or the unlearned can master it once the foundations are understood.</summary>
<keywords>Sons of the Art, City of God, philosophical brevity, child's play, alchemical foundations, instruction</keywords>

<meta>This page follows the prologue's promise of simplicity. ->Therefore<-

Jesus, God’s most beloved Son,
Fill the pen during the writing,
Through the Spirit from your throne,
Let us drive out all falsehood;
Upon the children of this world
What is untrue remains more readily clung to,
Than the sweet tabernacle of truth, <note>original: *Warheits-Zelt* — literally "truth-tent." This refers to the dwelling place of divine reality, contrasting with the fleeting illusions of the material world.</note>
Yet fulfill the longing
Of the one who trusts in your Word,
And from the very depths of the heart,
Builds upon you through earnest prayer;
Help him, LORD, at every hour,
Let Wisdom accompany him,
And lead him, well-prepared, onto the field, <note>In this context, the "field" (*Plan*) refers to the arena of spiritual work or the laboratory of alchemical practice.</note>
To rightly prepare his heart for God,
So that he may run the path of life;
Lead him to the radiance of wisdom, <note>original: *Weißheits-glast* — "Glast" is an archaic German term for luster or shining brilliance.</note>
Bless the effort, gently moisten it, <note>The request to "moisten" (*befeuchte*) the work carries alchemical weight, referring to the "Divine Dew" or spiritual grace needed to "animate" the dry matter of study.</note>
With the true Guest of Truth,
Of your Spirit; Lord, enlighten him.

## The First Chapter.
# Of the True Philo-
# sophical Matter.

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut border containing an ornamental capital letter W.</image-desc>

We should rightly begin our writing with Chemistry <note>original: *Chymia*</note>, defining what it is, its name, its truth and falsehood, its usefulness and uselessness, its operation, and the three principles <term>Sulfur 🜍, Mercury ☿, and Salt ⊖</term> <note>These three substances, known as the *Tria Prima*, were believed by alchemists to be the fundamental components of all material things.</note>. Or we might have spoken of the Chemist <note>original: *Chymico*</note>, his duties, the necessary state of his mind or spirit, his knowledge, his learning or lack thereof, and his conduct toward God, his neighbor, and his own calling or work. Or we could also have dealt with the question: Is it possible to transform the lesser metals into higher ones through art? Is it possible for gold to be prepared by art? However, we shall set all this aside—since all these matters are described extensively in a separate treatise called the *Chemical Rules* <note>original: *Regulæ Chymicæ*</note>, which, God willing, shall soon be shared with the reader through the press—and proceed to the title of our first chapter, which treats the true Philosophical Matter.

<meta>Catchword: All [original: Omnis]</meta>

<summary>This page marks the formal beginning of the treatise, introducing the "Philosophical Matter" as the primary subject. The author acknowledges common introductory topics like the nature of chemistry and the ethics of the alchemist but defers them to a future publication titled the "Chemical Rules."</summary>

<keywords>Chemistry, Philosophical Matter, Sulfur, Mercury, Salt, Alchemy, Transmutation, Chemical Rules, Ethics of Science</keywords>

Explanatory Appendix.

<meta>catchword: from</meta>

Every error in the art arises from this: that they do not take up the proper matter; and thus, he who does not know the beginning does not achieve the end, and he who does not know what he seeks does not know what he will find. <note>original Latin: "Omnis error in arte existit ex eo, quod debitam materiam non accipiunt..."</note>

Now, so that the beloved son <note>In alchemical traditions, the teacher often addresses the reader as "son" (*filius*), signifying a spiritual or initiatory lineage.</note> may know what it is, I say that it is neither gold nor silver, neither iron nor copper, neither tin nor lead, neither mercury (☿) nor antimony (♁), neither mineral nor metal, neither animal nor vegetable, neither fiery, watery, earthy, nor airy—and yet it is all of these things together at once. It is a mineral and a metal, a Moon (☽) <note>Silver</note> and a Sun (☉) <note>Gold</note>, a Mars (♂) <note>Iron</note> and a Venus (♀) <note>Copper</note>, a Jupiter (♃) <note>Tin</note> and a Saturn (♄) <note>Lead</note>, a mercury (☿), an animal and a vegetable, a Fire (△), Water (▽), Earth (🜄), and Air (🜁). So that I do not fail to provide its chemical name, know that it is called the Chemical Essence <term>Ens Chymicum</term>, or Mercury doubled by the Mercury of the Wise <term>Mercurius Sophorum</term>.

We say that quicksilver <term>Argentum vivum</term> is the primary matter of this kind of work, and truly nothing else; whatever is added to it arises from it. For this reason, it is called Mercury doubled by the Mercury of the Wise. Therefore, do not believe this to be the common mercury <note>Alchemists distinguished between "vulgar" mercury (the liquid metal) and "philosophical" mercury, a purified or spiritualized state of the matter.</note>, but that of the Philosophers; for common mercury is treated in various ways but to no avail, for common mercury and its preparation differ greatly from the Mercury of the Philosophers.

And now that we have established its certainty, know that we also wish to examine and consider its origin or fundamental root <term>Radium fundamentalem</term> more closely. When this is done, we find that it is a universal ore, which is related and belongs to neither this one nor that one, yet is more akin to one metal than another; it flows, however

<summary>This page defines the "First Matter" of alchemy as a paradoxical substance that contains all elements and metals yet is none of them individually. It specifically identifies this matter as "Mercury of the Wise," distinguishing it from the common quicksilver used by ordinary blacksmiths or chemists.</summary>

<keywords>First Matter, Mercury of the Wise, Ens Chymicum, Quicksilver, alchemical symbols, Philosophical Mercury, universal ore, Radium fundamentalem</keywords>

### Explanatory Appendix.

...from a Venereal vein <note>original: "Venerischen Ader." In alchemy, Venus refers to copper; a "Venereal vein" refers to a source of copper-bearing ore or a specific "vein" of metallic energy.</note>; not that it is **totally** <note>original Latin: "totaliter"</note> equipped and adorned with Venereal properties, but when we consider its **effect of exaltation** <term>Exaltations-effect</term> <note>Exaltation is an alchemical process that raises a substance to a higher state of purity or power.</note>, we must confess that it belongs close to Gold—and indeed closer than all other metals. So that we may now call it by its own name, let the **Son** <note>original Latin: "Filius." The traditional address for an alchemical student.</note> know that it is none other than the aforementioned *Lechezech* <note>A cryptic name, possibly derived from Hebrew roots or a specific cipher used by this author's school.</note>, whose correct name, known to everyone, consists of 7 dead and 7 living letters. Thus, its name-number is 7 times 7; the **solution-number** <term>solucions-Zahl</term> is 613 <note>The number 613 is significant in Jewish tradition as the number of commandments (Mitzvot) in the Torah; its use here suggests a Kabbalistic influence on the author's numerology.</note>; the **birth-number** <term>Geburts-Zahl</term> is 666. The **symbol** <term>Character</term>, however, which was imparted to it by all the **Adepts** <note>original Latin: "Adeptis." Those who have mastered the "Great Work" of alchemy.</note>, is ♁ <note>The symbol for Antimony or the Earth.</note> or also <margin>☿ <gloss>Mercury</gloss></margin>, all of which aims at nothing other than its birth.

It is a little male and a little female <note>A reference to the "Rebis" or the hermaphroditic nature of the prime matter, containing both active/masculine and passive/feminine principles.</note>, a red and a white; it is leaden, yet of a somewhat stronger softness; it is found in all pharmacies (though the best kind is that which has never come into any **Fire** [△]). There you have it clearly and distinctly; I cannot tell it to you any other way. Try it now, and because I know that you will consider it good, keep it in the quiet fear of God.

Yet, most beloved **Sons**, do not think that it is a silver or **Gold** [☉], but rather their **Primary Essence** <term>primum Ens</term> <note>The "First Being" or fundamental essence of a substance before it takes a specific form.</note>, in which resides the indestructible **generative power** <term>Gebährungs-Krafft</term>, not yet weakened by the **Fire** [△]; for know that even the most perfect metal, above which there is nothing, can finally be destroyed and uselessly spoiled by the **Fire** [△], and this destruction is most harmful to it; one also loses

<summary>The author identifies the prime matter through the cryptic name "Lechezech" and numerological codes (613, 666), describing it as a dual-natured substance found in pharmacies but easily ruined by heat. He cautions that this "Primary Essence" must be handled carefully to preserve its innate generative power, which even gold can lose if mistreated by fire.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the author's personal narrative regarding his alchemical breakthroughs. ### Explanatory Appendix.

...a considerable quantity of the gold which, after the **calcination** <term>Calcination</term> <note>A process of heating a substance to high temperatures, often in a crucible, to drive off volatile components or reduce it to a powder.</note> was completed, became volatile. I frequently and repeatedly experienced this in my **sophistical labors** <term>Laboribus Sophisticis</term> <note>original Latin: "Laboribus Sophisticis." In alchemy, "sophistical" refers to false, deceptive, or purely chemical operations that lack the true philosophical "soul" of the work.</note>, until a loving **Philosophical Ariadne’s Thread** <term>Filum Ariadneam</term> <note>original Latin: "Filum Ariadneam." Referring to the thread that guided Theseus out of the Minotaur's labyrinth; here, it represents the secret key or logic that leads an alchemist out of confusion.</note> was reached out to me through the merciful hand of God’s blessing. My errors were then powerfully set before my eyes, and I learned that the **seed of gold** <term>Semen auri</term> <note>original Latin: "Semen auri." The generative principle or "sperm" of gold that alchemists believed could grow if planted in the right "earth."</note>—the gold-seed most highly blessed by God—is still volatile and has not yet reached its full **maturity** <term>Maturität</term> in the first melting and separation. Through the violent power of fire, it leaves the **Sun** ☉ <note>The alchemical symbol for gold.</note> and flies off into the air. 

Since no one can say that the gold-seed or the **Matter of Wisdom** <term>Materia Sophiæ</term> <note>original Latin: "Materia Sophiæ." The raw material required for the "Great Work."</note> provides a **tincture** <note>The ability to "tinge" or transform the color and nature of a base metal into gold.</note> and transmutates imperfect metals into perfect ones before its maturation—rather, it is found in an imperfect state within the **most perfect** <note>original Latin: "perfectissimo." Referring to metallic gold.</note> (before melting) as well as in the **most imperfect** <note>original Latin: "imperfectissimo." Referring to base ores.</note>, though found more perfect in one than the other. Because the perfect must be produced from an equally perfect root, I have—upon good advice—left all other metals, minerals, and ores alone. I have taken the gold **ore** <note>The original text uses the letter E followed by a symbol for ore.</note> offered to me and proceeded with it **in the fear of God, silence, and hope** <note>original Latin: "in Timore Dei, silentio & spe." A common alchemical motto emphasizing the spiritual requirements of the work.</note>, just as it was revealed to me. Now God has blessed me and made my wish come true, which broke forth from a humble heart. To Him be praise and honor, glory, fame, and thanks eternally. Amen.

<summary>The author describes moving away from "sophistical" or false alchemical experiments to focus on the "seed of gold" (Semen auri) found in ore. He attributes his success to divine guidance and the realization that the golden essence is easily lost through violent heat if not properly matured.</summary>

Erläuterungs-Anhang. 9

There is a stone / despised and small /
It looks like gold / and is well-disposed to it /
And it grows nearby / now I speak it freely /
Take it forth / and proceed correctly,
Be chaste and pure / keep yourself apart /
Pray to God with zeal / from whom it comes /
The Philosopher's Stone / believe me well /
And let this be said to you / etc.

## The Second Chapter.

### On the Preparation of the Philosophical Matter, and specifically first concerning the Secret Purification.

Since without the **destruction** <term>destruction</term> <note>In alchemy, destruction or *corruptio* is the necessary first step where the physical form of a substance is broken down to release its inner essence.</note> and breaking down of a thing / and specifically more than others a metal or mineral / nothing fruitful can be generated from it / unless it be / that through destruction the same is first **purified** <term>purificiret</term> / and through the permitted means / the pure is separated from the impure.

*For the true **Subject of the Stone** <term>Subjectum Lapidis</term> <note>original Latin: "Subjectum Lapidis." This refers to the base material chosen to begin the "Great Work."</note> must be applied to the work, but let it be purged and clean. The **sophistical purification** <term>Purgatio materiæ Sophistica</term> <note>original Latin: "Purgatio materiæ Sophistica." Sophistical purification refers to common chemical cleaning using salts or acids, which the author considers a "false" or purely physical method compared to the true "Philosophical" path.</note> of matter with **Sal-Ammoniac** 🜔, **Common Salt** 🜩, **Nitre** 🜫, and **Vitriol** 🜶 <note>The symbols 🜔, 🜩, 🜫, and 🜶 represent common chemical salts. The author argues that using these common chemicals is a lower form of chemistry, not the high secret of the philosophers.</note> is not the true, nor the Philosophical purification; rather, wise men possess another way of purifying the matter.*

Pour this lye over the intended powder; rub it in a marble mortar until it transforms into a liquid like the one before. ## Chapter Three.
### Concerning the Solution and Philosophical Opening, through the Universal Solvent, along with its Preparation.

That the **solution** <term>solutio</term> is necessary in the Philosophical Work cannot be denied by anyone who understands the process and its preparation. > *Let the Nymph be pure, the spring water most pure.* <note>original Latin: "Pura sit Nympha, purissima Lympha." In alchemical poetry, the "Nymph" often refers to the secret, transformative water or solvent used in the work.</note>

# 12 Appendix of Explanations.

...universal **Mercurial Water**, along with its preparation, as follows.

> Beware that you do not understand this to be that common and leprous mercury <note>Alchemists used the term "leprous" to describe ordinary, commercial mercury which they considered impure or "sick" and therefore unsuitable for the Great Work without significant preparation.</note> which is totally unfit for the **Sophic work** <term>opus Sophicum</term> <note>The "Wise Man's work," referring to the creation of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>; know indeed that our **mercury** <term>☿</term> is imprisoned and bound with many chains. No one frees and dispatches it except the **Philosopher**, who sees it daily, although the prison lacks windows and doors, for which reason the common folk do not see nor recognize it. But it is necessary that first the body be dissolved and its pores be opened, so that nature may operate. For what kind of solution that ought to be, I would wish the reader to be warned here: there is a twofold solution; though there are many other solutions, they are useless. Only one is truly **natural**, the second is **violent**, under which all others are comprised. The natural one is of such a kind that the pores of the body are opened in our water, whereby the digested **seed** <note>Alchemical "seed" refers to the essential spark or formal principle within matter that allows it to grow or transform.</note> is emitted and placed into its **matrix** <note>The "matrix" is the receptive vessel or substance, often compared to a womb, where the alchemical seed is nurtured.</note>. <note>original Latin: "Cave ne intelligas vulgarem illum & leprosum ad opus Sophicum totaliter ineptum, scias verò nostrum ☿ incarceratū esse, & compedibus permultis vinctum..."</note>

Divide this into two parts; pour one part over the **crude mercury** <term>☿ crudum</term>, put it together into a glass flask, whose mouth or screw-top is sealed with a **lute** <term>Luto</term> <note>A specialized chemical cement used to create an airtight seal on laboratory glassware.</note>, and yet for extra security with a fitting screw. Shake it well for about a quarter of an hour in such a way that the flask becomes entirely black inside from the mercury; then take it out, pour it into another such flask, proceed with it as taught above; meanwhile, have ready in a **fire-pan** <term>Glutpfannen</term>...

# Explanatory Appendix.

<margin>I 4</margin>

Now we shall also provide a brief report on the **extraction** and **preparation** of the **Philosophical Mercury** <note>original: "abstraction und Zubereitung deß Philosophischen Mercurii"</note>; however, it must be done in this manner: Take the viscous, dissolved **water** (🜄) <note>This refers to the "viscous water" mentioned on the previous page, created from gold and spirit.</note>, put it together into a **glass flask**, and atop it place an **alembic head** (🜔) with a **receiver**, well-sealed with **lute** <note>original: "verlutirt". Lute was a clay or paste used to make glass joints airtight.</note>. Distill it through a **sand bath** (🜢) using gentle heat. Place the distillate in a cellar or another cold place so that it **crystallizes**; keep these crystals for further use, and hear more: Take the **dregs** <note>original: "feces". In alchemy, this refers to the solid residue left after distillation.</note> which remained at the bottom of the distilling glass; wash the salts from them in a **copper** (♀) dish until finished, using clear **spring water** (🜄). Pour the water off cleanly, and evaporate the remaining moisture through gentle **fire** (🜂), so that it becomes like an **intangible mercury** (☿) <note>original: "unbegreifliches ☿". This suggests a substance so subtle or fine it can scarcely be grasped by the senses.</note>. Then take two parts of this mercury and one part of the crystals that formed earlier; put them together, sealed with strong lute, in a **circulating vessel** within an **ash bath** (🜪). Begin to govern the heat from the first degree up to the tenth. The **first degree** should be managed in such a way that one can barely sense that any warmth is present; continue this for a **month** (🜫) <note>The symbol 🜫 represents a "philosophical month," traditionally forty days.</note>. The **second degree** should be like the warmth that precedes the **rising of the sun**; this too is held for a **month** (🜫). The **third degree** is like a **hen brooding her eggs** in the hottest part of the nesting; also for a **month** (🜫). The **fourth degree** is like the heat of the **human pit of the stomach** <note>original: "Hertz-grübleins". This refers to the solar plexus or the depression at the base of the sternum, used as a traditional reference for "blood-warmth."</note>, maintained for as long as the others. And this is the test: that one should feel the same warmth on the **Philosophical Glass** as one feels upon one's own **heart**, without any—

# Explanatory Appendix.

Treatment of the **Philosophical Universal Gold-Seed**, blessed by **GOD**.

Know also, most beloved **Sons of Wisdom** <note>original: "Sophiæ Filii"; a traditional term for initiates of the alchemical arts.</note>, and all you who have applied yourselves to this work with diligence and a singular fear of **GOD** until now, and have proceeded to the mystery of the great mystery—the **Philosophical duplicated Mercury** <note>A specialized form of "animated" mercury believed to be the essential solvent in the creation of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>, desired by the whole world but attained by few, yet reached by you through the preparation of mercury: That you are bound to bring and offer in humility to **GOD**, your most beloved Father in Heaven, a special sacrifice of remembrance, thanks, and praise for this grace of Divine revelation and the secret communication regarding the preparation of this great work—without which all that follows would be null and in vain. And I admonish you that you should not forget this, considering the seemingly small effort and such clear revelation, nor set it aside because it was obtained with little trouble. 

For know, even though you already have before your eyes and under your hands the true **First Matter** <note>original: "Erste materiam"; the primordial substance required to begin the Great Work.</note>, and the living, greening, fruit-bearing **Gold-Seed** <note>original: "☉ Saamen"; the symbol ☉ represents Gold or the Sun. Alchemists believed metals grew from "seeds" like plants.</note>—precious, highly valued, and dearly obtained—blooming in its innermost nature; nevertheless, the blessing and consecration of the same lies in the powerful hand of the **Almighty**. To those who are of a pure heart, He imparts it through His merciful, blessing hand without extraordinary toil, worry, or labor—freely, and as if in sleep; just as I know well of examples of this. But to those who are unworthy, and those

# Explanatory Appendix.

...those who are of such a godless, perverse, and prideful heart, [He] can snatch and take away [the work] from their hands in an instant, and again as if in a *sleep*.

I therefore admonish you in God’s stead, out of Christian compassion and merciful love, that during the preparation <note>original: "Zubereitung"; the laboratory process of refining and maturing the alchemical substance.</note> of this work, a prayer—pleasing to God, loving, earnestly zealous, and ardently devout—along with wishes and sighs, should be offered daily and hourly; let this be the first and last of all your conduct, actions, and omissions before God.

Therefore, ask of God before the <unclear>preparation</unclear>, and He will not deny it to you; seek it, and you shall find it. Knock upon it through diligent labor, and it will open to you, and give itself to you to possess forever. <note>The author is paraphrasing Matthew 7:7, "Ask, and it shall be given you; seek, and ye shall find; knock, and it shall be opened unto you," applying this biblical promise directly to the success of the alchemical labor.</note>

After obtaining it, praise and glorify, honor and thank, with a mouth full of devotion, the God of all gods, the Father of all grace and mercy, who has deemed you worthy of such an inestimable good; its power and effect can be expressed by no orator’s tongue, much less be sufficiently described by us.

Now hear further the Sixth Chapter, concerning the Philosophical Furnace <note>original: "Philosophischen Ofen"; also known as an Athanor, this was a specially designed furnace intended to provide a constant, regulated heat over many weeks or months.</note>, and its preparation and accessories, etc.

Since we promised you in the heading of this chapter to provide and teach a sufficient explanation regarding the understandable preparation of the **Philosophical Oven**, and its associated particular pieces, then know: that this is yet one of the most necessary pieces of the **Universal Work**, wherein the entire **Regiment of <term>Fire</term>** <note>original: "$\Delta$s"; the triangle is the alchemical symbol for fire. The 's' is a German grammatical suffix.</note> must be maintained from the beginning until the end. And such is prepared in the following manner:

Choose for yourself a convenient location, situated towards the **sunrise** <note>East was considered the most favorable direction for alchemical work, symbolizing beginnings and solar influence.</note>, located near the ground, paved over and over with stones, and also provided with a good chimney and double doors; in this same place, measure out a space according to your convenience, which in length and breadth should be exactly **two feet** <note>original: "zwen Schuh"; a 'Schuh' or 'foot' was a common historical unit of measurement.</note> wide, and at this exact length and breadth, begin to lay a hearth of brick, **four fingers** high; coat the same with a good, strong **putty** <note>original: "Kitt"; in alchemy, this usually refers to 'lute,' a sealant used to make vessels and furnaces airtight.</note>, or according to your means, with an **iron plate**; build upon this again to the height of a **span** <note>original: "Spannen"; the distance between the tip of the thumb and the tip of the little finger when the hand is fully extended.</note> (yet know, that you should leave a small door for the <unclear>fire-</unclear>hole, which must afterwards be sealed up or provided with an iron plate); then place upon this same small door a **grate**, which should be made for this purpose and fit upon it, so that the coals can burn out properly upon it; Now leave another small door, and raise the other wall again to the height of a span; this same small door must again be sealed up or provided with an iron plate; then leave at the top a...

->## Beginning of the Explanation.<-

<meta>catchword: Iron</meta>

On one side, build a **ledge** the width of a hand, or slightly more. On this same ledge, make a hole as thick as a fist, so that it goes down through the **grate** to the **Fire** <note>original: "$\Delta$"; the alchemical symbol for the element of fire.</note>. Then, build up a **tower**, four to five **feet** <note>original: "Schuh"; literally "shoes," a historical measurement approximately 12 inches long.</note> high, so that it is one **span** <note>original: "Spannen"; the distance from the tip of the thumb to the tip of the pinky, roughly 9 inches.</note> wide on the inside and hollow all the way around. It should be built so that, at most, no more than four pieces of **charcoal** can lie side-by-side within it to fall onto the grate. Place a **cap** <note>original: "Hut"; literally "hat," referring to the removable top of the furnace tower.</note> upon this tower and seal it with strong **clay** <note>original: "Leymen"; likely "lute," a specialized airtight sealing paste used by chemists.</note>; then the oven is ready.

Hear further concerning its additional accessories: have a **pipe** made for yourself from good, strong **iron sheet metal**, as thick as a fist or slightly thicker as needed, and the length of a man. Fit this into the hole which, as stated, should go down to the **Fire**. The pipe should be adjusted in the middle so that it can be taken apart from time to time to clean out the **soot**. Upon this same pipe, fit a perforated iron plate in the style of a **small sieve**. Then, have a **knob** <note>original: "Knopf"; here referring to a bulbous or spherical joint or regulator in the piping.</note> made, also of good iron sheet metal, in the thickness of two **nut-heads** <note>original: "Nuts-Köpf"; likely referring to the size of a large walnut or a specific mechanical fitting.</note> placed one on top of the other, yet designed so that they can be taken apart and joined together again. The lower part of this should have a ledge the thickness of a fist, so that it can be fitted over the other pipe. Over this same ledge, a **small sieve** <note>original: <term>cribriolum</term>; Latin for a small sieve or perforated strainer.</note> is fixed with small iron nails in the same manner as the other, though the holes should not align with one another, so that the heat is somewhat tempered by the obstruction. In this same bottom part of the knob, a ring beaten from **lead** <note>original: "Pley"</note> the thickness of a finger is placed with several...

To do this, the sheet metal of the knob must first be cut away as far as the windows are intended to go.

...through and be able to lead it into your room; afterwards, have that same hole plastered over again in such a way that the pipe can still be taken out and put back in. Cover the furnace with a casing that stands on rollers, so that it can be rolled over or away from it at your pleasure. You may lock or fasten this casing as you wish, for it is of no great consequence. However, over the **knob** <note>original: "Knopf"; the bulbous viewing chamber in the furnace pipe.</note> in your room, have a small cabinet or desk <note>original: "Contörlein"; a small writing cabinet or bureau.</note> made according to your liking, which you can open and close with a door and a small lock.

I know a **philosopher** <note>original: "Philosophum"</note>, of whom I assure you that he is also a **Lion of the Golden Cross** <note>original: "Löwe deß güldenen Creutzes"; a title likely referring to a high-ranking member of an alchemical or Rosicrucian brotherhood.</note>. This man had his **philosophical globe** <note>original: "philosophischen Globum"</note>, or the so-called **philosophical brood-hen’s nest** <note>original: "Glückhennen-Nest"; a metaphorical name for the alchemical furnace where the "chick," or the Philosopher's Stone, is "hatched" through constant, gentle heat.</note>, gilded. The ring, which was near the wall, he had bypassed with small water pipes, and he placed a golden **tap** <note>original: "Granen"; a faucet or spout.</note> on it. He let everyone use it as a hand-basin or water-cask to wash their hands, they being quite ignorant of what it actually was.

This practice pleased me exceedingly well, so that I have used it in the aforementioned manner several times. However, because while washing the hands the water felt somewhat lukewarm, and some heat from the **fire** <term>$\triangle$</term> <note>The alchemical triangle symbol representing the element of Fire.</note> could otherwise be felt from the **knob**, I devised another **precaution** <note>original: "cautelam"</note> for my own necessity (which is not necessary to reveal here). It is left to each person to try their luck with it, and to consider the aforementioned as good for man as...

...to hide, and also to form from it whatever he pleases. Nevertheless, I wish to add another example <note>original: "motum"; here meaning a method or decorative arrangement.</note> here, which the (now-blessed) **White Eagle of the Golden Cross** <note>original: "Weiße Adler vom güldenen Creutz"; likely a pseudonym for a prominent member of the Rosicrucian or a similar alchemical brotherhood.</note> used to practice: This same person proceeded with the installation of the **philosophical globe** <note>original: "Globi philosophici"; the round glass vessel where the alchemical transformation takes place.</note> as instructed. Beneath it, he arranged a **cabinet** <note>original: "Tresor"; here referring to a decorative sideboard or display chest.</note>, which, at that same height, covered <margin>+ 1</margin> the entire wall of the room where the pipe <note>denoted in the original diagrams as "K."</note> was led through. 

He had lined this cabinet with red velvet; over the globe, he had mounted a **mask** <note>original: "Larve"</note> of a bearded man. On the cabinet were distributed the symbols of honor of the **seven planets** <note>In alchemy, the seven planets correspond to the seven metals: Sun/Gold, Moon/Silver, Mercury/Quicksilver, Venus/Copper, Mars/Iron, Jupiter/Tin, and Saturn/Lead.</note>, namely: the golden **harp of the Sun** <note>original: "Solis"</note>; the white doves and the bow and arrow of **Cupid or Venus**; the flashing bolts of fire along with the double **thunderbolt of Jupiter** <note>original: "Jovis"</note>; the crutch and scythe of **limping Saturn** <note>original: "Saturnis"</note>; and the victorious helmet, shield, sword, and lance of **blood-cruel Mars** <note>original: "Martis"</note>. 

All of these were so artfully crafted from beaten silver that they appeared to be a true wonder of nature. They were arranged so thoughtfully that the helmet of Mars was placed upon the philosophical globe and its mask; beneath it hung the shield, sword, and lance, tied with various **ribbons** <note>original: "Favoren"; decorative knots or favors.</note> in such a way that it looked almost more **casual** <note>original: "liderisch"; meaning unstudied or informal, as if the items were just hung there naturally.</note> than man-made. To ensure the secret would not be noticed, the other six planets were treated in the same manner. Above the entire display was a banner made of white **satin** <note>original: "Atlaß"</note>, on which were written these words: ***Thus one journeys to the stars*** <note>original: "Sic itur ad Astra"; a quote from Virgil's Aeneid, suggesting that the alchemical path leads to divine or celestial heights.</note>.

# Explanatory Appendix.

What do you think now, beloved sons <note>original: "Filii"; a traditional term of address for students of the alchemical arts.</note>, is this not a truly sensible astral precaution <note>original: "astralische Cautela"; here referring to the clever use of astrological/planetary symbolism to hide the true nature of the work.</note> and concealment of such a noble work?

### The Seventh Chapter.

## Concerning the Philosophical
## Egg, its Form and Shape,
## Matter and Preparation.

Regarding the description of the philosophical glass <note>The "Philosophical Egg" or "ovum philosophicum" is the hermetically sealed glass vessel where the Great Work is performed.</note>, we wish to briefly add the following: have a glass-material prepared at a glassworks in the following manner: take 6 pounds <note>indicated by the symbol "℔"</note> of the best grapevine ashes (do not be surprised that I set so much, for one can bring no smaller amount of material into flux in the melting furnace), half as much of the best white pebbles <note>original: "Kißlingstein"; likely flint or quartz pebbles used to provide silica for the glass.</note>, and an equal part of common salt <note>original: "Sal com. ana."; from Latin *sal commune* and *ana*, meaning equal parts of each.</note>. 
Let it melt well together, and when it is in flux, then add 6 loth <note>A traditional German unit of weight, roughly 15-17 grams.</note> of borax into it; let it stand for 8 hours <note>represented by the symbol for Jupiter "♃" and the number 8; in this context, it refers to the duration of the refining process.</note> therein, so that it purifies itself well; then it is fit for your purpose.
Now give instructions to make a glass which has the following form and shape.

# Explanatory Appendix.

K 2

This, however, shall be its shape: it should be perfectly round in every direction, like a true circle, with a thickness of the glass material of a middle finger’s width, or perhaps slightly more; it should have a small neck one finger long, with an opening wide enough that one can turn a small iron awl <note>original: "Pfrimlein"</note> around inside it as desired. This glass should be left in the cooling oven <note>original: "Kühl-Ofen"; an annealing furnace used to slowly cool glass to prevent it from shattering due to internal stress.</note> for 3 <term>days</term> so that it may properly anneal and cool in the best possible manner. Its size should be 8 apothecary ounces, or 16 <term>loth</term> <note>A traditional German unit of weight; 16 loth is approximately half a pound.</note> according to the silver weight <note>original: "☽ Gewichte"; the symbol of the moon represents silver, used here to specify the weight standard for the vessel.</note>. And when it has been prepared according to the prescribed and illustrated pattern, it is then fit and useful for your purpose. This, then, is the description of the <term>Philosophical Egg</term>, the <term>Aludel</term>, a vessel blessed with well over a hundred names, in which both ancient and modern philosophers (provided they have truly achieved the philosophical name and omen <note>original: "Nomen & Omen Philosophicum"; a Latin phrase suggesting their title as "philosopher" is not just a name but a true sign of their success.</note>) have performed the operations of the entire Great Work <note>original: "Universal-Werck"; the total process of the alchemical transformation.</note> in all things according to nature.

> My son, take this little pattern
> Of the true Peacock’s Egg <note>original: "Pfauen Ey"; likely a reference to the "Peacock's Tail," a stage in the alchemical process where many colors appear in the vessel.</note>
> Bury it deep in the hot earth,
> Just wait, and have no regret.

## The Eighth Chapter.
# Of the Sealing of the Glass
# and the Instruments
### belonging thereto.

<margin>be</margin>
<margin>Whoever</margin>
Now we will provide a simple account of the Seal <note>original: "Sigillo"; the airtight closing of the alchemical vessel, essential for maintaining the pressure and "spirit" of the substance within.</note> and its specific instruments, which are illustrated below.

<image-desc>Woodcut illustration of laboratory tools. At the top is a long, thin metal rod with a hooked end and a turned wooden handle. Below it are three cylindrical objects: one shown from a top-down perspective showing a circular face with a central mark, and two shown horizontally with small clusters of holes or indentations on their sides.</image-desc>

The seal should not be a "sophistical" one <note>deceptive or false; in alchemy, this refers to using mundane, physical pastes that do not undergo the proper philosophical transformation.</note>, such as one made from egg white, unslaked lime, brick-dust, clay, and cow-hair, nor from filings or iron-shavings, and other such things.

Rather, it should be the right and true Seal of Jupiter <note>original: "Sigillum Jovis"; while Jupiter usually represents the metal tin in alchemy, here it refers to a specific method of sealing that involves fusing the glass itself or using a metallic plug.</note>, and the company of all the gods; for which reason, know the Seal of the great Hermes <note>original: "Sigillum deß grossen Hermetis"; referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of alchemy. A "Hermetic seal" is one made by melting the neck of the glass bottle shut.</note>, which—

<meta>catchword: —ich (continuing "welche" or "which")</meta>

<summary>This page introduces the "Seal of Hermes," explaining that a true alchemical seal must be a permanent, fused closure rather than a temporary paste or putty made from mundane materials.</summary>
<keywords>Sigillation, Seal of Jupiter, Seal of Hermes, laboratory instruments, hermetic seal, alchemical glass</keywords>

-># Explanatory Appendix.<-

...who carried it in this way in his <term>signet</term><note>original: "Perschafft"; a personal seal used to stamp wax or clay, here symbolizing the unique mark of the alchemist.</note>, signet-ring, or distinguishing mark.

<image-desc>Two small woodcut emblems showing geometric configurations. On the left, a shield contains an upright triangle with an inverted triangle inside. To its right is the phrase "or thus" <note>original Latin: "vel sic"</note>. On the right, a circle contains the same triangular motif. To its right is the fragmentary word "...ing" <note>original Latin: "andi", likely the suffix of "signandi", meaning "of signing" or "of sealing"</note>.</image-desc>

Others draw it differently; however, one may name it as they wish, for the specific style of drawing is not of great importance—provided that if one were to draw it correctly, in its proper size and with all the inscriptions written out, it would appear in the following manner:

## The Ninth Chapter.
### Of the Coals, their quantity and quality, required for the Work.

So that one cannot complain about the <term>obscurity</term><note>original: "obscurität"; referring to the common alchemical practice of hiding secrets in dark or confusing language.</note> and clouding of the Work, I will also speak a brief word of exhortation regarding the coals, whose quantity and quality are required for this Work. Concerning the quantity, the Work can be accomplished with a single wagon full, provided they are good coals; thus, no one can complain that the coals are too expensive for him, or that he must give the money he should spend on food and provisions to the <term>charcoal burners</term><note>Tradespeople who manufactured charcoal by slow-burning wood in specialized mounds.</note> for fuel every day and week. As for the quality, everyone knows well how to distinguish the <term>grades</term><note>original: "gradus"; here referring to the different burning intensities and properties of various wood types.</note> of wood. Oak wood is indeed the best for building, but its coals are the worst for burning and the most useless; they are also not fit for our intended Work to [be used]

Yet for certain reasons, where they are available, we choose above all others either **Beech** or **Pear-wood**; let everyone now know how to guide themselves sufficiently according to this.

## The Tenth Chapter.
# On the Artificial Fire /
## its Preparation and Governance.

That the <term>artificial fire</term><note>original: "Artificial-Feuer"; in alchemy, this refers not just to a literal flame, but to the carefully regulated heat or "secret fire" used to transform matter.</note> is not a minor, poor, or common matter, as all <term>sophists</term><note>original: "Sophisten"; a term used by alchemists to describe those who practice the art incorrectly or for greed, lacking true philosophical understanding.</note> believe, but a necessary—indeed the most necessary—and an artistic—indeed the most artistic—thing, I intend to prove with a few words.

I therefore pose this riddle to the foolish, and say with God Almighty from Job, chapter 39, verses 16 and 17: "The feathers of the peacock are more beautiful than the feathers of the stork, who leaves her eggs on the earth, and lets the <unclear>hot</unclear> earth hatch them."

### <insert>3</insert> Hear, O Sons. <note>original: "Audite Filii."</note>

Govern your fire in such a way, that the external heat does not overcome the internal heat; for if it should exceed it, the union could not take place, because excessive external heat would ravage and burn the matter. If the external fire were also less than is lively and proper, the spirit would remain in the matter without motion and without any action, nor would it perform anything with its **radical moisture**, <note>original: "humiditate radicali"; the fundamental life-fluid of matter.</note> that is, it would coagulate, dry out, and fix nothing. If you govern the fire rightly as is fitting in its degree and temperature, your matters will embrace one another, and cherish each other with mutual heat, and in a short time all things will be converted into water—not indeed common water, but a viscous <note>original: "glutinosam"; meaning sticky or oily, often referring to the "mercurial" stage.</note> one. ## The Eleventh Chapter.

# Concerning the <term>Philosophical Institution</term><note>original: "Philosophische Einsetzung"; refers to the formal setup or "installation" of the chemical materials into the vessel.</note>, Measure and Weight, also concerning the Son toward God his Almighty Father and Creator in Heaven, etc.

Take, therefore, in the name of GOD, of the **Doves of Luna** <note>original: "Tauben Lunæ"; a symbolic term for purified silver or the "white" stage of the mercury.</note>, one part [☽], three ounces [℥ iij] of **doubled Mercury of the Philosophers** <note>original: "Mercurii Philosophorum duplicati"</note>, and **crystallized Sulfur of the Philosophers** <note>original: "Sulphuris Philosophorum Cristallisati"</note>. Put **incense** <note>original: "Thus"</note> together into your glass; **seal it in the customary manner and as previously prescribed** <note>original: "sigillirs more solito, & antea præscripto"</note>.

For the **arrow** and **jewel of the Sun** <note>original: "Kleinod Solis"; the "Jewel of the Sun" refers to gold or the "red" solar principle.</note>, you must go backwards; understand me well: take less of the latter, but more of the former.

You will already know yourself how to add and take away; indeed, anyone who understands anything at all understands that when a **little boy** <note>original: "Knäblein"</note> is born, the male seed was present in greater quantity than the female; but for a **little girl** <note>original: "Mägdlein"</note>, it is the total opposite.

<meta>catchword: as</meta>

A red rose requires a red <term>Quintessence</term><note>original: "Q E", abbreviation for "Quinta Essentia"; the fifth element or pure spiritual essence extracted from matter.</note>. In summary: everything that grows forth red is tinctured and endowed with the red **Universal Tincture** <note>original: "Universal Tinctur"; the supreme alchemical medicine capable of transmuting base metals into gold and perfecting the human body.</note>.

Conversely, everything that grows forth white in color requires a similar white one.

That which is green requires a green one; that which is black, a black one; that which is yellow, a yellow one; and so on and so forth.

A similar **natural sympathy** <note>original: "Sympathiam naturalem"; the Hermetic principle that "like attracts like" and that objects share occult connections through their qualities.</note> is also required **universally** <note>original: "Universaliter"</note> by that which follows nature: the blessed **Universal Gold-Seed** <note>original: "Gold-Saame"; the germinal or "spermatic" principle within gold that allows it to "grow" or multiply.</note>. If an artist works it toward the red, it requires more of the red and the **Masculine**; on the contrary, for the white, it requires more of the white and the **Feminine seed**.

Regarding what was said above concerning **ounces**, know that these are not common, but **Philosophical Ounces**. One of these is [calculated thus]: 9 times 9, multiplied with 7 times 7, and then again with 3 times ten <unclear>21</unclear>, adding 3 more to it; thus the correct **Philosophical Weight** result of 444 comes out. Divide this again by 4. Thus you have the Philosophical Ounce with the number 111.

It is also free to everyone to double the same according to their pleasure, so that the number of both the **Masculine** and **Feminine gender**—as it pleases him—comes out to 222 and then 666 <note>In solar alchemy, 111, 444, and 666 are significant numbers related to the magic square of the Sun.</note>. Yet let him be reminded that when doubling the **matter** <note>original: "Materiæ"</note>, the **Philosophical Time** <note>original: "Philosophische Zeit"</note> must also be doubled.

Let this then be what I wished to report concerning the **Philosophical Institution** and its weight. Whosoever has ears to hear, let him hear what the Spirit says to the communities <note>A common alchemical appropriation of Revelation 2:7, implying the text contains hidden spiritual truths for the initiated.</note>.

I wish for everyone that he [may achieve] it in the best manner...

## The Eleventh Chapter.

# Of the Philosophical
# Preparation / Measure and Weight /

### also of the Son toward God

### Creator in the Heavens, etc.

Now we also wish to say a little about the **Philosophical preparation** <note>original: "Einsetzung"; refers to the "putting in" or charging of the materials into the alchemical vessel.</note>, as well as the correct, unadulterated **Philosophical weight**, and then about the praise of God, which is **fitting** <note>original: "gebühret"</note> to give to Him as the Most High at the preparation and beginning of the **Philosophical Universal Work** <note>The "Universal Work" refers to the creation of the Philosopher's Stone, which was believed to have power over all matter.</note>.

Therefore, in the name of God, take 1 ounce of the **Doves of Luna** <note>original: "Tauben Lunæ"; an alchemical code for purified silver or the "white" mercurial spirit.</note>, 3 ounces of the **duplicated Mercury of the Philosophers** <note>original: "Mercurii Philosophorum duplicati"</note>, and **Crystallized Sulfur of the Philosophers** <note>original: "Sulphuris Philosophorum Cristallisati"</note> together into your glass; **seal it in the usual manner and as previously prescribed** <note>original: "sigillirs more solito, & antea præscripto"; this refers to hermetically sealing the glass flask with heat to make it airtight.</note>.

For the **Arrow and Jewel of Sol** <note>original: "Pfeil und Kleinod Solis"; symbolic names for the "Red" or Gold-making process.</note>, you must proceed in reverse: understand me well, take less of the latter, but more of the former.

You will already know how to add and subtract for yourself; for everyone who understands anything at all understands that when a little boy is born, the **Masculine seed** was present in greater abundance than the **Feminine**; but if it is a little girl, it is the complete opposite.

A red rose requires a red **Quintessence** <note>original: "Q E", abbreviation for *Quintessentia*; the "fifth essence" or purest spiritual core of a substance.</note>.
