Conversely, everything that grows forth white in color requires a similar white [Quintessence].

What is green requires a green; what is black, a black; what is yellow, a yellow; and so on.

A similar **natural sympathy** <note>original: "Sympathiam naturalem"; the alchemical belief that like attracts like and that materials with similar properties or colors are naturally linked.</note> is also required **universally** <note>original: "Universaliter"</note> by the follower of Nature, the blessed **Universal Gold-Seed** <note>original: "Gold-Saame"; the spiritual "seed" or starting material used to "grow" alchemical gold.</note>. If an artist **labors** <note>original: "laboriret"; works in the laboratory</note> upon it for the Red [work], it requires more red and male [seed]; on the contrary, for the White [work], it requires more white and female seed.

One of these—**multiplied** <note>original: "Multiplicirt"</note> as 9 times 9 with 7 times 7, and then again with 3 times [ten plus] 1, with another 3 added to it—results in the correct philosophical weight **total** <note>original: "facit"</note> of 444; divide this again by 4. Thus you have the Philosophical Ounce with the number 111 <note>This cryptic arithmetic is typical of "Hermetic Gematria," where numbers represent spiritual proportions rather than literal measurements.</note>.

It is also free for anyone to double this according to their pleasure, so that the number for both the male and female sex—as it pleases him—comes out to 222 and then 666. However, let him be reminded that when doubling the **matter** <note>original: "Materiae"</note>, the **Philosophical Time** must also be doubled.

This is, then, what I wished to report concerning the **Philosophical preparation** <note>original: "Einsetzung"</note> and its weight. Whosoever has ears to hear, let him hear what the Spirit says to the congregations <note>A biblical reference to the Book of Revelation, signaling that the preceding text contains hidden, divinely-inspired secrets.</note>;

### The Thirteenth Chapter.
## Of Putrefaction, or Rotting, and also of the Highest Life in the Deepest Death.

That Putrefaction is the principal and most noble action, and also the *cause* <note>original: *causa*</note> from which all subsequent well-being, and human as well as metallic health and fruitfulness grows, and flows forth as if from a spring:

> Nature’s elements, and also the foods of generation and nutrition—the principle of which is almost the same—commence from putrefaction: for it is necessary that both putrefy and, through putrefaction, be resolved into a moisture, as if into the *first matter* <note>original Latin: *prima materia*; the formless base of all substance</note>. Then a <term>Chaos</term> <note>The state of primordial matter before it is organized into a new form</note> is formed, to which all things [necessary] for generation or nutrition... <note>The sentence continues on the following page.</note>

# Explanatory Appendix.

... [necessary things] are present within it; therefore, it is called **Digestion** <note>represented by the symbol <image-desc>alchemical symbol for digestion, a square with a cross inside</image-desc></note>, because just as food is broken down in the stomach before it passes into the blood and similar parts, so this operation grinds down the nourishment with a ripening <note>original Latin: *pepantico*; referring to heat that matures or "cooks" matter</note> and stomach-like heat, and in a way putrefies it, so that it may be better fixed, and changed from **Gold** <note>represented by the symbol <image-desc>alchemical symbol for Sol/Gold, a circle with a central dot</image-desc></note> into a sulfurous nature. Furthermore, it is called **Inhumation** <note>The act of burying or placing in the ground</note>, because through it the spirit is buried, and interred like a dead man. ]

No one who has wit and understanding can deny this or defend against it by saying "no." Let us take the example of a dead and deceased person: after he has died, he is scraped into the earth. Through the **moisture** <note>original: *humidität*</note> that penetrates to his innermost through the **pores** <note>original: *poros*</note>, he finally rots and decays into a **slime** <note>original: *Muco*</note> and poisonous, stinking sludge and filth. This same substance transforms back into that from which it was taken, no differently than any other earth. Thus, to look upon it, one cannot distinguish this earth from other rich soil, and according to human reason, one cannot perceive even the slightest speck of life in it. 

The reason is that the flesh and blood have been abandoned because the **soul** has departed from them. The flesh has rotted, becoming filth, dust, and ashes. The bones, the **rattling skeleton** <note>original: *Gerässel*</note>, the head, and the entire form of death lie there—black, hideous, and unsightly. He who previously could not bear to have another man, his fellow brother, look at him sourly, must now endure having dogs and beasts urinate on him <note>original: *anseichen*</note> and even defecate upon him <note>original: *hofiren*</note>.

## The Thirteenth Chapter.
### Concerning Putrefaction, or Rotting; also concerning the Highest Life in the Deepest Death.

That **Putrefaction** <note>The process of decay or decomposition, considered a vital step in "killing" the old form of matter so it can be reborn</note> is the most principal and foremost action, and also the **cause** <note>original: *causa*</note> from which all subsequent well-being—human as well as metallic health and fruitfulness—grows and flows forth as if from a spring;

> The elements of nature, as well as its nourishments, begin **generation and nutrition** <note>original: *generationem & nutritionem*</note> (the logic of which is nearly the same) from putrefaction: for it is necessary that both rot, and through putrefaction be resolved into a liquid state as if into the **first matter** <note>original: *primam materiam*; the primal, formless substance from which all things are created</note>. Then a **chaos** occurs, in which all things [necessary] for generation or nutrition...

...[nece]ssary things are within; for this reason it is called **Earth** <note>original: <image-desc>alchemical symbol: a square divided into four segments</image-desc>; a symbol representing the four elements or the earthly state of matter</note>, because just as food is broken down in the stomach before it passes into the blood and similar parts, so this operation grinds down the nourishment with **ripening and stomachal heat** <note>original: *calore pepantico & stomachali*; 'pepantic' refers to heat that aids digestion or the ripening of abscesses</note>, and in a way it putrefies it, so that it may be better fixed, and changed from a **solar** <note>original: <image-desc>alchemical symbol for the Sun (circle with center dot)</image-desc>; representing Gold or the Sun's perfect nature</note> state into a sulfurous nature. Indeed, it is even called **inhumation** <note>burial in the ground</note>, because through it the spirit is buried, and is interred like a dead man.

---

No one who possesses wit and understanding can deny this or defend against it by saying no. We take an example from a dead and deceased human: after he has passed away, he is buried in the earth. By the **moisture** <note>original: *humidität*</note> which penetrates through the pores to his innermost parts, he finally rots and decays into **mucus** <note>original: *Muco*</note> and toxic, stinking slime and filth. This same material transforms back into that from which it was taken, no differently than any other earth. Thus it is that, to all appearances, one cannot distinguish this same earth from other rich soil; and according to human reason, not even the **smallest speck** <note>original: *Pünctlein*</note> of any life can be sensed in him. The reason is that the flesh and blood are abandoned because the **soul** <note>original: *Seele*</note> has departed from them. The flesh has rotted, becoming **filth** <note>original: *Koth*</note>, dust, and ashes; the bones, **skeletal remains** <note>original: *Geräffel*</note>, head, and the entire form of death lie there—black, hideous, and unsightly. He who previously could not bear even a sour look from another man, his fellow brother, must now suffer dogs and animals to urinate upon him, and even to **relieve themselves** <note>original: *hofiren*; a euphemism for defecating</note> upon him.

It describes how heat and moisture break down the "solar" nature of matter into a sulfurous, earthly state, just as a body turns to dust.</summary>

### Explanatory Appendix.

He who once ruled others must now also be ruled by a 
shovel full of earth and be held beneath it; 
he who before protected others, 
cannot now shield himself from maggots and worms, 
snakes and adders. In short, 
he who lived before is now dead, and lies before 
everyone's eyes helpless and deceased. He who was 
before a human is now an image of death, a 
dwelling for worms and snakes, a stinking 
carcass <note>original: *Aaß*</note>, a helpless creature, from whom people would 
much rather flee than approach, 
much rather turn away their faces than look upon him. 
All this is caused by the gruesome and hideous, yet 
unavoidable image of death; for man, 
even if he lives 1000 years and yet another 1000 years, 
it is finally said for certain: Man, you must 
die <note>original: *sterben*</note>, and pay nature's debt, even down 
to the last farthing <note>original: *Grot*; literally a small coin, used here to mean "to the very last bit"</note>, bone, and little bone.

But in this terrible and fearful 
state, is all life gone forever? Is all hope 
of regaining it and enjoying it hereafter in a 
perfect form lost? Does 
the deceased then remain eternally dead, and has his spirit 
vanished like the spirit of a beast? Does 
the rotted, stinking, disregarded earth, 
ash, and dust remain forever in such an unsightly 
form, and is life, in summary <note>original: *Summariter*</note>, lost 
forever? To this we answer most properly: No! 
But just as the hand of God the Lord came

# Appendix of Explanations

...was led through it; very many dry bones of the dead lay there, and the field was large; there were many dead bones, and they were exceedingly and powerfully withered. And the **Lord** said to **Ezekiel**: "Prophesy to me of these dead bones, and say to them: 'You withered bones, hear the **Lord’s** word: Thus speaks the **Lord GOD** <note>original: "HErr HErr," a rendering of Adonai Elohim</note> concerning these bones: Behold, I will bring a breath <note>The original word "Odem" refers to the divine breath of life, similar to the Spirit.</note> into you, so that you shall become alive; I will give you sinews, and let flesh grow over you, and cover you with skin, and will give you breath, so that you become alive again; and you shall know that I am the **Lord**.'"

And I prophesied as I was commanded; then there was a rustling as I prophesied, and behold, there was a movement, and the bones came together again, each to its respective bone. And I looked, and behold, sinews and flesh grew upon them, and the **Lord** covered them with skin; but there was as yet no breath in them.

And he said to me (as to **Ezekiel**): "Prophesy to the wind, you Son of Man, and say: 'Thus speaks the **Lord GOD**: Wind, come here from the four winds, and breathe upon these slain, that they may live again.'"

And I prophesied as I was commanded; then the breath came into them, and they became alive again, and stood up on their feet, and they were a great army. And the **Lord** said to me: "(**Ezekiel**), these bones, you Son of Man, are the whole **House of Israel**; behold, now they say: 'Our bones are withered; behold, our hope is lost...'"

# Explanatory Appendix.

...rejoice that your matter is being blackened <note>Latin original: *denigrari*</note>, for it is the beginning of the work; and know that the head of the art is the **Raven** <note>Latin: *Corvus*, a symbol for the first stage of the Great Work</note>, which flies without wings in the blackness of night and the clarity of Day. Therefore, convert bodies into bodiless things, and take care that they putrefy <note>Latin: *putrefiant*</note>, for after the matter has been putrefied, it is ready to become a spiritual body. For in putrefaction, there is a weaving together of natures <note>Latin: *complexio*, referring to the balance of bodily humors or qualities</note>, where the **Masculine** and **Feminine Light** truly embrace and join together; from them a new light is born, to which no light in the whole world is similar.

Thus one sees that in **Putrefaction** <note>German: *Fäulung*</note> all is in all—indeed, the highest life follows from and is contained within the deepest death, the highest honor in the greatest shame, and the greatest bliss out of the greatest misery. Therefore, **Putrefaction** is necessary in our work, because nothing is ever born, or grows, or is given life, except after putrefaction; for if it has not been putrefied, it cannot be melted or dissolved, and if it has not been dissolved, it will be reduced to nothing. For unless that fixed stone is made volatile, and the volatile is given life and fixed, it will amount to nothing at all.

This **Putrefaction**, however, is poisonous and yet not poisonous; it is muddy <note>German: *kothicht*, implying something filthy or excremental</note> and yet clean, pure—yes, even the purest of all. In this state, all operations happen universally, which subsequently proceed in particular steps; here, they occur all at once. Our putrefaction is therefore neither filthy nor unclean, but it is a mixing of <term>Water <gloss>represented by the symbol $\nabla$</gloss></term> with <term>Water</term> through the smallest parts <note>Latin: *per minima*</note>, until the whole body becomes one. For in this <term>Water</term> all methods occur: namely, **sublimation**, **distillation**, **solution**, **calcination**, and **fixation**. Therefore, our grinding, dissolving, and other operations are not done with hands, but by the most gentle boiling of the **Sun** <note>Latin: *Solis decoctione*, referring to a slow, steady heat like that of the sun</note>.

# Explanatory Appendix.

For our whole operation is nothing other than the extraction of water from water <note>original: *▽a.*, the alchemical symbol for Water</note> and the releasing of this water over the earth <note>original: *🜃m.*, the alchemical symbol for Earth</note> until it putrefies and is purified; when it has been purified, the whole <term>Mastery</term> <note>Latin: *Magisterium*, the complete process of creating the Philosopher's Stone</note> will be directed by Divine help. For in this way the Spirit of the Lord was carried over the waters <note>A reference to the Book of Genesis</note> and the wind carried it in its belly <note>A famous phrase from the Emerald Tablet of Hermes Trismegistus, referring to the "air" or "spirit" containing the volatile essence</note>.

Therefore, putrefaction is a cause of <term>volatilization</term> <note>the process of turning a solid substance into a gas or vapor</note> or making things volatile—which is well to be noted—for the most significant part of the work depends on this process of making things volatile and making them fixed. For if you dissolve the fixed, and make the dissolved fly, and capture the bird <note>An alchemical metaphor: the "bird" is the volatile spirit that must be captured and solidified</note>, it will make you live safely. For the whole Mastery consists in volatilization and repeated fixation. O blessed volatilization of putrefaction, then, and putrefaction of volatilization, which generates all subsequent good things, and causes them to grow and sprout!

But beware, my son, lest in the beginning you burn the flower of blackness <note>The *nigredo* or black stage must be reached by gentle heat; too much heat destroys the "bloom" of the matter</note> nor its vigor. Therefore, such irreparable damage must be prevented in time with gentle manipulations, and everything must be brought into putrefaction. For I have never seen anything animate grow without putrefaction; unless it is putrid, the study and alchemical work will be in vain. For without <term>corruption</term> <note>In the Aristotelian sense: the breaking down of one form</note>, generation cannot occur; for the corruption of one is the generation of another.

Thus it is sufficiently proven that in both the animal and mineral kingdoms, the highest bliss is hidden in the deepest death, and the highest and most blessed elevation is hidden in the deepest humiliation, and grows forth through delay <note>Latin: *per moram*, suggesting the necessity of patience and time in the chemical process</note>. And thus we proceed to the beginning of the 14th Chapter.

## The Fourteenth Chapter.

# Of the Ascension and Descension of the Putrefied Body and its Spiritual Coagulation, which is not yet the perfect and final fixed Coagulation.

This ascension and descension of the Matter is nothing other than an indication of spirituality, and the loss or disappearance of corporeality; this is the true Philosophical <term>Sublimation</term> <note>In alchemy, sublimation is not merely the transition from solid to gas, but a process of purifying a substance by "lifting" its spirit from its dross.</note>,

> [For to sublime properly, according to the true sense of the Philosophers, is nothing other than to volatilize and perfect again, and to exalt matters from perfection to imperfection, and from imperfection to perfection. This is that wonderful <term>transmutation</term> <note>the transformation of one element into another</note> of the Philosophers: of body into Spirit, and of this Spirit into body, about which it is left said to us by the Sages: make the fixed volatile and the volatile fixed, so that you may have our <term>Mastery</term> <note>Latin: *Magisterium*, the successful completion of the Great Work</note>; understand, make from the stubborn body something manageable, which by the excellence of the spirit agreeing with the soul becomes a most constant body for enduring all trials <note>original Latin: "Sublimare enim propriè... sustinendum examina."</note>.]

without which it cannot be certain that the fixed has been made volatile; for after the matter has been putrefied, to make a spiritual body—which however is impossible to happen, except in air, which is

...through sublimation <note>original: "per sublimationem"</note>, which is the conversion of the ash of metals into Spirit, and thus the fixed becomes volatile, etc. This **sublimation** or ascension is nothing other than a separation of spirituality from corporeality, a division of the light from the darkness and gloom, a lovely dawn of the breaking day, during which a lovely dew [appears]. It moistens the dead <term>body</term> <note>In alchemy, the "body" refers to the solid, physical residue of the experiment.</note> subsiding at the bottom—which looks like dust—but as the day begins, it ascends once again. 

When, however, the <term>mercurial</term> <note>original symbol: ☿; referring to the volatile, "living" principle of the matter.</note> matter sublimes itself, then the body remains behind like ash; and this is called by the common name of "sifting and grinding" <note>original Latin: "cribratio & contritio"</note>. This is therefore the **sublimation of the Sages**, and not the common kind. Some have thrown away this unsightly matter that subsides at the bottom; they imagined that because Christ left the world and ascended to heaven in a glorified body, he would not return. 

But He says otherwise: *Where two or three are gathered in my name, there am I in the midst of them, for I am with you until the end of the world.* Therefore, it is necessary and useful for every **Son of Art** <note>original Latin: "Filio artis"; a traditional term for a dedicated student of alchemy.</note> to know: Do not despise the ash at the bottom, for it is the crown of your heart and the permanence of the ash <note>original Latin: "Cinerem in fundo ne vilipendas, nam ipsa est diadema cordis tui & permanentia cinis."</note>.

Thus, he acts unwisely—indeed, quite foolishly and senselessly—who opens the "crystalline chamber" <note>the glass laboratory vessel</note>, takes out the <term>dead earth</term> <note>Latin: "Terram mortuam"; the depleted physical remains of the substance.</note>, and puts it away or aside. He thinks, on the contrary, that he can catch birds in the **water** <note>original symbol: 🜄</note> and fish in the air. Therefore, I admonish everyone, for the sake of their happiness and worldly well-being, not to open the glass, much less touch it or let air in too suddenly...

To ensure that all these poisonous crocodile tears <note>In alchemy, "crocodile tears" often refers to the pungent, acidic drops that condense during distillation</note> are not shed in vain, the wise artist <term>Artist; original: Artista, the practitioner of alchemy</term> intervenes: *Indeed, it is necessary to have a proportion between these things, so that coldness and moisture are tempered with heat and dryness, so that they do not flee from the fire* <note>original: ▽ (symbol for Ignis/Fire)</note>. *Therefore, make it so that the coldness and moisture receive the heat and dryness which were hidden, and let them become one substance.* Thus, one must strive and ensure that the fiercely poisonous spirit <note>original: Spiritus</note> is so impressed upon, coagulated, and melted into the heart of the body, and also through a very lovely and pleasant fire <note>original: ▽ (symbol for Ignis/Fire)</note> is brought to the point that what was previously the greatest poison is transformed into the best, most lovely, and most pleasant Tincture. Thus, in the treatment of the following 15th Chapter, the artist shall hear of the highest medicine of human life, which follows here.

...possible. Much is said about the *particular way* <term>via particular<note>A "particular" process in alchemy refers to a method that transmutes a specific amount of a base metal into a precious one, as opposed to the "Universal" way which creates the Philosopher's Stone capable of infinite transformation.</note></term>, and the world is filled with such countless false processes, opinions, cheap pamphlets <note>original: "Scartecken"</note>, and books, that it seems to me almost possible that if one had them all together in a single pile, one could dam up entire rivers and streams with them. It helps neither to sing nor to say it; the bottom line is: *The world wants to be deceived* <note>original Latin: "Mundus vult decipi"</note>. Let it be so; the majority will yet learn through their own loss. They will labor and seek *particulars* <note>original: "particularia"</note> for so long until money, goods, house, home, and property are all labored away and feigned into these "particulars." Yet they do not stop, even if honor and reputation—yes, even life and limb—should finally all become "particulars." Let everyone open their eyes and look at Nature; *For I advise you all, let no one enter into this art of discovery unless he acknowledges the principle of true nature and its governance; once these are known, he needs no more things than one, nor does it require great expenses. Because our art is not perfected in a multitude of things, for there is one matter and one way of arriving at the complete mastery.* <note>original Latin: "Nam Consulo vobis omnibus, ne quise huic arti intromittat, ad inveniendum, nisi principium veræ naturæ, & ejus regimen agnoscat, quo cognita, pluribus non indiget rebus, nisi una, neque magnas requirit expensas ; Quia in rerum multitudine, ars nostra non perficitur, nam est materia una & via una ad completum magisterium perveniendi."</note> It matters not that one begins many different things, if afterward, when it comes to the end, the very least is accomplished. *Therefore, let no one approach the investigation of this art who is a man filled with a coarse and hard wit, nor one so greedy or stingy in costs and expenses, nor a man of double mind (lacking a firm purpose), lacking spirit or backbone, or of a variable mind, or too hasty or deceitful; but rather a son of Doctrine* <term>filius Doctrinæ<note>A traditional term for a true student or initiate of alchemical secrets.</note></term>*, a man adorned with a most subtle wit, sufficiently wealthy, firm in purpose, and wealthy nevertheless con-* <note>The text cuts off here, continuing to the next page with the catchword "stans" (constant/steady).</note>

M 3

*Steadfast, patient, gentle, long-suffering, and temperate.* <note>original Latin: "stans, patiens, mitis, longaminis & Temperatus." These are the virtues required of a true alchemist.</note>
I advise, therefore, that one avoid and abandon all particular dead-ends <note>original: "Holtzwege," literally wood-paths or logging trails that lead deep into the forest but end nowhere.</note> and sophisticated, deceptive processes that vanish in smoke. Instead, remain on the level, paved, and unerring universal highway of the true Philosophers, which shows the way to the right Philosophical Palace. I assure you, one will pass through enough small villages before the exquisite building—the magnificent Palace of the Wise <term>Palatii Sophorum<note>A metaphorical destination representing the completion of the Great Work and the attainment of ultimate wisdom.</note></term>—becomes visible and one enters into it. If the time feels long, and someone, tired from the journey, wishes to rest a while in a village that pleases him so that he may afterward travel on more conveniently, that is free for anyone to do; yet one cannot over-hurry the pace. *Therefore, do not complete your work too quickly, but make haste slowly; for patience and delay are necessary in our mastery, as haste is indeed from the Devil.* <note>original Latin: "Noli ergo citò perficere opus tuum, sed festina lente, nam patientia & mora sunt necessaria in nostro magisterio, Festinantia quidem ex parte Diaboli est." The phrase "festina lente" (make haste slowly) was a favorite motto of Renaissance scholars.</note> Therefore, do what pleases you, only do not hurry; for haste seldom does good. Nature will have its course. One can certainly make wine from unripe grapes, but it tastes accordingly; and so it is with all plants, and likewise with this work. Indeed, this is the most magnificent medicine <term>Medicina<note>Alchemists often referred to the Philosopher's Stone as the "Universal Medicine" because it was believed to "heal" base metals into gold and cure all human ailments.</note></term> of the human body, of which a dosage of a single drop performs and achieves more than all other Galenic medications <term>Galenische Medicamenta<note>Traditional medicines based on the theories of the ancient Greek physician Galen, which relied on balancing "humors" through herbs and minerals without alchemical purification.</note></term>. It is also the beginning of perfection <term>Vollkommenheit</term>, yet not perfection itself; just as leaven is the cause of the bread's souring but is not the bread itself, and as a fruit-bearing seed is the cause of the fruit but is not the fruit itself, so it is with this.

<summary>This page advocates for the "Universal" path of alchemy over deceptive "particular" methods, emphasizing that the Great Work requires extreme patience and follows the slow laws of Nature. It describes the resulting alchemical Medicine as a single, powerful drop that far surpasses traditional Galenic pharmacy.</summary>

# The Sixteenth Chapter.

## Concerning the emergence of the 4 elements with their colors, from the white incombustible oil of the Philosophers, or the so-called Peacock’s Tail. <term>cauda Pavonis<note>A famous stage in the alchemical process characterized by a sudden eruption of many iridescent colors, signaling that the impurities are being consumed and the matter is transitioning toward the final white or red stage.</note></term>

When everything has been brought to this point, one will then see that this above-described white incombustible oil <term>$\circ\circ$ incombustibili albo<note>The symbol $\circ\circ$ is an alchemical shorthand for *Oleum* or oil. Incombustible oil refers to a substance that has been purified so thoroughly that it can no longer be destroyed by fire.</note></term>, Potable Silver <note>original Latin: *Argentū Potabile*</note>, Liquid Tincture <note>original Latin: *Tinctura humida*</note>, or the Philosophers' particular oil, will transmute or change itself into such a clear, transparent material that even the best purified mercury <gloss>quicksilver</gloss> cannot compare to it. It is so clear, in fact, that one will find even the smallest little hairs and veins of a human face most beautifully reflected and represented within it, right to the very bottom <note>original Latin: *ad imum*</note>.

In such pleasant and lovely clarity, it remains for 6 days. Then, it begins to churn and surge within itself, just as silver "drives" <note>original: "treibet." This refers to the shimmering movement of molten metal during cupellation, the process of refining precious metals in a shallow dish called a cupel or "test."</note> when it is being refined on the cupel. This movement, once it has settled somewhat, causes the most beautiful, pleasant, and lovely colors and hues <note>original: "Colores und Farben"</note> to all rise up together. When these colors have completed their "regiment" <note>The "regimen" or "regiment" refers to a specific period in the work where a particular planetary or elemental influence dominates the flask.</note> or rule, they all disappear and leave the "Body" <note>The solid matter at the bottom of the vessel.</note> lying behind pale white, like a coagulated mercury <note>original Latin: *☿io coagulato*</note>. It lies in this state for 14 days, during which time the craftsman <note>original Latin: *Artifex*</note> might think that the work is now lost, because in that same time...

[Even if for a] time nothing shows, yet do not despair, but only keep the heat going at all times; on the 14th or 15th day it begins to rise: The lead-color of Saturn <note>original: ♄</note>. Which now takes total leave of the **Body** <term>Corpus<note>The solid matter within the alchemical vessel, often called the "King" or the "Earth."</note></term>; then follows a black-brown [color], which also still belongs and is related to Saturn; these colors last for a good while, and although they are truly a pleasure to behold, they make the time feel long until they take their final leave. And even if the subtle, volatile corporeality passes away, the spiritual Saturnine Soul-Spirit is so deeply impressed upon the **Jupiter** <note>original symbol: ♃</note> that it does not leave until things reach the very limit. After these two colors, a deep red emerges, like **minium** <note>A bright orange-red pigment, also known as red lead.</note>, and this belonged to Jupiter; because he is pressed and forced to follow on the heels of Saturn, he leaves the **Jovian substance** <note>original: ♃ium</note> entirely, and gives his total farewell, as seen by his departure.

Then **Mars** <note>original symbol: ♂</note> also soon approaches with his followers, quite magnificently, yet with deception; for a deep red vapor rises from the Body, which becomes more and more citron-colored the longer it is seen. There the artist thinks he has already achieved his goal, but he has still missed the mark. Mars <note>original: ♂</note> gives sweet words, seemingly yielding, but he has a rogue in his heart; what he thinks, he does not say until one feels the harm in the actual deed and work itself. So also here, many artists and **Sons of the Doctrine** <term>Filii Doctrinæ<note>A traditional name for initiates or students of the "hermetic art" of alchemy.</note></term> have allowed themselves to be deceived by these colors; either when the Body began to turn white-yellow, they broke it open, took out the **material** <term>Materiam<note>The substance undergoing transformation.</note></term>, and wanted to begin **tincturing** <term>tingiren<note>The final process of using the Philosopher's Stone to transform base metals into gold or silver.</note></term>; but some wanted to be even cleverer...

In alchemy, the Sun or "Sol" signifies both the metal gold and the active, heating, masculine principle of the work.</note></term> comes / and moistens the cold body / these heat and cold / which are to be united there with one another / subsequently cause at first a mixed-colored smoke! Saturn corresponds to lead and the "nigredo" or black stage of decay and melancholy.</note></term>, which lets its black body go / and unites its soul for a time / with the innermost part of **Mercury** <term>♿<note>Represented by the name Mercurii. The "inner Mercury" is the fluid, mediating spirit within the vessel.</note></term>, then the **Sun** <term>☉</term> also begins to attack **Jupiter** <term>♃<note>Jupiter corresponds to the metal tin; here it is depicted as fleeing the intense heat of the "philosophical sun."</note></term> / who leaves everything behind / and flees with body and soul / for his goodness is an enemy to all heated violence / therefore he does not mix / but rather flees in a red smoke or vapor. <unclear>Afterward</unclear> the **Sun** <term>☉</term> also comes to **Mars** <term>♂<note>Mars corresponds to iron and the "rubedo" or reddening stage of the work.</note></term> and wants to either make him spiritual / or drive him away / but because his innermost heart is a purely clear being capable of fire / it ignites within his body / and burns away his coarse physicality / and chases it away in a blood-red smoke-vapor / but meanwhile his innermost part unites with the **Sun** <term>☉</term> or Sun of the Philosophers / until it attains from the same a better perfection / and the remaining physicality completely departs / meanwhile, however, the **Sun** <term>☉</term> or **Sol** <note>original: "Sol"</note> intensifies its heat / and attacks **Luna** <term>☾<note>Represented by the name Lunam. Luna corresponds to silver and the "albedo" or white stage of the work.</note></term> / but since **Luna** is accustomed / to receiving heat from the **Sun** / she accepts it good-naturedly and willingly / and gradually ignites / as the moon increases / for he awakens the ignition / yellow and blue-white colors / until finally the **Full Moon** <note>The "Full Moon" signifies the peak of the lunar/white stage (albedo), where the matter reaches a brilliant, reflective purity.</note> begins to shine.

## The Seventeenth Chapter.

# Concerning the highest Whiteness, the Philosophical Crowned Swan, the Full Moon, the Queen, and the fixed Silver Tincture; how projection must be performed with it, and how it must be augmented in quantity and quality, and carried into infinity.

The full moon, which is complete in its brilliance and is therefore a "whole moon," is not without reason used as an allegory for the philosophical <margin><image-desc>Square alchemical symbol containing an X</image-desc></margin> <note>This symbol, a square with an 'X' or cross inside, often represents the "Salt of the Earth" or the fixed physical matter that has reached the stage of purification.</note> in the following way. Just as the moon, when it is full, likens itself to a heavenly whiteness, so too the Philosophical Tincture—having been brought so far—is not unfairly compared to the moon due to its supreme whiteness. For just as the moon begins to increase in its light before it becomes full, so too must the Tincture first increase in strength before it is full of power; for what Nature has left undone in this matter, Art <term>Art<note>In alchemical texts, "Art" (Ars) refers to the labor and skill of the alchemist which assists and perfects the natural processes of the laboratory.</note></term> must bring to completion. The moon, when it is full, is recognized by its light when that light is seen completely upon it; so also the Tincture of the Moon <note>original: *Tinctura Lunae*. This refers to the "White Work," the stage of alchemy that produces silver.</note>, the same must also be recognized in too much body— <note>The text ends mid-word; the catchword "ris" indicates the full Latin phrase is "nimio corporis," likely referring to the "excess of the body" or the physical substance being transformed.</note>

# Explanatory Appendix.

It is not at all more to be wondered at, than if Art dares to accomplish in the space of a single hour what the long series of years has completed, having begun from the primal seed, and to overcome Nature in its own course. Art follows the same path through the great bowels of the earth where Nature held the matter for so long, preserving the very same traces from the lowest depths, through all the windings and through dark caves.
->While Gold is produced by Art<-
But yet,
here Art should work more closely, having imitated Nature.

Even if it has already performed its *operations* <note>original: "operationes"</note>. *For Alchemy is a natural action, which has its own motion and determined time, so that some things are finished in a greater, and some in a lesser space of time.* <note>original: "Alchymia enim est actio naturalis, quæ suum habet motum & tempus determinatum, ita ut quædam majori, & quædam minori temporis spacio terminentur."</note> For after the <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: 🜔, often representing the Earth or the mineral antimony as a starting material</note> in forty [parts/days of] <term>Mercury</term> <note>original symbol: ☿</note> is finished, the *work to the white* <note>original: "opus ad album"; in alchemy, the "Albedo" or whitening stage that follows the "Nigredo" or blackening</note> is completed. When this has been brought so far that one hears the beloved Shulamite of Solomon <note>A reference to the Song of Solomon, often interpreted alchemically as the union of chemical principles</note> begin and sing the following song of honor:

> *I am my beloved's and his desire is toward me,* <note>original: "ego dilecti mei sum & erga me est desiderium ejus"</note>

one may then boldly ask and say:

> *Who is she that looks forth like the dawn, fair as the moon, pure as the* <term>Sun</term> <note>original symbol: ☉, representing the Sun or Gold</note>, *your breasts are like clusters of the vine, and your palate like the best wine, going straight to its beloved, making the lips of the ancients speak.* <note>original: "quæ est ista, quæ videtur sicut aurora, Pulchra ut Luna, Munda ut ☉, ubera tua sunt sicut racemi vitis, & palatum tuum veluti vinum optimum, vadens ad dilectum suum directè, faciens loqui labia veterum."</note>

Then one will certainly receive as an answer:

->Here is produced the rich Daughter of the Sages,<-
->Who shall always rule alike,<-
->The Sages' silver-white kingdom of honor.<-

How one is to *proceed* <note>original: "procediren"</note> with her so that one can enjoy her, is not unreasonable to ask?

...the cleanest substance in a new glass mortar, which is smooth and well-polished on the inside, and whose pestle is prepared in the same manner as the mortar—smoothly—to an inconceivably fine powder. From this <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁</note> weigh out 1 part; of <term>Silver</term> <note>original symbol: ☽</note>, cut and beaten very small and thin, 100 parts; let these flow in a well-luted <note>sealed with clay</note> crucible by <term>degrees of fire</term> <note>original symbol: ∆</note> for 4 hours. Then take it out, and you will have a brittle matter which is pure Medicine. From this <term>prepared substance</term> <note>original symbol: ✡, likely referring to the "Starry Regulus" or the fermented compound</note> take again one part and throw it upon 100 parts of lead or tin, or upon 150 parts of crude <term>Antimony</term> <note>original symbol: ♁</note> (which it tints even more readily), and it will become fine <term>Silver</term> <note>original symbol: ☽</note>. The precautions <note>original: "cautelæ"</note> to be observed in this are: 1. That one should not let oneself be noticed much with <term>Mercury</term> <note>original symbol: ☿</note> or such things. 2. That the <term>Silver</term> <note>original symbol: ☽</note> used for fermentation must be the best cupelled silver without any additive. 3. That one takes good care of the <term>degrees of fire</term> <note>original symbol: ∆</note> and the time, so that it does not remain in the <term>fire</term> <note>original symbol: ∆</note> past the allotted time and thus penetrate through the crucible. 4. That everything is weighed against each other correctly and without error. 5. That one does not simply place the tincture onto the molten metal, but wraps it in clean wax. So that I may also teach the reader how to perform an honest projection <note>the final stage of alchemy where the Stone is added to base metal to transform it</note> without deception, know that it must be arranged as follows: take a good strong crucible and place it in an open blast fire; therein let the metal which you wish to transmute flow well; pour it out into a <term>refining dish</term> <note>original: "Treibscherben," a shallow vessel used for cupellation</note> which has been well-warmed and is now glowing; let them drive together for a quarter of an hour; then throw upon the <term>mark</term> <note>a traditional weight unit for precious metals</note> 2 <term>Loth</term> <note>approx. 1 ounce total</note> or 1 <term>ounce</term> <note>original symbol: ℥i</note> of wax—the newer the better, virgin wax being the best—

...let them be driven together <note>a metallurgical process of heating substances together to refine or combine them</note> until the wax is entirely burned off; then it is pure enough. Then pour it again into a preheated, glowing crucible and let it flow once more. Then have your **Tincture** <note>original: *Tincturam*; the concentrated alchemical catalyst</note> at hand; throw it, well-wrapped, onto the flowing medium so that it congeals. Then give it stronger **fire** <note>original symbol: △</note> so that it may be brought into flux again. When it has flowed well, pour it out into a casting. Place the **silver** <note>original symbol: ☾</note> with lead onto the cupels <note>shallow, porous ceramic bowls used to separate precious metals from base metals like lead</note> and refine it in the **proper manner** <note>original: *debito modo*</note>. This is done so that if the **capable Tincture** <note>original: *Tinctura capax*</note> were able to tint even more, it would not be hindered, but rather furthered all the better by this means. Gold is poured **for the third time** <note>original: *ad tertium*</note> through **antimony** <note>original symbol: ♁</note>; one also adds a **Regulus of antimony** <note>a metallic button of purified antimony</note> to it in the flux, and afterward lets the same smoke away again before the bellows. There are still several **methods** <note>original: *modos*</note>, but the brevity of time will not permit any lengthy writing.

However, regarding how the **augmentation in quality or universal virtue and power** <note>original: *Augmentatio in qualitate seu virtute & potentia universali*</note> should occur, the following little saying teaches: "The more the stone is moistened with its own water, the more it is increased in quantity, weight, quality, and power of tinting; for the multiplication of the Tincture is the augmentation of the color and natural virtue." <note>original Latin: *Quanto magis cum sua aqua irroratur lapis, tanto magis augmentatur in quantitate, pondere, qualitate & virtute tingendi, nam multiplicatio Tincturæ, est coloris & virtutis naturalis augmentatio.*</note>

But how and in what specific way this should and must happen has been intentionally saved here until the conclusion or **Perfection** of the Work, where the **General Augmentation** is handled, because it is **one and the same** <note>original: *unum idem*</note>. It is enough that the Work has been described and taught here from the beginning to the end, including its preparation.

## The Eighteenth Chapter.
# On the Further Procedure
# for the Universal Tincture, and on
the second **solution** <note>the process of dissolving a solid substance into liquid form</note> into the **Philosophical Red Oil** <note>original: *$\circ\circ$ rubrum Philosophicum*; a highly refined alchemical liquid, often associated with the final stages of the work</note> of truly **Potable Gold** <note>original: *Auro potabili*; gold processed into a drinkable medicinal form, believed to have miraculous healing properties</note>, concerning its **powers** <note>original: *viribus*</note>, **dosage** <note>original: *Dosi*</note>, and **carrier** <note>original: *vehiculo*; the liquid medium, such as wine or spirits, used to deliver the medicine</note>, concerning the **wet path** <note>an alchemical method involving liquids and gentle heat over a long period, contrasted with the "dry path" of high-heat crucibles</note> to the **red** <note>the *Rubedo*, or the final successful stage of the alchemical process</note>, concerning the **colors** <note>original: *Coloribus*; the sequence of color changes indicating progress in the alchemical vessel</note> before **coagulation** <note>the process where the liquid substance thickens into a solid</note>, concerning the coagulation, and concerning the beginning of the **Universal** <note>the Universal Medicine or Philosopher's Stone</note>, as well as the outcome of various **particulars** <note>alchemical transmutations that only affect specific metals, rather than the "Universal" transformation of all things</note>.

He who exerts himself to travel the **universal roads** <note>the primary, most difficult, and most rewarding paths of alchemy</note> must be diligent in that pursuit, and see how he [transforms] all **particular**...

...and avoid wrong paths and departures. But if he wishes to wander the **Universal Path**, then he must with patience and hope await the desired goal. Woe, therefore, to you, sons of doctrine <note>original Latin: *Doctrinæ filii*</note>, who hope to gather fruits before they are ripe, and hope to harvest before the crop. <note>original Latin: *He ergo vobis Doctrinæ filii, qui speratis fructus colligere, antequam maturi sunt, & speratis meter ante messem.*</note> It is not, however, accomplished solely by delaying the gathering of fruit <note>original Latin: *collectionem fructus*</note> until the right time; no, but one must also know how to proceed according to Nature. For Art, the emulator of nature <note>original Latin: *Ars naturæ æmula*</note>, experiences the same in its completions and compositions. 

How one should further proceed and act according to Nature, hear this: When you have attained the highest manner, when you see the **Royal Princess of the Sages**—now born before your eyes in her highest innocence, lying there completely naked—then take care, if you wish to bring forth something fruitful and reach the **Universal Goal**, that you indeed do not open the crystalline vessel <note>original: *Stupham Crystallinam*; likely referring to the glass maturation chamber</note> or the **Hermetic Egg** <note>a sealed glass vessel used for long-term heating</note>. Instead, let it lie in its center, just as it lay before, and give it still some <term>Fire</term> <note>symbol: $\Delta$</note>. 

Then you will see that the **fixed Silver-Tincture** will behave in a wondrous manner. For first, it will rise in a white mist, which will hang in bluish droplets that incline toward redness, and gradually drip down again. At this point, take care that you do not hurry or give a stronger <term>Fire</term> <note>symbol: $\Delta$</note>. 

But when you are in the work, let assiduousness and patience be present. Let laziness, negligence, and diabolical haste be absent, for its end is sadness and despair; therefore you will go most safely by the middle path. <note>original Latin: *sed cum in opere fueris, adsit assiduita & patientia. Absit pigritia, negligentia & festinatio Diabolica, & ejus finis tristitia & desperatio media ergò via tutissimus ibis.*</note> What is more, pay attention to the operations that occur, for through these you recognize the entire **Signature of Na-**

### Explanatory Appendix.

...nature, and especially the birth of man. *For the generation of the Philosophic Stone* <note>original: Lapis Sophici</note> *is likened to the generation of a human being; because when the Water* <note>$\nabla$ represents the element of water</note> *has brought forth the infant, it nourishes it until it becomes stronger and able to resist the flame of the Fire* <note>$\Delta$ represents the element of fire</note> *; and the more it is nourished and grows, the greater the transmutation you will see, and you will recognize within yourself the visible, admirable powers of nature. For its operations are manifested as its power increases.*

Therefore, pay close attention now to all the *operations* <note>The specific steps of the alchemical process</note> of the work, for now is the right time. You will see that the red-blue and blue-red drops will *dissolve* the *body*—which was previously entirely white—into a completely yellow color. And when this *solution* has *matured* for 14 *days* <note>$\delta$ is used here as a symbol for days</note>, it becomes the *perfect potable gold* <note>original: aurum potabile; a legendary liquid gold medicine believed to grant longevity</note>. This is an excellent *medicine*, whose *powers against all ailments* <note>original: vires contra omnes affectus</note> cannot be described. In short, a single little drop is life for a human being, but death for illnesses, by whatever names they may be called.

The *vehicle* <note>The liquid used to deliver the medicine</note> is a good wine from this year's harvest, which should be slightly warmed. One may eat whatever one desires, provided that one has fasted before and after the use of the medicine. This is what **Paracelsus** <note>A famous Swiss physician and alchemist (1493–1541) known for using minerals in medicine</note> means when he says he *cured his patients between cups and while drinking* <note>original: inter pocula & bibendum curiret</note>. No special *diet* is necessary, because the *constitution* <note>original: Complexion; the balance of bodily humors</note> of the entire human body is, in a single *moment*, taken hold of, cleansed or *purged*, established, and *improved*. It is even *tinted* <note>original: tingiret; in alchemy, "tinging" refers to the permanent color change that signifies the transformation of a base metal into gold</note> from an imperfect state into a more perfect one, just like the imperfect metals. If someone does not drink wine, let them use in its place the *water of all flowers* <note>distilled floral essence</note>. This is the *red oil of the Philosophers* <note>original: $\therefore$ rubeum Philosophorum</note> incom-

### Explanatory Appendix.

[incom]bustible, the **Universal Tincture** and **Azoth of Paracelsus** <note>Azoth was considered the "universal remedy" or "mercury of the philosophers" in alchemical traditions</note>, the "wet path" of medicine; for it is *dyeing from* <term>incombustible oil</term> <note>symbol: 🜖; a substance that resists fire, often associated with the fixed sulfur of the philosophers</note>, the *first* <term>Mercury of the Sages</term> <note>symbol: ☿; not common quicksilver, but the purified "living" essence of metal</note>, *the first Matter of Metals extracted from minerals, and left to the judgment of the craftsman until its perfect completion*. Therefore, although *Projection* <note>The process of casting the alchemical "stone" onto base metals to transform them into gold or silver</note> can be performed with this—just as seen with the *white* <term>oil</term> <note>symbol: 🜖</note>—it is not useful, much less advisable, that one should mutilate the Universal [medicine] now. But he who cannot rest may proceed as taught; however, he who has patience, which is highly necessary, let him give **GOD** the most bounden thanks for the aforementioned Universal Medicine.

*Now therefore let the sublime God of natures be praised, blessed, and glorious, who has revealed to us the sequence of all medicines, along with the experience of it, which by the goodness of His prompting and the persistence of our labor we have sought out, seen with our eyes, and touched with our hands—the completion of it discovered by our mastery. And with Solomon: The Most High created medicine from the Earth, and a prudent man will not abhor it. Therefore let the Lord our God be praised forever.* <note>The author quotes a variation of Sirach 38:4, traditionally attributed to Solomon in alchemical contexts.</note>

### Explanatory Appendix.

...would be a lamentable loss; **therefore, it must be especially guarded against** <note>original: maximè ergò cavendum</note>, lest the spirit, which dries the body, is itself dried away from the body and becomes volatile; for if it flies away from the body, the body cannot be made perfect. How this is to be prevented, however, is: let the <term>fire</term> <note>symbol: Δ; ignition or heat</note> be slow, and it is also required that the vessel be sealed with the **seal of Hermes** <note>sigillum hermetis; an airtight, "hermetic" seal often made by melting the glass neck of a flask</note>. One must carefully observe these similar regimens of the <term>Magistery</term> <note>the "Masterwork" or the professional practice of alchemy</note>, for every light thing [moves] upward, but the heavy [moves] downward. After this ascent and descent has occurred, you will see various colors once again contending with one another, rising and falling, which were already described in the previous treatise and are omitted here for the sake of beloved brevity. Yet we recall the words of **Isaiah**:

> The heavens shall melt away like smoke, and the earth shall be worn out like a garment, Chapter 51. <note>original: Coeli sicut fumus liquescent, & Terra sicut vestimentum atteretur</note>

**Peter** says:

> The heavens and earth which now exist are preserved for <term>fire</term> <note>symbol: Δ</note> until the day of judgment, in which the heavens shall pass away with a great rush, but the elements shall be dissolved by heat, and the <term>earth/waters</term> <note>symbol: ∇; though the symbol usually represents water, in this biblical context it refers to the terrestrial world</note> with all the works that are in them shall be burned up, 2 Peter Chapter 3. <note>original: Qui nunc sunt coeli & terra servantur...</note>

It is therefore well to be noted that this is a trial and testing by <term>fire</term> <note>symbol: Δ</note>, of which **David** reports:

> The words of the Lord are pure words, silver examined by <term>fire</term> <note>symbol: Δ</note>, proven for the earth, purified sevenfold; <note>original: Eloquia Domini, Eloquia munda, argentum Δ e examinatum...</note> or according to others: The words of the Lord are pure words, silver examined by <term>fire</term> <note>symbol: Δ</note>, proven thrice, purified sevenfold; or thus: The words of the Lord are pure words, silver examined by <term>fire</term> <note>symbol: Δ</note> in an earthen vessel, purified seven times; or more correctly: The words of God are pure words, silver burnt in an <term>aludel</term> <note>a pear-shaped earthenware bottle used for sublimation</note> into earth or powder, recast seven times.

### Explanatory Appendix.

In the **Universal Work** <note>original: Universal-Werck; the final stage of alchemy aimed at the Philosopher's Stone</note>, one recognizes the **perfect tincture** <note>original: Tincturam perfect</note> by its **shimmer** <note>original: Blicker; a metallurgical term for the sudden flash of light seen when a metal reaches its highest purity</note>; thus, the **ultimate light** <note>original: ultima lux</note>, the **flash** <note>original: fulminatio</note>, the shimmer, or the reflection may well be called a primary cause and the **perfect mastery** <note>original: Magisterium perfectum</note> of the Tinctures. Its primary cause is the **radical moisture** <note>original: humidum radicale; the essential life-fluid or "oil" within matter</note>; this has coagulated shortly before and became **fixed** <note>original: figiret; rendered stable and resistant to fire</note> in the **coagulation** <note>the process of thickening or solidifying</note>, and has turned itself into the **more-than-perfect tincture** <note>original: Tincturam plusquam perfect</note>. Now, it shows its perfection to the artist through an external **sign** <note>original: Signum</note>, so that he may know how to proceed hereafter, and also so that he may know the beginning and the end of everything, but foremost that of the Universal.

**Before all things, however, it is necessary for the one who wishes to be introduced into this art to know and have knowledge of the natures of both the primary and natural metals, and to recognize their generations, weaknesses, and imperfections in their ores, before reaching this art; for he who is ignorant of the principles never reaches the end, for nature rejoices in nature, and like is delighted by like.** <note>original Latin: "Præprimis omnibus autem oportet illam... natura gaudet natura, æqualis æquali delectatur."</note>

But so that we may examine the general cause of the Tincture, know that it is the **universal solvent** <note>original: Menstruum universale; a substance capable of dissolving all matter to its first principle</note>. The **particular cause** <note>the specific agent for this individual work</note>, however, is the **incombustible red of the Philosophers** <note>original: rubeum incombustibile Philosophorum; represented here by the symbol for Gold or the Sun: <image-desc>alchemical symbol consisting of a circle with a central dot, possibly representing Gold or the Sun, with decorative horizontal strokes above it</image-desc></note>. This penetrates like a fertile rain into a dry land; this penetration successively awakens a **fixed coagulation**, which can be dissolved by nothing other than the **watering** <note>original: irrorationem</note> of its own universal solvent; and the true masters of the art call this **imbibition** <note>original: imbibitionem; the process of "feeding" the stone by adding liquid until it is saturated</note>, which will be dealt with further below. Upon this fixed coagulation, and

### Explanatory Appendix.

Whose flashes are now followed by the highest stability <note>original: fixitåt; the state where a substance is no longer volatile and can withstand the heat of fire</note> and its apparent scarlet-redness, which then may well be named—as it also truly is in the work itself—the <term>universal Sulfur</term> <note>Symbol: 🜍; the active, masculine principle of the Stone</note> of the Philosophers that tinges and transmutes, the **blessed seed of gold**, the **fixed universal tincture**, the **red bloodthirsty dragon** which eats and devours all other animals, and—because of the exceedingly hot <term>fire</term> <note>Symbol: 🜂</note> with which its innermost heart is infected and ignited—transforms them into itself and its own kind.

This is the **Philosophical King**, whom his most beloved bride subsequently praises and rewards: *“My beloved is white and ruddy, outstanding among ten thousand; his head is like the finest gold, his locks are curly and black as a raven, his eyes are like those of doves.”* <note>original: "Dilectus meus candidus & rubicundus..." This is a quote from the Song of Solomon 5:10-12, used by alchemists to describe the perfect Red Stone.</note> This King possesses the philosophical realm, glorious, mighty, and magnificent; it shines like the sun among the stars—thus it shines in the philosophical heaven. And just as the sun in the firmament has no equal in its brilliance, size, and qualities or properties, so too does the penetrating **Philosophical Ray-Sun** have no equal in its qualities, **virtues** <note>original: virtutibus; powers or healing properties</note>, **magnitude** <note>original: magnitudine</note>, and properties. It is the only light beside its sister **Luna** <note>the Moon, representing the White Tincture or Silver</note>, which gives light to the philosophical day and illuminates everything with its rays, also making all its brothers in philosophical metals rich and glorious, and setting them powerfully high. 

This Sun must often first draw water to itself so that it may attain the highest quality of being fruitful: *The soaking is multiplied six times, and the process of the mastery three and four times.* <note>original: "Inhibitio [Imbibitio] multiplicatur sexies & terque quater Magisterii processus"; referring to the "feeding" of the Stone with liquid to increase its power of projection.</note>

...is completed, reiterate the said operation; for the more you repeat it, the more it is **multiplied in power** <note>original: multiplicatur in potentia; the process of increasing the strength of the alchemical Stone</note>. For example, if at first one weight of the medicine converted a hundred parts, the second time it will convert a thousand, the third time ten thousand, and so on to infinity. Yet know that you must feed the **Philosophical Sun** with its equal, **fire** <term>$\Delta$</term> <note>the symbol for fire; here likely referring to the secret alchemical heat or the "sulfur" of the metal</note>, so that it may appear all the more powerful and overflowing; for the more it is nourished and fermented, the more it grows. You will see a greater transmutation, and in these wonderful powers of nature, you will recognize yourself as visible within it. 

The **fire**, however, that must be added to it is the **metallic sun** <note>Gold</note>; it devours it totally and transforms it into itself. It also becomes much stronger and bloodier in its physical body. However, one must also place this sun into its heaven, so that one may expect day and light from it; this happens as follows: cast one part upon 1,000 parts, let it flow like water, pour it out, and test what you have. The **preparation of this Sun** <note>Sonnen Bereitung</note>, however, occurs briefly in the following manner:

Or in the following way: enclose your matter in the **philosophical egg** <note>the sealed glass vessel used for the final stages of the Work</note>, seal it hermetically, but beware that you place neither too much nor too small a quantity into our vessel. For if the vessel were too large, our **physical Mercury** <term>$\Mercury$</term> <note>the essential spirit of the matter</note> would surely vanish into nothingness. If, however, the glasses were too small, our **flowers** <note>the chemical vapors or colors that appear during heating</note> would be so suffocated that they could never lead their fruits to perfection. ...of your matrix <note>The alchemical vessel or "womb" where the transformation occurs</note>, let three parts of your vessel be empty, and no more; and into one glass, do not put more than 1 ounce <term>℥j</term> <note>apothecary weight</note> or 3 ounces <term>℥iij</term> <note>common weight</note> at most. This Sun, after its preparation, is clothed with **Ambrosia** <note>In alchemy, a name for the celestial or divine nourishment/solvent</note> and given to its 6 sisters to eat <note>The "6 sisters" usually represent the other six metals: Moon/Silver, Mars/Iron, Venus/Copper, Jupiter/Tin, Saturn/Lead, and Mercury/Quicksilver</note>. The entire time of the birth is 9 times 6, multiplied by 4 <note>This equals 216, likely referring to the days required for this stage</note>. Thus let enough be said in the name of the most Holy Trinity. He who has ears to hear, let him hear. *Not everyone is granted to go to Corinth* <note>original: Non omnibus datur adire Corinthum; a classical proverb meaning that not everyone can achieve difficult or elite goals, here applied to the difficulty of the Great Work</note>. # Preface Disposition

We wish to write a little something concerning <term>Chemistry<note>original: "Chymia"; in this period, the term encompassed both practical laboratory work and spiritual alchemy.</note></term> / not out of our own scholarship / but rather out of benevolence and goodwill / for the treasure of the <term>fifth understanding<note>likely a reference to the "quintessence" or a spiritual level of comprehension beyond the four earthly elements.</note></term> does not lie buried with us / yet what has been bestowed upon us / we wish to share with others from a true and uncorrupted heart ; We do not possess the tongues of orators / and cannot produce magnificent words / for us it is not said: <note>original: "Vox es præterea nihil"; Latin for "You are a voice and nothing more," a phrase used to describe empty eloquence without substance.</note> *You are but a voice and nothing more* / as it is with those who dress their speeches according to the fashion / and write them down / nor do we have <note>original: "Englische Zungen"; in this context, the German "Englisch" refers to "angelic" (from "Engel"), referencing the biblical "tongues of men and of angels."</note> angelic tongues / but must speak with human tongues / therefore we ask the reader / that he would accept this benevolence in place of scholarship / and these works in place of magnificent words ; For although we have not applied ourselves to lofty speech and intellect / we have nonetheless applied ourselves to writing of the most noble work's beginning, middle, and end / for our purpose is [to write] of <term>Chemistry</term><gloss>The 'i' has a diacritic mark, likely indicating an abbreviation for Chymia or Chymie</gloss> , its origin / name / form / and final causes—

<summary>The author opens the preface with a humble disclaimer, stating that his work is based on practical experience and goodwill rather than academic oratory. He frames the text as a guide to the origins and purposes of chemistry for the benefit of others.</summary>

# Preface

...write / we wish to defend and support its truth / not out of our own selves / though we could well do so / but rather to speak of others / and so that they may hold precedence over us / we shall remain in the background. We also wish to describe a <unclear>proper</unclear> <term>laborant<note>original: "laboranten"; a practical laboratory worker, as opposed to a purely theoretical philosopher.</note></term> or <term>chemist<note>original: "Chymicum"; at this time, chemistry and alchemy were not yet distinct disciplines.</note></term> / how he ought to be minded and disposed / toward **God** in heaven / toward his neighbor / and toward all poor and needy people / toward himself / toward his money and property / toward his labor and the like ; We also wish to introduce those who lack understanding into all chemical labors / and provide them with a living model of all <term>operations<note>original: "operationen"; the specific laboratory procedures, such as distillation or sublimation, used to transform matter.</note></term>. When we have done all that is possible for us / and all that lies within our power / we hope / the reader will—after we commend him to Divine protection—remain favorable and well-disposed toward us.

->*The Red Lion of the Golden Cross.*<-
<note>original: "Aureæ Crucis Leo Ruber." This is the author's pseudonym; the "Red Lion" is a famous alchemical symbol for the "Philosopher's Stone" at a specific stage of its perfection.</note>

<summary>In this concluding section of the preface, the author outlines the moral and ethical qualities required of a true chemist and expresses his desire to guide beginners through practical laboratory operations. He signs off using his alchemical pseudonym, the Red Lion of the Golden Cross.</summary>

<meta>This page marks the beginning of the first book of the treatise. Alchemical Information,
<meta>Catchword: 15. The</meta>

# On Chemistry.
## BOOK I.
<note>original: "LIB. I."; an abbreviation for the Latin *Liber Primus*.</note>

1. It is an excellent art.
2. It takes its primary origin from **God**.
3. Its second origin comes from miners and the art of cooking. <note>The author links the high art of alchemy to the practical, "earthy" labor of extracting ore and the domestic science of the kitchen.</note>
4. It has stood firm from the beginning of the world until now.
5. It is defended and proven with examples.
6. If it had not been proven, it would have become a fool’s errand and old wives' gossip.
7. Many mock it.
8. It teaches how to prepare magnificent medicines. <note>original: "Medicamenta"; Latin for healing substances or remedies.</note>
9. It transforms all metals into gold and silver. <note>The original uses the planetary symbols ☉ for the Sun (Gold) and ☽ for the Moon (Silver).</note>
10. It has a bound, translucent, and dissolved shape and form. <note>This likely refers to the three states of matter or the alchemical processes of coagulation and solution.</note>
11. It is a natural art and not sorcery, as the slanderers say.
12. It is, rather, a glorious gift of **God**.
13. A nurse of health, and an enemy and banisher of diseases.
14. It is related to many magnificent arts; Theology, Astronomy, and Medicine are its neighbors and kinswomen. <note>In the 17th century, these fields were seen as interconnected parts of a single understanding of the universe.</note>

<summary>This page introduces the first book of the treatise, defining chemistry as a divine and ancient art rooted in practical labor. The author defends its legitimacy against skeptics, highlighting its role in medicine, metallurgy, and its kinship with theology and astronomy.</summary>
<keywords>origin of alchemy, transmutation, gold and silver, medicinal chemistry, natural art vs sorcery, sister sciences</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the numbered list of rules from the previous page, concluding the first book and beginning the second. The text shifts from defining the nature of the "Art" (alchemy) to describing the character and behavior required of its practitioners.</meta>

# Rules.

15. Philosophical Physics <note>original: "Philosophische Physica"; at this time, "physics" referred broadly to the study of the natural world and the laws of matter.</note> is its ruler.

16. Because of its excellence and the utility arising from it, everyone should love it and pursue it.

17. Yet they should know how to make a distinction between a true Philosopher and a Sophist. <note>In alchemical traditions, a "Sophist" (or "puffer") was a charlatan who lacked true spiritual and natural understanding, often seeking only material wealth through false methods.</note>

18. A true Philosopher, who writes from his own experience, will agree with all true Philosophers.

19. A Sophist, on the other hand, writes disparagingly of them and fills the whole world with worthless pamphlets. <note>original: "Scartecken"; a pejorative term for trashy or poorly produced books and booklets.</note>

20. True Philosophers are therefore recognized by their Harmony or Philosophical Agreement. <note>The author refers to the *Consensus Philosophorum*, the belief that all genuine masters of alchemy across history are in fundamental agreement with one another.</note>

21. Everyone should read diligently and record what is noteworthy, so that the agreement may flow all the better from those notes. <note>original: "Notatis"; Latin for things noted or written down.</note>

22. But so that Theory <note>original: "Theoria".</note> may now be brought into Practice <note>original: "Praxin".</note> through labor, <note>original: "labores".</note> it is necessary to act as a laboratory worker. <note>original: "Laboranten"; one who works "at the coals" or performs the physical experiments in a laboratory.</note>

# Alchemical Information-

...with all your heart and all your strength, for that is the highest wisdom of your soul.

2. You shall surrender yourself entirely to **God** your **Lord** as His own, and do nothing without His will; you must also submit all your actions and omissions <note>The phrase "Thun und Lassen" is a traditional German legal and ethical idiom referring to the totality of one's conduct—both what one does and what one chooses not to do.</note> entirely and without any resistance to Him.

3. You should also thoroughly explore and seek out the will of **God** through diligent reading of His Word.

4. You should diligently pray to **God** from a devout heart and with fiery-zealous desire for His grace and enlightenment.

5. Begin and complete all your actions and omissions for the holy honor of **God** and the spreading of His almighty Name.

6. You should always maintain a holy intention <note>original: "Intention"; the author uses the Latinate term to emphasize a focused, purposeful state of mind.</note> pleasing to **God** in your spirit, so that you may better arrange and carry out all your deeds according to it.

7. You should also take care that you are not a curser, a swearer, or a **blasphemer**, so that through such loathsome sins you do not become a partaker of cruel punishments, and conversely, lose all Divine blessing.

8. You should gladly and diligently hear the Word of **God**, so that you may have a living guideline <note>original: "Richtschnur"; literally a "measuring line" or "plumb line," used here metaphorically for a moral standard.</note> by which to conduct your whole life and know how to guard yourself against sins.

9. You should thank **God** with all your heart for His grace and merciful blessing, which He...

### BOOK 3. <note>original: "LIB. 3."</note>

## Concerning the Alchemical Rules of Instruction, regarding the conduct or disposition of the Christian Laborant toward himself and toward his neighbor.

Chapter 1. <note>original: "Cap. 1."</note> Above all things, you should examine yourself to see if you are not lame or unhealthy in body and limbs, and whether you also possess a natural understanding to grasp and learn such lofty matters.

2. You should look for a **Master** <term>Lehrmeister: A mentor or teacher who provides the "roots" of alchemical knowledge.</term> who may fully instruct you in the origins of all metals and minerals, so that you have a good root or foundation to stand upon more firmly, and may direct your affairs and labor accordingly.

# Alchemical Instruction:

3. You should also search within yourself whether you
have a true passion and love to learn this art,
according to the old proverb: **Passion and love for a
thing make heavy toil and labor light.** <note>original: "Lust und Lieb zu einem Ding / macht schwere Müh und Arbeit gering."</note>

4. You should also not allow yourself to be vexed
by the effort of contemplating in your soul and spirit the
origin and beginning of all **Natural Things**, as
well as their complete endings; and whatever you
contemplate, record it all on paper, so that you may have
cause to repeat its contemplation often, and through
frequent repetition, you may know how to distinguish the
true from the untrue, and the possible from the
impossible.

5. You should be of a steadfast will, so
that you do not let yourself be driven from your purpose
by every wind which blows one way today and
another tomorrow; otherwise, through frequent abandonment
of this or that work you have begun, you will be led
away without any particular benefit, and you will become
certain of nothing through **trials**—whether it can be or
cannot be—and finally, because of such confusion, you
will abandon everything, and ultimately, to your great
detriment (which one cannot blame upon the **Art** <term>Art: referring to Alchymia, the "Great Art" of transformation and purification.</term>),
you will attain nothing at all.

6. You should also know how to steward your money,
and to save in such a way that if perhaps in the
first **trial** <term>Probe: An experimental test or attempt at the alchemical process.</term>, the work should fail because of your
ignorance and false **procedure** <term>Procedierung: The specific method or sequence of steps followed in an alchemical operation.</term>, you
would not be forced by poverty and lack of money
to <note>The sentence continues onto the next page.</note> the **repetition** <term>Reiterirung: From the Latin "reiteratio"; the act of repeating a process, such as distillation or digestion, to reach a higher state of perfection.</term> of the work for the aforementioned reason...

# Rules.

...be abandoned, and you thus attain nothing at all, to your great detriment.

7. Know, therefore, that you should not parade about in proud and haughty clothing that exceeds your means, nor wantonly consume your money through vanity, pride, and splendor. God is an enemy to these sins from the bottom of His heart, and in His own time, He casts them down from the heights into the depths. By such behavior, you would be forced to cease the **Work** <term>Work: Here referring to the "Great Work" (Magnum Opus), the long and expensive process of alchemical transformation.</term> due to lack of funds, leaving it unfinished to your own great misfortune.

8. Furthermore, you should not surrender yourself to the gluttonous and drunken demons, <note>original: "Fraß- und Sauff- Teuffel." In early modern German literature, vices were often personified as specific "devils."</note> so that the completion of this Work is not hindered by a dissolute life.

9. You should also pledge to your God in Heaven that, upon attaining this Work, you will reveal it to no one without first conducting a sufficient investigation of their spirit and will—specifically, how they stand toward God and how they intend to apply this Work toward their fellow Christians in need. This is so that you do not cast **pearls before swine** and allow this magnificent treasure to fall to robbers and murderers.

10. You should not forget your fellow neighbor; <note>original: "Neben- Christen," literally "fellow-Christian," used here to mean any neighbor or person in need.</note> you should not let your heart be made of stone and iron against your brother who suffers in poverty, lest you become a manslayer <note>The author suggests that failing to help someone in mortal need is a form of passive killing.</note> toward him and thus bring temporal punishment and eternal damnation from your God upon yourself. Rather, rescue him...
