# Alchemical Instructions,

...[rescue] him out of his distress, so that God may bless you: For if you see your brother in want, and close your heart to him, tell me, how does the love of God remain in you?

11. The talent <term>pound; a reference to the 'talents' or spiritual gifts entrusted by God in the Gospel of Matthew</term> that God has entrusted to you, employ it diligently through spiritual increase <note>original: "Wuchern" — while often translated as usury, in this context it refers to the productive use of one's wealth and skills for the benefit of others</note>, clothe the naked, break your bread for the hungry, give drink to the thirsty, visit and help the sick, release the prisoners, bury the dead, so that you may always be found in the state of good works, and finally, through grace, inherit the goal of faith: the eternal salvation of your soul.

### Praise to Trismegistus. <note>original: "Laus Trismegisto." Hermes Trismegistus is the legendary Hellenistic figure regarded as the founder of alchemy and hermetic philosophy.</note>

# The Song of Songs,

->Which is,<-

## Of Solomon, King of Israel,

->Concerning<-

# THE UNIVERSAL MEDICINE. <note>original: "MEDICINA UNIVERSALI." In alchemical literature, the Universal Medicine or Panacea is a substance capable of curing all physical ailments and perfecting base metals into gold.</note>

Just as there is eternal agreement regarding the author of the Song of Songs—the most wise Solomon—so too is there disagreement regarding its subject matter;

Those who are occupied with investigating and explaining divine things hold various opinions.

The Rabbis of the Hebrews judge it to be an *epithalamium* <term>epithalamium: a song or poem written specifically for a bride on her way to the marital chamber</term> intended for the celebration of the marriage of Solomon with the daughter of Pharaoh Vaphres <note>original: "Vaphris," usually identified with Pharaoh Hophra or Apries, though the biblical timeline refers to an earlier unnamed Pharaoh who captured Gezer.</note>, King of Egypt, and the conqueror of Hazor, Megiddo, and Gezer;

<summary>This title page introduces a hermetic interpretation of the biblical Song of Songs, framing the poem as a coded treatise on the alchemical "Universal Medicine." The author notes that while Solomon's authorship is undisputed, scholars—including Jewish Rabbis—disagree on whether the text is a literal wedding song or a divine allegory.</summary>

<keywords>Song of Songs, Solomon, Universal Medicine, Hermes Trismegistus, alchemy, epithalamium, biblical allegory, Pharaoh Vaphres</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the introduction to a hermetic and alchemical interpretation of the biblical "Song of Songs." While the previous page established Solomon as the author and linked the text to the "Universal Medicine," this page categorizes how different groups and nations have historically interpreted the poem’s allegorical meaning.</meta>

❧ (0) ❧

The Talmudists <note>original: "Talmudistæ." Jewish scholars and authorities on the Talmud.</note> interpret it as being about the union of God and the Synagogue.

The Tropologists <term>tropologists: scholars who interpret the Bible through moral allegories or figurative language</term> [interpret it] as being about the soul of Faith.

The Romans <note>Referring to the Roman Catholic tradition.</note> [interpret it] as being about Jesus Christ and the Virgin Mary;

The Germans [interpret it] as being about the State, which flourished in the most beautiful peace during the time of Solomon;

The French establish it as a dramatic script of the King of Peace, Jesus Christ, with the Church as his bride: and they determine it to be a praise of the spiritual union and most venerable betrothal, which the great Apostle of the Gentiles <note>St. Paul</note> calls "a great mystery" <note>original Greek: "τὸ μυστήριον μέγα," Eph. 5:32</note>. In this, characters are introduced speaking: the Bridegroom, the Bride, and the male and female Friends of both for this marriage, etc.

Those who consider human Wisdom to be the ultimate goal of knowledge understand it as being about the Blessed Stone <note>The Philosopher's Stone, the goal of the alchemical "Great Work."</note>, celebrated in the praises of all the Sages <term>Sophorum: a term often used for alchemists or hermetic philosophers</term>.

For they believe that within this [song], the dissolution of the coarse <note>original: "grassi," likely a transcription for "crassi" (thick or coarse matter).</note> into the subtle, the dark into the lucid, the moist into the dry, and the volatile into the fixed is excellently and regally described.

Created matter and form from God through nature, Chapter 1, verses 5 and 6.

The Subject, verse 8.

<meta>This page continues the alchemical interpretation of the "Song of Songs" by describing the nature and preparation of the "Universal Subject"—the raw material of the Great Work. (0)

...which is properly called the Matter of the Philosophers <note>original Latin: "Philosophorum." Referring to the secret substance from which the Stone is made.</note>; it contains within itself the white and the red; one is called the male, and the other the female. No such thing is found in the animal, vegetable, or mineral kingdoms. It possesses an active force and a passive one; it carries with it a dead substance and a living one, as well as the Spirit, the body, and the soul. The ignorant call it a most worthless thing. It contains the four Elements, it is everywhere and can be found anywhere, it is held in common by everyone, and it is purchased at a small price—one pound <note>original: "1. ℔."</note> is had for a single shilling <note>original: "uno solido." This is a common alchemical paradox: the "Matter" is both priceless and physically dirt-cheap or common.</note>. It is a thing that the Earth produces and that descends from heaven; it is called our Eagle, our Magnesia, our Green Lion, and our egg.

Its preparation is solely a <term>coctio: the process of "cooking" or heating a substance over a long period to mature it.</term> over a <term>Δ: the alchemical symbol for fire.</term> that is always slow. It ascends by itself, it turns black, it descends and turns white, it grows and diminishes, it turns pale and turns red, it is born, it dies, it rises again, and afterwards it lives forever. In short, let the center of the universe be sought; once found, let it be purified; once purified, let it be dissolved; once dissolved, let it be cooked; once cooked, let it be ripened; once ripened, let it be fixed; once fixed, let it be fermented; and thus you will have the supreme medicine.

The Monad of the Philosophers <note>original: "Monas Philosophorum." A term for the ultimate unity or the Stone as a single, indivisible entity.</note> possesses a certain earthly body, lurking in the Center of the Center, by which

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the activation of alchemical substances. The catchword "No-" from the previous page connects to the word "Nostra" (Our) in the second section.</meta>

...thus it must be activated by divine power; even if words are silent, there is now a generic influence, both Lunar and Solar <note>Refers to the alchemical qualities associated with silver (the Moon) and gold (the Sun)</note>, for its activation. For just as man, though created from the Earth, does not create another man directly from the Earth but from within himself—yet he is nourished by the Earth and gains his growth from it so that he may be able to reproduce—so it is with Gold and Silver. It is necessary that they first be nourished by a primordial and pure substance. Their nurse is the Earth, which, to use its true name, is the **True SOL BUB** <note>original: "SOL BUB," likely a coded or kabbalistic term for the "Solar Soul" or a specific "exalted" earth</note>. This is the One Stone and the one thing; it is gathered carefully with joy and produced without any price. It cannot be found in apothecary shops. Indeed, these pearls are gathered in the streets and are trampled underfoot <note>A common alchemical trope: the "philosopher's stone" is made from a common, lowly substance that most people overlook or despise</note>.

## Epistle of the Mission of the Rosicrucian Brothers
<note>original: "Epist. Missio Fr. R. C." refers to the *Epistola Missiva*, a document associated with the Rosicrucian brotherhood, a secret society of doctors and sages</note>

->Page 77<-

<gloss>and</gloss> <note>original: "e," likely a shorthand for the Latin "et"</note>
Our matter is Spirit, not body;
Our matter is not a mineral, but mineral Sulfur and Mercury, an oily richness and vapor; an immature mineral <term>Electrum: a naturally occurring alloy of gold and silver, often considered a "raw" or "primitive" state of precious metal</term>;
Our matter is not gold, but the seed of gold, or virgin gold;

<summary>This page describes the "true matter" of alchemy, explaining that while it is nourished by the earth like a living being, its essence is spiritual rather than physical. It emphasizes that the secret substance (the "One Stone") is common and inexpensive, yet unrecognized by the masses.</summary>
<keywords>Rosicrucians, alchemy, Sol Bub, Philosopher's Stone, Sulfur and Mercury, Electrum, seed of gold, spiritual alchemy</keywords>

Our matter is not a Metal, but the Root of all Metals <note>original: "Nostra materia non est Metallum, sed Metallorum omnium Radix." A fundamental alchemical principle: the "philosopher's stone" is not made from common gold or silver, but from the primordial substance from which all metals grow.</note>.

->*Ibid. page 77.*<-

<margin>7</margin>
**The ugly rogue Saturn** <note>original: "Sat." The author uses "rogue" to personify Saturn (lead), who in alchemy is often depicted as a grim, dirty old man or a "base" metal that must be purified.</note> **is always closest to our King, the Electrum** <term>Electrum: a naturally occurring alloy of gold and silver, viewed here as a royal or perfected state of matter</term>, **the Star of the Sun** <note>original: "Astro Solis," referring to the spiritual essence of gold</note>, **followed by the Red Lion** <note>The Red Lion is a famous alchemical symbol for the "ferment" or the stage where the substance attains the power to transmute</note>, **and the Air of Hermes** <note>original: "Æri Hermtis," referring to the volatile, spiritual "Mercury" that carries the life-force of the metals</note>.

<summary>This concluding page defines the alchemical "matter" as the spiritual root of all metals rather than a physical mineral. It highlights the paradoxical relationship between Saturn (base lead) and the most exalted substances like the Red Lion and the Star of the Sun.</summary>
